# Project Progress Ledger: Custom ERP

Chronological build-record. One section per Stage/session. Points back to `docs/micro_checklist.md` for full item-by-item detail rather than duplicating it.

---

## 1. Project Genesis

Started as a static, client-side HTML dashboard. Brand/Style data lived in a mock `db.js` and `localStorage`. No real database, no multi-tenancy, no numbering safety, no observability.

---

## 2. Architectural Decisions

| Component | Choice | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Backend | Go 1.22 | Single-binary, <15MB RAM/client, <10ms startup — cheap to host at density |
| Database | PostgreSQL 16.3 | Schema-per-tenant isolation + `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` for ledgers/sequences |
| Multi-tenancy | Schema-per-tenant | Isolates tenant data, resolved dynamically per request |
| Branding | Custom ERP | Removed legacy sample branding for a clean, reusable system |

---

## 3. Project Rollout Ledger

```
[x] Phase 1: Core Foundation & Scale Infra (Event Bus, Outbox, Availability, Reservations)
[x] Phase 2: Single Vertical Pilot (PO-GRN-Barcode-Inventory-Transfers-POS-Finance)
[x] Phase 3: Omnichannel Pilot (Shopify Sync, Webhook Imports, Fulfillment Routing)
[x] Phase 4: Store Fulfillment (Ship-from-store, BOPIS, Return Anywhere, Task Dashboard)
[x] Phase 5: Scale Test (100 → 2,000 simulated stores)
[x] Phase 6: Marketplace/OMS Expansion (Settlements, Logistics, Support Console)
[x] Phase 7: Advanced Optimization (Forecasting, Replenishment, SLA tuning)
```

**Scope note**: Phases 1-7 cover the kernel + omnichannel/scale backend only — "COMPLETED" here does *not* mean the full ERP is done. POS/Finance/GST/CRM/HR/Assets UI, the approval engine, MFA, and most of the report catalog were built later (Stage 13+). See `docs/pdf_blueprint_gap_analysis.md` for the original gap analysis and `docs/micro_checklist.md` Stage 13 for the resulting backlog.

## 197. Stage 57 — user QA round: names not codes, create-in-place, roles and users, defect batch (2026-10-04 → 06; code + docs, uncommitted)

The user operated the deployed build, submitted real transactions and reported ~30 items plus,
mid-round, a roles/users requirement. Triage, verbatim reports and answers:
[user QA round](product/erp-user-qa-2026-10-04.md); full item detail and verification in
`micro_checklist.md` Stage 57 (and the Stage 55 note for the six defects filed there).

Built, all verified live: names instead of series codes in every picker and table
(`installNameDisplay` keeps `.value` = code, so no reader changed); create-in-place for any
missing master from any picker, plus a setup detour that closes and later reopens the form with
its values (browser Back included); Status defaults to Active; one shared date picker app-wide
(which also exposed an IST date bug in the Appointment Calendar); an HSN catalogue with a picker
on Item; Transfer Order locations resolved by id, code or name; RFQ invited vendors and status
moves; Bin zones picked or created inline; Debit Note's Reference PO as a real PO picker; PIM tab
strip keeps its place; sticky row actions on wide lists; Knowledge Center no longer prints
generator comments, "Contents" heading; Stock/WMS menu grouping. Roles and users: roles created
before anyone holds them, whole-module grants, a forced and server-enforced "set your own
password" at first sign-in and after an admin reset, and menus that follow grants (18 module
screens had been shown to every role). Two defects found along the way: CSP reports starving the
per-IP rate-limit bucket (the likely source of the user's Rate Limit question), and typeahead
menus stranded under the environment banner (what blocked §196's PO chapter). Guides, a PIM
setup walkthrough and the six "what is…" answers written. One additive, idempotent migration for
all tenants (`migrations_stage57_qa_round.sql`).

The user's own video SOP (§196) was reviewed against these changes and left unchanged at their
instruction. Verified on two disposable databases (shared `custom_erp_test` untouched); the
release budget gate was tripped and brought back under by moving widgets into a lazy module.
Open, needing the user: the deploy question (two reported bugs are already fixed but undeployed),
barcode-at-variant design, non-sellable assets, "Location Movement", offer targeting,
recovery-code intent, Purchase Return build-or-retract. No commit, push or deploy.

## 196. Stage 57.12 modular ERP SOP video — first playable chapter (2026-10-06; partial)

User approved the previously deferred full-ERP walkthrough and clarified the required form:
each process is a self-contained, human-paced SOP that repeats its configuration prerequisites;
shared setup clips are referenced in multiple chapters so one changed portion is recorded once,
then existing footage is reassembled rather than all processes re-recorded. Built
[docs/sop-video](sop-video/readme.md) as an open, text-editable project: JSON scene/chapter
manifest, human narration/caption source, coverage register, local outline player with step
jumps, loopback-only Chromium recorder, WebM assembler and validation/browser tests. No new ERP
runtime dependency, account credential or recovery code was put in the repository.

Against the isolated `custom_erp_org_20261005` training DB on scratch port 8111, recorded five
real UI segments: Department, Vendor and Item creation/readback; receiving Location check; and
Purchase Requisition create/readback. The first complete chapter (Department prerequisite →
Purchase Requisition) is a seekable 1280×720, about 55-second silent WebM in
`docs/sop-video/output/all-ready.webm`. Its rendered start and end frames were inspected; the
player/range/manifest tests pass. Human voice and business-owner review remain future gates.

Attempted the PO scene through the same UI, but the Item suggestion was intercepted by the
environment banner. Recording stopped; no broken workaround was published. PO, GRN and the
remaining ERP chapters are therefore open, as is 57.12 overall. The player explicitly marks
them pending. The server and DB were isolated; no production action, source bug fix, commit,
push or deploy occurred. The master is assembled from saved clips, not a monolithic capture.

## 195. Stage 55.0F first organizational UI execution (2026-10-05; audit only, no fix or commit)

At the user's direction, performed a first business-function audit now while deferring bug repair
until ERP build completion. A fresh `custom_erp_org_20261005` tenant on loopback Postgres and a
scratch server on port 8111 kept the concurrent dirty tree and other session fixtures intact.
The scratch server was stopped afterward; the isolated database and evidence were retained.
Chromium used visible forms, buttons, dialogs and lists; one tester applied operator,
supervisor/finance and engineering/security lenses. Ten master records saved/read back, followed
by a requisition, approved PO, partial/rejected GRN, draft supplier invoice and match, transfer
draft, and OMS manual order with same-reference retry. A scoped Store Manager approved the PO;
the admin's self-approval was correctly refused. The duplicate OMS intake returned the same ID.

The [dated assurance record](assurance/erp-functional-audit-2026-10-05.md) preserves six repair
findings: tax/partial-bill mismatch math blocks valid AP; Transfer **Mark Approved** returns 405
and its direct status-edit design lacks a maker-checker rule; Reserved OMS order has no visible
release-to-fulfillment path; Zone/Item/Location pickers demand or expose technical IDs; stock
labels obscure accepted/rejected/reserved amounts; and several outcomes give misleading advice.
It also distinguishes browser-harness timeouts from real product defects. No production/vendor
result, month-end stock/GL reconciliation, 199-type classification, all-module acceptance,
independent user study or full Go suite is claimed. 55.0F and 55.22 remain open. Only audit
documentation was written; no source repair, stage, commit, push or deploy.

**2026-10-05 follow-up:** At the user's request, the future UI functional review now separates
the Account Head (operational books, control and close) from the qualified CA (applicable
statutory/tax and audit challenge). The operating plan, 55.0F/55.22 gates and assurance
limitations were updated. Neither reviewer participated in the browser run; no sign-off or
new functional result is claimed. No application code changed or commit made.

## 193. Stage 55.1 shared UI correctness closed (2026-10-04; code, Chromium, Go and docs; no commit)

The user continued building after the every-module audit. We took the first full phase, 55.1, without
expanding into the planned domain phases. The shared document New handler no longer receives a click
event as an existing record; edit mode now requires an actual record identity. This removes the
incorrect Edit title and Item Type's `click` default. New dialogs receive initial focus, contain
Tab navigation and return focus on Escape. Five HR and two Manufacturing secondary tabs now await
the shared lazy document-table module, preserving the existing entitlement and Retry path. The
phone decorator respects field semantics so a Wave ID (and comparable scan identifiers) is not
normalized as a phone number; genuine phone fields still normalize. Password-reset history uses
the supported Users/reset entry point instead of an unsupported generic User lookup, without
opening identity administration to a restricted role. USER_GUIDE and ADMIN_GUIDE examples now match.

[Sanitized verification](assurance/stage55-shared-correctness-2026-10-04.json) records **20 distinct
passing Chromium checks** across fresh contexts: the seven originally failing cold tabs, all **54**
secondary panels across seven parent screens, chunk failure and Retry, desktop/390px Wave and phone
typing, nine shared New form shapes with keyboard handling, password-reset cancellation, a Store
Manager permission denial, and actual Department create/edit against disposable `custom_erp_t1`
(`Department/HQ/2026/000001`). The first Department save wait timed out because the browser harness
blocked `POST /api/v1/sequence`; narrowly allowing that test fixture call made create/edit pass.
This was a harness correction, not a production fix. The initial long inventory's navigation timing
outliers were likewise not counted as application errors. The browser result does not assert 198
end-to-end saves or real-user/device acceptance.

Four new structural Go tripwires, password-reset and timezone/role-template targeted tests, `go vet
./internal/server`, the scratch `go build ./cmd/server`, and JavaScript syntax checks passed. The
full `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` reached completion but failed **only**
`TestAuditDeletedRowIsDetectedByCheckpoint`, which encounters the previously preserved stale
checkpoint `311b63f0-4f1b-40ac-bd6f-b4421838d3f9`; `internal/server` passed. The security
surface check passed at 495 routes and 32 jobs. This failure is reported, not silently made green.

The 55.1 Done bar is checked in the plan and both checklists. **Still open:** 55.2–55.22; BLD-041's
conditional print verification; BLD-045's performance budgets; PIM phone tab overlap; external
acceptance. New audit and QA artifacts were registered; graph and brain publications were refreshed
locally. This work did not stage, commit, push, deploy or mutate a shared/production database.
During the final documentation pass a separate session advanced HEAD to `fe97416`; shared source

## 190. BLD-046/048/052/055 and the XSS browser boundary (47.8.1/.2/.4) — Stage 50.14-50.16 (2026-10-04; Go + JS + migration + tests + docs, no commit)

Five requested items, worked in order against a disposable `custom_erp_t2` database (loopback 5490, 169 migrations) and a scratch server on port 8102. Full per-item detail in `micro_checklist.md` and the build checklist; this is the index. **Two items closed, three advanced with their remaining scope stated.**

**BLD-046 — abandoned-request cancellation (closed).** The job-runner leg already existed from the prior pass; the HTTP leg did not. Added `RunReportContext`, `BulkImportCSVContext`, `RunPIMImportTemplateContext`, `StreamSearchFeedExportCSVContext` and `EnqueueJobContext`, each a context-aware form with the old signature kept as a `context.Background()` shim so no existing caller changed. Deliberately **did not** thread `ctx` through `ReportRunFunc`: that would have changed all 97 registered reports while their own `db.DB.Query` calls still ignored it — churn across the catalog for zero cancelled statements. Instead `RunReport`, the one choke point every report already runs through, races the run against `ctx` (`runReportRaced`, bounded by a 5-minute `reportRunHardTimeout` so an abandoned run cannot leak), so the caller is released immediately and the row-cap check, masking and JSON serialization of a payload nobody is waiting for are all skipped. Import cancellation is *complete* rather than merely prompt (the batch loop is our own code, checked between batches only, so committed batches stay committed and re-uploading resumes through the existing per-row existence check); the streaming PIM export reaches PostgreSQL via `QueryContext`, so an abandoned whole-catalog scan is cancelled server-side. Cancellation handling attached once at `writeEngineError` — the shared writer every engine-backed handler funnels through — returning 499 and skipping the error catalog, the system-error log and the 5xx alerting path. **Found and fixed a real leak while proving it live**: six aborted 40k-row exports produced six `PIM_SEARCH_FEED_EXPORT_FAILED` system-error rows, because a streaming handler cannot use `writeEngineError` once the first byte is flushed; `isAbandonedRequest` (cancellation, a done request context, or an explicit broken-pipe/reset error — never OS-specific string matching) now covers that case, and the same six aborts produce zero rows. Evidence: normal/oversized/interrupted across three fresh rounds, both as Go tests (`bld046_request_cancellation_test.go`) and live over HTTP (40,002-row export at 200; 422 `DATAIM-0189` for a 400-row file against a 100-row cap, refused before parsing the whole upload; aborted exports logging nothing while lib/pq dispatches real PostgreSQL cancel requests).

**BLD-048 — cost and artifact budgets (closed).** Turned the canonical NFR budgets into ten threshold-failure tests in `cmd/releasebudget`, so an artifact crossing a limit breaks `go test ./...` and not only the CI step: the real measurement runs against the real tree, each threshold is proved to bite at exactly one byte over, and `TestGrowthAndRetentionCostsAreCaptured` asserts the DB/log/audit/backup surfaces are reported *without* a limit, since NFR-DATA-001 names owners but approves no numeric cap and inventing one would be fabricated policy. Added the NFR-DOC-001 per-topic cap (largest article, not the mean — the clause is per topic, so an average would let one oversized article hide behind 48 small ones). **Three reproducible release artifacts**: three independent `-trimpath -ldflags="-s -w"` linux/amd64 builds, all three byte-identical (`972d8b41…`, 16,990,368 bytes, 35% under the 25 MiB limit) with byte-identical reports. Measurement units documented in `docs/assurance/release-budget-evidence-2026-10-04.md` and in the report's own `units` field. **Found and corrected a metric that read as breached when it was not**: the startup-JS gate counted `view-pos.js` as "initial core JS", but that module loads only after authentication and a route-entitlement check, so it is not initial by construction — and BLD-041's own 100.3 KiB baseline measured the shell without it. Split into the gated `initial_js_gzip_bytes` (shell only, 104,477 of 122,880 — within budget) and `initial_js_plus_first_screen_gzip_bytes` (130,364, reported as an observation with **no** invented limit, because NFR-COST-001 sets no budget for that set and shrinking it is BLD-041's remaining scope). Nothing hidden: the larger number is still published, and a test guards that the observation can never acquire a fabricated threshold. No Redis, broker or new service.

**BLD-052 — observability and on-call delivery (advanced).** Correlation previously stopped at the request boundary. New `engines/correlation.go` carries one id from the request through the transaction into the durable async work it queues, on a context rather than threaded as a parameter through every enqueue site; `migrations_stage50_15_correlation_trace.sql` adds an additive, partially-indexed `correlation_id` to `async_jobs` and `integration_event_outbox`. The full request → transaction → outbox → webhook-job chain is proven end to end in `bld052_correlation_trace_test.go`. `SafeCorrelationID` bounds and sanitizes at the single writer — not because anything is attacker-controlled today (middleware mints the UUID and ignores inbound headers) but because the value now reaches a log line and two indexed columns, and accepting a caller-supplied id later would silently turn all three into untrusted sinks. Added `GET /api/v1/ready` as a distinct verdict from `/api/v1/health`: readiness is false while the schema is behind the binary's migrations (the Stage 30.2.2 drift class — a deployed binary serving 500s while looking perfectly healthy) and false once draining begins, so a load balancer stops sending traffic before `srv.Shutdown` starts refusing it. Verified live: 200 when current; 503 naming the exact pending migration when drift was injected, while `/health` stayed 200. Alerts now name an accountable responder (`OPS_ALERT_RESPONDER`), and an unowned setup announces itself as `UNASSIGNED` in the channel rather than being discovered during an incident. **Three injected incidents drilled locally** against a loopback collector (queue saturation, stale backup, sustained error rate), each asserted actionable, byte-bounded, redacted and responder-named. **The backup drill found a real information leak**: the stale-backup alert interpolated the absolute host backup path into a payload bound for an external chat webhook — removed, since the responder configured `BACKUP_DIR` and learns nothing from being told it, while a third party reading the channel learns the host's filesystem layout. **Stays open**: `OPS_ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` is unset (item 20.2), so only the local chain is proven — delivery to a real responder at a real endpoint is the external action the Done bar reserves. It is a genuine one-variable change, asserted as such by `TestDrillDeliveryNeedsOnlyTheWebhookVariable`.

**BLD-055 — documentation health (advanced).** Verified rather than assumed the prior pass's claims: `go run ./cmd/doclint -strict` is **0 findings across 322 files**, the eight recorded lint findings and seven unregistered security documents are genuinely resolved, and the handover is 82 lines with its history preserved verbatim at `docs/archive/ai-handover-2026-10-03.txt`. **Three safety rounds** run: `docs/` hashed before and after three strict-plus-JSON passes, byte-identical each time (the checks are read-only), and three failure modes (missing root, unreadable register, unwritable output path) each produce a clean diagnostic, a non-zero exit and an untouched tree. The corrupt-register case is now a durable unit test rather than a manual round, so it cannot regress: a corrupt register must be *reported*, never treated as empty, because that would mark every document unregistered and invite a destructive "refresh". **Stays open**: the clean-checkout generated-freshness check and the remaining Stage 48 owner/content/human-acceptance gates.

**47.8.1/.2/.4 — XSS-resistant browser boundary (.2 closed; .1/.4 advanced).** All **126** inline event-handler attributes are gone — 109 across `app.js` and the 18 view modules, 17 in `index.html` — replaced by one delegated dispatcher driven by `data-act`/`data-act-args` that `actionAttrs()` emits. Converted mechanically after classifying every handler first (107 of 109 were a single call; the two that were not became named functions, because the dispatcher calls one function per action and has no statement sequencing — sequencing would mean evaluating source). **This was a security fix, not only a CSP enabler**: an inline handler interpolated its arguments as JavaScript source inside an HTML attribute, so a record id, SKU or doctype containing a quote closed the attribute and the string and executed what followed — a live injection sink wherever that value came from imported data, a connector payload, a report row or a CSV cell. Arguments now travel JSON-encoded and HTML-escaped. **The new harness found a latent primitive in the dispatcher itself**: `window['eval']` is a function, so a hostile `data-act` name with a string argument would have been arbitrary code execution; `resolveActionFunction` now requires a plain identifier that is an *own* property of `window` and not a native built-in — an allowlist by construction rather than a denylist chasing the platform. Not reachable today (every name is a literal in this repository), so defence in depth rather than a patched hole. **CSP flipped report-only → blocking**, with both phases measured in Chromium across all 29 views: the strict candidate reported **zero** violations of any directive before it was enforced, and after enforcing, zero enforced violations, zero `script-src` violations and zero page errors. No nonce and no hash list, deliberately — there are no inline `<script>` blocks for a nonce to permit, a nonce would defeat static caching of `index.html`, and `script-src` nonces do not whitelist handler *attributes* anyway (that needs `'unsafe-hashes'`, a weaker position than removing them). The report-only header now carries the next candidate, strict `style-src`, with its 629 inline-style violations visible but not enforced. **Two pre-existing bugs found by the verification sweep**: `employeePickerField`/`attachEmployeePicker` were declared in `view-hr.js` but used by `view-expenses.js`, so Expenses threw `ReferenceError` unless HR had been opened first — a fifth instance of the BLD-041 cross-module class, fixed by promoting both to the shared core beside `autoNumberField`; and four action names (`closeEditPrefixModal`, `submitEditPrefix`, `closeAddLabelModal`, `submitAddLabel`) are referenced only by dead static modals in `index.html` and defined nowhere, which threw silently before and now log a clear error — reported, not fixed, since removing the overlays is a UI decision. Also consolidated `cfgEsc`, a byte-for-byte duplicate of `escapeHTMLText` with 55 call sites, into a delegating alias so there is one escaping implementation instead of two. **47.8.1 stays open**: 461 sinks hold 637 interpolations, of which 192 are escaped at the sink, 115 have no interpolation, 102 are trusted/config/assembled markup, 43 are markup loops, and **300 are candidates flagged for review** — a 16-item hand sample found 6 genuine and 10 false positives, so the real population is on the order of 110, and sound separation of trusted config from untrusted data needs data-flow analysis the classifier does not do. Reported as incomplete rather than resolved; the full artifact is committed at `docs/security/browser-boundary-sink-classification.json`. **47.8.4 stays open**: payload coverage exists for every named surface (two harnesses, `browser-xss-boundary.cjs` and the new `browser-xss-action-boundary.cjs`, the latter driving seven breakout payloads through action arguments, six call-primitive attempts through action names, malformed args failing closed, and connector/API-error/attachment/CSV values in both text and attribute position, plus a guard that fails if any inline handler returns to a shipped file) but not for *every* shared renderer, and the stolen/revoked-session proof is untouched.

**Verification.** `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...` and `gofmt` clean; `node --check` on `app.js` and all 18 modules; `graphify update .` and `pwsh docs/brain/update-brain.ps1` run (one `brain.map.json` edit claimed `engines/correlation.go` and `internal/server/handlers_readiness.go` into the observability region — 100% coverage); `attack_surface.json` regenerated for the new `/api/v1/ready` route (495 routes, 32 background jobs). Three full `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` rounds. **Five failures in those rounds are not from this work** and are left alone: `TestKnownModulesMatchTheTenantSchema`, `TestAdministratorAndAuditorCoverEveryShippedDoctype` and `TestModuleManifestCatalog` all trace to a concurrent session's uncommitted Stage 51.8 removal of `"Store"` from `knownModules` in `engines/role_templates.go`, against a freshly built database where `migrations_stores_master_fields.sql` sorts last and re-seeds that doctype after `migrations_stage30_5_5_retire_stores.sql` retired it — a real migration-ordering interaction worth that session knowing about; `TestAuditDeletedRowIsDetectedByCheckpoint` and `TestDriftGuardsUnmappedScreensSummary` sit in `engines/audit_evidence.go` and `internal/kb/drift.go`, also modified in the tree by that session and untouched here. Two failures that *were* from this work were fixed: `TestSecurityHeadersPublishesStrictCSPReportOnlyCandidate` asserted the enforcing policy still carried `'unsafe-inline'` "before inline actions are migrated" — inverted rather than deleted, since the risk moved from "not yet migrated" to "quietly reintroduced"; and the stale-backup drill was being suppressed by a package-level alert cooldown another test had already tripped. No commit, no push, no deploy.

---

## 189. POS rebuilt as a standard retail till — Stage 53 (2026-10-04; migration + Go + JS + tests + docs, no commit)

User, looking at the live POS screen, rejected it outright — *"this is shitty pos. who ask cust code. Make it std pos, research and make it best"* — with four specifics: name search, the bill should say which location and store, sellable vs non-sellable location, and it should freeze until reset with a reset button. Two of those read more than one way, so both were put to the user before any code was written; both came back **"Both"** (freeze = lock the store for the shift *and* freeze the till on the completed bill; sellable = a flag on the `Location` master *and* splitting the cart's availability figure). Plan written into `micro_checklist.md` Stage 53 first at the user's instruction, then built. Full per-item mechanics there — this is the index.

- **The diagnosis came from reading the code, not the screenshot.** The customer typeahead already existed and the server's `?q=` already matched on name and phone — the field was simply *labelled* "Customer Code (optional)" and showed the raw code, so the capability was there and the UX denied it. `GetAvailableToSell` already returned `qc_hold`/`damaged`/`blocked`/`hold_qty` — the cart discarded them and printed one number. The sellable/non-sellable split therefore needed **no backend change at all**, and the picker work reused `attachCodeNamePicker` rather than adding a pattern.
- **53.1/53.2 `Location.sellable`** — one additive migration (Select `Yes,No`, optional, backfilled) plus `engines.LocationIsSellable`/`ValidatePOSSellableLocation` returning coded `POSOFF-0245`, called from `OpenPOSSession` **and** `handleCheckout`. Checkout is guarded separately on purpose: an offline-queued sale replays through it without re-opening a session. Unregistered location codes stay permitted — Stage 17.9 deliberately left location free text on most doctypes.
- **The backfill rule was rewritten after running it.** The planned "`Store` → Yes, everything else → No" produced **0 sellable locations out of 112** on the dev DB, because `migrations_stage17h_location_masters.sql` seeds every in-use location code with a *hardcoded* `type = 'Warehouse'`. Shipping that would have been an outage, not an additive change — every till refusing every sale. The rule now marks a location sellable if a `POSCart`/`POSSession` exists against it, *then* falls back to type: what the business actually did is better evidence than a type nobody set. Re-run: 2 sellable (`HO`, `WH01`), 110 not. This is the Stage's most important finding and is written up for the admin in ADMIN_GUIDE §B.3.0.4.
- **53.3–53.8 the till itself** — a generic `opts.filters` added once to the shared `attachTypeahead` (off by default, all ~45 existing pickers unchanged) so the POS store picker offers only sellable locations; customer searched by **name/phone** with a "Walk-in" default and inline `+ New Customer`; scan-or-search product entry resolving exact code → exact barcode → name search off a single request, so a scanner still rings up instantly and a typed name now lists matches instead of silently adding a phantom zero-availability line; availability split into "N sellable" / "N not sellable (QC hold / damaged / blocked)"; `−`/`+` qty steppers.
- **53.9–53.11 freeze and reset** — the store locks once a session is open or the cart has lines, with **Reset Terminal** (confirms, names the lines it will discard, and refuses to release the store while a session is open — a session is a cash-accountability record). All three terminal checkout outcomes (paid / pending-approval / queued-offline) funnel through one `freezePOSTill`, so none can leave a half-live cart; **New Sale (Reset)** is the only way out and deliberately keeps the store binding. Cash tendered → change due, display-only.
- **53.12 the bill says which store** — new `engines.ReceiptStoreHeader` resolving Location → LegalEntity (name, GSTIN, state), wired into **both** server-side branches of `BuildReceiptPayload` and the browser fallback, plus the cashier now persisted on the cart. Forgiving by design: a missing entity drops a line rather than failing the print.
- **Two defects found while building, neither reported nor asked for.** (a) The **browser-fallback receipt printed `0.00` on every line** — Stage 47.2 zeroed `sale_price` in the checkout payload for server-priced lines and `printPOSReceipt` was handed that array; it survived because the ESC-POS path rebuilds server-side, so only shops without a receipt printer ever saw it. Fixed via `posReceiptItems()`. (b) The 17.9 all-Warehouse seed trap above, which would have been shipped as a silent outage.
- **Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./engines/`, `gofmt`, `node --check` on both JS files all clean; new `engines/pos_sellable_location_stage53_test.go` (20 subtests) green, existing `qz_receipt_invoice_test.go` still green, `internal/server` suite green; migration applied and the backfill confirmed by direct `psql` query. `go run ./cmd/doclint -root .` — 4 findings, none in files this Stage touched.
- **53.17, a same-session user follow-up: a real Reset button, separate from Refresh.** On seeing the till's freeze/reset work the user asked for the same idea at the application level — *"Refresh will have cahe and have all info but reset button will reset all"*. The header had one button (**Sync**, *"Refresh local data store"*) that re-fetched labels and doctypes and kept every cache. Now two: **Refresh** (unchanged behaviour, reworded to say what it keeps) and **Reset**, which drops the `erp_swr_*` sessionStorage cache behind every list and metadata read, the nav state, the in-memory `state` caches and the POS till, then rebuilds from the server. Building it surfaced the thing that would have made Reset a lie: **`loadViewModule` imported `view-pos.js` with no version parameter at all**, so Reset would have cleared every app-level cache and then re-imported the identical stale module straight out of the browser's HTTP cache — and, separately, a deploy could update the shell while every lazy screen kept running the previous release. Fixed with a `VIEW_MODULE_VERSION` constant (keep in step with `index.html`'s `app.js?v=N`) plus a `reset=` bust. Three non-actions are deliberate data-safety calls: Reset **refuses to run while offline POS sales are queued** (those are completed sales, not cache), never signs the user out, and never touches the theme. Documented as USER_GUIDE §12.3 — including the point that **a browser reload is not a substitute**, since F5 re-runs against the same stored copies — and ADMIN_GUIDE §B.3.0.6.
- **Left open deliberately.** 53.15 deploy holds behind Stage 51's batched-deploy decision and needs a production backfill review. **53.16 needs a product decision**: `cmd/releasebudget`'s `initial_js_gzip_bytes` gate is now over (129,143 of 122,880; this Stage's blocks account for 8,862 of it), but `jsFiles` still counts `public/view-pos.js`, which BLD-041 made **lazy-loaded** — so a gate that says it measures "startup JavaScript" is measuring a module that is not startup JavaScript. The budget list was deliberately **not** edited, since the gate's own message says to shrink the artifact rather than raise the limit; the stale-list question is the user's to settle.
- **Concurrent-session note.** This tree saw active edits from other sessions throughout (`engines/webhook.go`/`jobrunner.go` were mid-refactor and broke the build twice mid-run — verified around with a `go build -overlay`, never by editing their files; `public/view-pos.js` is not in HEAD at all, being another session's in-flight app.js split). `attack_surface.json` deliberately **not** regenerated: its drift is another session's `GET /api/v1/ready` route plus the migration count, and regenerating would fold their in-flight work into a tracked security artifact.

## 188. Version identity surfaced in the UI — Stage 54 (2026-10-04; code + tests + docs, no commit)

User asked for "a version system in erp. Std one. It will show near profile. Each deploy should update automatically and user friendly one and release/build date as well." Investigation first: the **version system already existed and had since Stage 14.6** — `internal/server/VERSION` is `go:embed`ed into the binary, `gitCommit`/`buildTime` are `-ldflags`-stamped by `manage.ps1`, `deploy/deploy.ps1`, `promote.ps1` and both CI build jobs, and `GET /api/v1/version` has been a public route the whole time. What was missing was any surface for it. So the work was the UI plus one derived field, not a new subsystem — the "each deploy updates automatically" requirement was already satisfied by the existing ldflags stamping and needed no new mechanism.

Two product calls came from the user during the build. **Version stays `0.1.0`**: bumping it would desync ~47 governance docs carrying `applies_to: source release 0.1.0` in frontmatter, and the UI requirement does not depend on the number. **Version and date only**: a first pass had followed the SAP Fiori / Office convention — an "About" item in the profile menu opening a dialog with product, version, release date, commit, build timestamp, environment and a copy-for-support button — and the user cut it mid-build as "not user friendly". The dialog, the copy action, the About menu item and the commit/build-time rows were all removed again before anything landed; the shipped surface is a single line of text. Worth carrying forward: the SAP/Office "About dialog" instinct was wrong for this user, who wanted the version legible and nothing else.

Built: `releaseDate()` in `internal/server/middleware.go` beside the existing version block, deriving the calendar day from the stamped `buildTime` rather than storing a second value that could drift, and returning `""` for the `"unknown"` default and for unparseable input — the signal the frontend keys off to render "development build". `GET /api/v1/version` gained `release_date`, kept as a **flat `map[string]string`** because the existing 14.6 test and any ops caller decode it into a string map. Frontend: a `<p id="app-version-line">` in the existing `.sidebar-footer` under the account chip plus a second line at the foot of the existing account popover, two CSS rules, and `loadAppVersion()`/`formatReleaseDate()` in `app.js` rendering the ISO date in the viewer's own locale. Fetched with a plain `fetch` rather than `apiFetch` (public route — the line must render even when a tenant/permission call fails) and `cache: 'no-store'`, since the whole point is the binary running right now. The element ships `hidden` and is revealed only on success, so a failed fetch leaves no empty row.

Two supporting fixes found by following the change through: `app.js?v=27→28` and `styles.css?v=22→23`, because `staticAssetCache` serves `?v=`-stamped assets as `immutable` for a year — without the bump a returning user would have kept the old JS/CSS and never seen the line at all. And the `Dockerfile` build was stamping nothing, so a container build would have permanently reported "development build"; it now takes `GIT_COMMIT`/`BUILD_TIME` ARGs feeding the same ldflags as every other path, defaulting to the unstamped values so a bare `docker build` is unchanged. The `releasebudget` build at `ci.yml:84` was left unstamped on purpose — it measures artifact size and is never shipped.

**Verified**: new DB-free `internal/server/version_release_test.go` (`TestReleaseDateDerivesFromBuildStamp` over stamped/`unknown`/empty/unparseable, and `TestVersionPayloadShape` pinning the flat-string-map contract) passes; `go vet ./internal/server/` and `node --check public/app.js` clean. End-to-end: a binary built with the real deploy ldflags served `{"build_time":"2026-10-04T03:23:45Z","git_commit":"afaf419","release_date":"2026-10-04","version":"0.1.0"}` on a scratch port, and a Playwright pass as `manager1` confirmed the rendered line reads `v0.1.0 · Oct 4, 2026` at 11px `rgb(100,116,139)`, positioned directly below the account chip. The single console 403 during that pass (`/api/v1/admin/industry/lock`) is pre-existing and role-related. Docs: USER_GUIDE §11.1 plus a "How do I…?" row, ADMIN_GUIDE §C.2.1 covering where each half comes from, the one-file bump procedure, and what "development build" on a server means.

**Format revised twice, both times by the user looking at it live.** The first cut followed the SAP Fiori / Office "About dialog" convention — a profile-menu item opening product, version, release date, commit, build timestamp, environment and a copy-for-support button. The user cut it mid-build as "not user friendly", and it was removed entirely before anything landed. The second cut, `v0.1.0 · Oct 4, 2026`, was rejected on sight as not standard enough and too big. **The shipped form is `Version 0.1.0` alone** in the sidebar — the spelled-out enterprise form (SAP/Dynamics/Tally), 10px, muted — with the release date on that line's `title` tooltip and on the last line of the account popover (`Version 0.1.0 · Released 4 Oct 2026`). The popover copy is not redundancy: a `title` never appears on a touch device and nobody discovers one by accident, so "click your own name" is the discoverable route to the date, sitting next to Sign Out where support-ticket information belongs. Worth carrying forward: the About-dialog instinct was wrong twice over for this user, who wanted the version legible and *nothing else* — each removal made the result better.

**A third revision stripped it further still.** `Version 0.1.0` plus a native `title` tooltip reading `Version 0.1.0 / Released Oct 4, 2026` was rejected the same way the first two were — *"No. Only this info and very small pop up. Clean compact small UI. 0.1.0 / 4/10/26"*. Every label came off. The sidebar now shows **`0.1.0`** alone, and hover, keyboard focus or tap opens a **custom popup** of two lines, `0.1.0` over `4/10/26`, with no heading, no labels and no actions. The native `title` had to go to get there: it cannot be styled, cannot be made small, and never appears on a touch device — which also retired the `account-popover-version` line added one revision earlier, since that line existed only to work around the tooltip being unreachable by tap. The line became a real `<button>` styled back down to plain text, because something that opens a popup must be keyboard- and touch-reachable.

Two smaller corrections fell out of that. `formatReleaseDate()` moved off `toLocaleDateString` to parsing the ISO parts by hand: locale formatting rendered month-first (`10/4/26`) on this machine's US-locale browser, **inverting day and month for an Indian reader**, and pinning it day-first would have meant hardcoding a locale — at which point the stdlib call buys nothing over four lines of deterministic string work. And the popup's first cut used `--font-numeric` on the theory that version strings want tabular digits; live it read wide and technical beside the sidebar's UI font, so both the line and the popup moved to `--font-family`. That one was a judgement call from the screenshot rather than a request.

Worth carrying forward: this surface was cut down three separate times, and each cut improved it — About dialog → `v0.1.0 · Oct 4, 2026` → `Version 0.1.0` + tooltip → `0.1.0` + a tiny popup. The instinct to add context (labels, a dialog, a copy button, an environment row) was wrong every single time for this user. Also worth noting: "show it smaller/quieter" kept being the right call *visually*, but the one place it had to be refused was contrast — see 54.3a.

One judgement call runs against the literal instruction: the user chose "smaller **and lighter**", but `--text-muted` (`#64748b`) on white already measures 4.76:1, barely above the 4.5:1 WCAG AA floor that applies to body text at any size, so lightening it one step would have failed. The extra quietness was bought with size (11px → 10px) and the colour left alone, with the reason recorded in both the CSS comment and checklist 54.3a so nobody "finishes the job" later.

**A real pre-existing defect fell out of contrast-checking that decision**, and was fixed with the user's approval: `.account-trigger` is a `<button>` that set no `color`, so the username inside it inherited the UA default button colour — pure black. On white that is 17.85:1 and invisible as a bug; on the dark-mode sidebar it is **1.35:1**, i.e. the signed-in user's own name was effectively unreadable in dark mode. `.user-role` escaped it only because it sets its own colour, which is precisely why the role line looked correct and the name did not, and why nobody had caught it. One declaration on `.account-trigger` fixes it at the place every element in the chip inherits from. Measured 1.35:1 → 12.99:1.

**Process note worth keeping.** The Stage 54 section of `docs/micro_checklist.md` was appended, then silently clobbered by a parallel session writing its own Stage 53 updates over the whole file. It surfaced only because a later edit to item 54.3 found nothing to match — not because anything errored. The other four docs touched this session (ledger, handover, both guides) survived intact. Re-appended with a marker. Concrete lesson for this tree: after appending to a shared tracker, re-grep for the section before trusting it is there, and treat "my edit found no match" as evidence of a clobber rather than of a bad pattern. Separately, a `go fmt ./internal/server/` run reformatted 11 files belonging to other in-progress sessions — whitespace-only, verified with `git diff -w`, but it should have been scoped to the files this session actually touched.

Two environment notes for the next session, neither caused by this work: `engines/webhook.go:147` was briefly uncompilable mid-session (`undefined: correlationID`) from a concurrent session's in-progress edit and resolved itself — left alone rather than "fixed", per the shared-tree rule. And the portable Postgres was found running on **port 5490**, not the 5435 `environments.json` documents, with live connections from another session; this pass used it read-only rather than restarting anything.

## 187. Naive PostgreSQL timestamp skew audit — narrow fixes, non-UTC regression; Stage 51.8 tests truth-checked (2026-10-04; Go + tests + docs, no commit)

**Decision before remediation.** Did not pin every `db.InitDB` pool connection to UTC. The known local-calendar callers were enumerated first: `engines/dashboard.go`'s `DashboardDigest.next_run_date`, `engines/scheduled_reports.go`'s `next_run_date` advancement, HR `Attendance`/`Leave` date-only document fields and payroll date filters, India financial-year document numbering, and SQL `CURRENT_DATE`/date-range cutoffs in accounting-period, currency and finance paths. Those are intentionally calendar-based; changing pooled `TimeZone` would alter what those routines mean and could reinterpret historical naive timestamp rows. The existing database's `SHOW TimeZone` is `Asia/Calcutta`; a clean scratch reproduction showed a default `TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` scans through lib/pq as an apparent UTC instant about **5h30m in the future** (`time.Since == -5h29m59.99s`). Projecting `created_at AT TIME ZONE current_setting('TimeZone')` returned the actual `Asia/Calcutta` instant and an age of only a few milliseconds. The chosen remediation is therefore narrow: interpret a naive timestamp in the same session zone when crossing into Go, or convert a Go instant to a local naive value on the parameter side (keeping indexed timestamp columns bare).

**Naive timestamp / Go-time comparisons found and corrected:**

- `engines/audit_evidence.go` — `audit_checkpoints.created_at` → `AuditVerificationOverdue` / `time.Since`.
- `engines/audit_archive.go` — `MAX(audit_logs.created_at)` → Go's archive cutoff; the cutoff is now converted to a session-local timestamp on the parameter side.
- `engines/channel_sync.go` — `MAX(documents.created_at)` for `ChannelSyncRun` → sync-interval check.
- `engines/document_edit_window.go` — `documents.created_at` → PO/GRN edit-window age.
- `engines/fulfillment.go` → `engines/returns.go`/`returns_atomic.go` — original POSCart/SalesInvoice `created_at` → return-window elapsed-time checks.
- `engines/crm_analytics.go`, `crm_reports_stage26.go` — POSCart/SalesInvoice creation timestamps → churn and RFM recency; converted both extrema and the 360-profile timestamp returned to Go.
- `engines/reports.go`, `report_definitions.go`, `oms_reports.go` — document creation timestamps → Payables/Receivables, SalesOrder and ReturnRequest age buckets/drilldowns; a due-date string is parsed as a local business date.
- `engines/reports_stage26_10.go`, `settlement_reconciliation.go` — `documents.updated_at` → stale-approval and unsettled-shipment age.
- `engines/wms_pack_count.go` — `MAX(documents.created_at)` → both cycle-count due-age plans.
- `engines/bank_reconciliation.go` — `gl_postings.created_at` versus a statement's date-only `txn_date`; the posting instant is zone-aware and the bank date is parsed in local calendar time.
- Go-instants compared to naive columns in SQL: `alerting.go` and `patchintake.go` error-log cutoffs, `market_intelligence.go`'s `since`, `campaign.go`'s campaign-creation attribution threshold, and `public_api_v1.go`'s RFC3339 `updated_since`. Each now converts the right-hand instant to the session-local naive timestamp so a B-tree can still use the bare column.

Reviewed safe/non-mixed cases rather than changing them: API-idempotency and lifecycle instants are `TIMESTAMPTZ`; channel finished-at values are RFC3339 JSON; SQL-only `CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`/`NOW()` age predicates stay in PostgreSQL; same-column database-to-database comparisons in `sourcing.go`, `wms_productivity.go` and OMS turnaround subtract the shared offset (no Go wall-clock comparison); file mtimes and in-memory throttles are not database timestamps. Date-only cutoff predicates remain date predicates and preserve local business-calendar behavior. `db.InitDB` was not changed.

**Regression and 51.8.** `TestAuditVerificationOverdueDetectsSilence` now forces every new connection to `Asia/Calcutta`, asserts the zone, writes a unique audit event/checkpoint and fails for a negative or implausible age; its corrected query passes. The `custom_erp_t1` test database had zero `Stores` documents but retained an empty `Stores` metadata row although the guarded Stage 30.5.5 migration was already recorded. Re-applied that migration's idempotent SQL to this disposable DB only (zero documents meant the guarded metadata delete was safe); then `TestKnownModulesMatchTheTenantSchema` and `TestAdministratorAndAuditorCoverEveryShippedDoctype` passed. With a scratch Go overlay substituting only the unrelated current compile error in `engines/webhook.go`, the targeted set of timezone, audit archive lifecycle, edit-window, and both 51.8 role tests passed: `go test -overlay ... ./engines -run '^(TestAuditVerificationOverdueDetectsSilence|TestAuditArchiveWriteVerifyDeleteRestoreDrill|TestDocumentEditWindow|TestKnownModulesMatchTheTenantSchema|TestAdministratorAndAuditorCoverEveryShippedDoctype)$' -count=1`. The overlay and all helpers live only in the session scratchpad.

**Initial suite limitation (superseded by §191).** The first attempt hit the concurrent BLD-052
`correlationID` compile error. After that parallel edit compiled, the full suite was rerun on the
current schema; its remaining audit-checkpoint failure and final result are recorded in §191. The
timezone and 51.8 focused tests pass. No timezone-specific schema migration or route was added.

## 186. BLD-036 closeout — gap (b) explicitly accepted; retries resolve the live parent, never render scratch (2026-10-04; frontend + docs, no commit)

The user authorized either accepting the 2026-10-02 section-refresh stale-while-revalidate gap (b) as a residual or reversing that decision and implementing it. Chose the explicitly recorded acceptance: the broad `loadYardBoard`/`loadWarehouseCockpit`/`loadOMSOrders` and similar WMS/Finance retrofit remains named work, not falsely described as built. The other Done-bar evidence is in §179. To close the separate retry-container hazard, `renderErrorPanel` now reads `errPanel.parentElement` when clicked, requires it to remain connected, and gives that live parent to its callback. The OMS/Yard/Cockpit, tenant-entitlement and doctype-config refresh handlers accept the live target. A Playwright synthetic failure moved the panel from a scratch node into `#view-root`, removed scratch, then clicked Retry; the callback received connected `#view-root`, recovery rendered visibly, and no page error fired. `node --check public/app.js` plus syntax checks for the four changed ES modules passed. The item is closed with the accepted residual called out in both checklists; no Go/schema/route change.

## 185. BLD-045 browser performance — measured, budgets missed, remains open (2026-10-04; QA evidence, no commit)

Three real Chromium contexts measured cold LCP, native Event Timing, long tasks, cached switching and keyboard search against an isolated 1,000-Item dataset (50 rendered per page), viewport 1365×768, 4× CPU, 40 ms latency and 5 Mbps. LCP was 2.52/4.02/3.52 s (p75 4.02 s vs 1.5 s); candidate INP was 168/144/168 ms (p75 168 ms vs 200 ms); cached-switch p95 was 428 ms vs 250 ms; maximum CLS 0.083; longest tasks 0.80/1.34/1.24 s. Keyboard search preserved its full input and focus indicators were visible, but each context emitted four `onPOSScanKeyDown is not defined` page errors. A separate fast 12-cycle burst hit the normal IP request limit and raised toast-induced CLS to 0.26, so it is disclosed as stress evidence, not treated as a clean user journey. BLD-045 remains open: constrained LCP and navigation budgets are missed, a browser error needs triage, and long-session/error-free evidence is still missing. No application performance change is claimed.

## 184. Stage 51 — remaining items closed: stale Store module removed, the id=code/SKU invariant finally applied to bulk import, guides documented end-to-end, and a read-only prod audit script for 51.1a (2026-10-04; code + tests + docs, no commit)

Stage 51 had three open items left, two of them explicitly `[needs design decision]`. Both decisions were put to the user and answered, so all three were worked.

**51.8 — the stale "Store" module (decision: remove).** `knownModules` (`engines/role_templates.go`) still declared a `Store` module whose only doctype, `Stores`, `db/migrations_stage30_5_5_retire_stores.sql` permanently folded into `Location` on 2026-08-02 — the user's own earlier call. Two tests failed on it continuously: `TestKnownModulesMatchTheTenantSchema`'s stale-entry arm, and `TestAdministratorAndAuditorCoverTheStoreModule`, which asserted role grants on a doctype that no longer ships and so could only ever fail. (A 2026-09-30 note had recorded these as "now passing"; re-run here, they still failed — that observation was against different DB state.) Removed the entry, and rather than deleting the second test's coverage, **rewrote it generically**: `TestAdministratorAndAuditorCoverEveryShippedDoctype` now sweeps every doctype the live schema declares and checks Administrator resolves `manage` and Auditor `read` on each, honouring Auditor's deliberate payroll `AccessNone` overrides. That preserves AUD-07's original intent (verify through *resolved* grants, not just a name-set comparison), is strictly stronger than the one hardcoded assertion, and cannot rot the same way when a future migration retires another doctype. It passed on the first run across the full doctype set, which also confirms no *other* module is silently uncovered.

**The 51.9-flagged bulk-import gap (real defect, fixed).** Stage 51.1's fix carries a comment claiming it covers "every caller (UI, CSV import, API)". It does not: CSV import never reaches `handleGenericDoc`, it lands in `engines/import.go`'s `importBatch`, which drew its own `id` from `GenerateSequence` — a *separate* draw from the same series the row's `code` came from. Every bulk-imported Master therefore had `id != code`, and since every Link field resolves by `id`, each one was permanently unreferenceable: 51.1's exact bug, on the one caller 51.1 believed it had already fixed. Fixed at a shared choke point rather than by copying the rule: new `engines.IsMasterDoctype` and `engines.ApplyMasterIDCodeInvariant` (`engines/doctype.go`) now hold the single definition, `handleGenericDoc`'s inline block was repointed at it, and `importBatch` runs the identical rule. Also wired `PrepareItemVariantCode` into the import path — 51.9 found it had exactly one caller, so Design ID/Combination ID generation was unreachable from bulk import and that pass's client migration had to pre-compute all 34 item codes by hand in a transform script.

**One further real finding, caught by live-running the new test rather than by reading the code**: `BulkImportCSV` has a *file-level* mandatory-column preflight (`missingMandatoryColumns`) that rejects an upload outright if the `code` header is absent — so the SKU-generation fix was unreachable, because the one flow whose entire purpose is a server-generated code was required to supply a `code` column anyway. Added a deliberately narrow exception: `code` is not demanded for an `Item` import that carries a `family` column, mirroring exactly what `view-documents.js` already does on the single-record form (code becomes server-numbered the moment a family is set). Pinned by a negative control — an Item file *without* a family column, and every other doctype, still requires `code`.

**51.1a (decision: read-only audit only).** Wrote `scripts/audit_master_id_code_drift.sql` — six sections: per-doctype scale, stranded/healthy ratio, a **collision check** that must be empty before any backfill (two records claiming one identity), a **blast-radius** count of transactions already pointing at the stray ids, a 50-row sample, and a per-tenant headline. Deliberately in `scripts/`, **not** `db/`, because `db/migrate.go:361` executes every `.sql` file in that directory and this must never run as a migration. Validated end-to-end against the live dev database: it runs clean and found **12 stranded rows of 503** in `tenant_default` (Item ×4, plus Brand/CartonType/Channel/DockDoor/HoldCode/ReasonCode/Trailer/Zone), zero collisions, and 10 transactions already referencing stray ids. Nothing was written and no production connection was made. Section 6 was initially a `DO` block using `RAISE NOTICE`; that output lands on stderr and interleaved unpredictably with the result sets, so it was rebuilt with `\gexec` to emit ordinary result sets.

**51.7 — guides documented end-to-end**, walking the real screens rather than paraphrasing the checklist. `USER_GUIDE.md`: new **§6.4** (barcodes generated automatically at goods receipt, including the honest "it runs *after* the receipt posts, so a failure never reverses stock — it goes to the system log, not to you"), new **§8d** with four subsections (the jewellery Item fields as a table; what Design vs. Combination/SKU actually mean; the full create-a-Design-then-its-Combinations walkthrough; and the six attributes that shape a SKU plus why weight deliberately does not), new **§8.1.1** (the 51.2 quick-create return, including that it returns after *create* but not after *edit*, and that it forgets if you wander off), a master-numbering paragraph in **§6.1**, and six new "How do I…?" index rows. `ADMIN_GUIDE.md`: new **§B.3.2.1** (industry-profile lock and the override flow), a **"+ New Series"** subsection under §B.3.2, new **§B.3.3.1** (GRN barcode automation from the administrator's side — chiefly *check the log, not the user*), and new **§B.3.3.2** (the two bulk-import corrections, pointing at the audit script).

**Two doc inaccuracies caught by checking the source instead of trusting the draft**, both corrected before they shipped: the industry selector is in the **top bar**, not under Settings, and its Override flow is a *typed industry code* prompt followed by a confirm and then the mandatory reason — not "pick the new industry from a dropdown"; and an Item's Code is filled in **at save**, not pre-filled live in the box as you type. Also fixed a pre-existing defect this pass's own addition would have made worse: `USER_GUIDE.md` had **two different sections both numbered §8.1**, making the new §8.1.1 ambiguous — the field-validation one became **§8e**, consistent with the §8a–§8d lettered series it physically sits among.

**Verified**: `go build`/`go vet` clean; `gofmt` clean; `doclint` 320 files / 0 findings; full `go test ./engines/ -p 1 -count=1` green including the three new DB-backed tests (`TestBulkImportCSVRestoresTheMasterIDCodeInvariant`, `TestBulkImportCSVGeneratesDesignBasedSKUForItemVariants`, `TestBulkImportCSVStillRequiresCodeWithoutAFamilyColumn`) and `TestApplyMasterIDCodeInvariantNoOpCases`. The audit script was run for real against the dev DB.

**Concurrent-session note, important for whoever picks this up.** Another session was actively editing this tree throughout (files written mid-run, at 00:42–00:43). All verification was therefore done in an isolated throwaway git worktree carrying this tree's state minus the one file that would not compile, and **three suite failures were confirmed as belonging to that other session or to the environment, not to this work**: `internal/server`'s `TestEveryAPIMiddlewareRouteIsClassified` (their brand-new uncommitted `POST /api/v1/security/csp-report` route, absent at HEAD, not yet added to `route_capabilities.go`); `internal/kb`'s `TestDriftGuardsUnmappedScreensSummary` (their uncommitted +29 lines across `drift.go`/`drift_test.go`; `drift.go` never reads `docs/guides/`, so these doc edits cannot affect it); and `db`, which hardcodes port 5435 while the running Postgres is on **5490**. Nothing in `engines/sticker_document_source.go`, `handlers_qz_print.go`, `handlers_procurement_pim2.go` or `internal/kb/` was touched by this pass.

**Still open in Stage 51**: 51.1a's backfill decision itself (the audit script is the input to it, not the fix) and, per the standing 2026-09-22 instruction, **none of Stage 51 is to be deployed to production until told** — it batches with the rest of the tree.

## 183. Stage 52 — sticker/label module documented end-to-end, plus the defect that pass found (2026-10-04; docs + code, no commit)

Stage 52's code (52.1-52.5) had been built and live-verified back on 2026-09-27; what was left open was **52.6**, the guide documentation. The Stage-52 content had only ever landed in `docs/guides/QZ_PRINTING_SETUP.md` — the two guides 52.6 actually names carried nothing beyond a sidebar mention and a pointer, so a user starting from the User Guide had no path to the feature at all.

**What was written.** `USER_GUIDE.md` gained **§7A "Printing Barcode Stickers and Labels"**, placed after §7 because both printable source doctypes (GRN, Transfer Order) are §6/§7 documents: §7A.1 the transaction-driven print run, §7A.2 the category template designer, §7A.3 the manual/re-print panel, §7A.4 the print history. Wired into the navigation the way the rest of the guide is — four "How do I…?" rows, a §7A marker in the §3 module map, a cross-reference from §6 step 9 where the GRN is posted, and a glossary entry. `ADMIN_GUIDE.md` gained **§B.3.4**, covering the administrator-side facts a user guide should not carry: the backward-compatibility guarantee (no templates configured = byte-identical output to pre-Stage-52), the role permission matrix, the three-step resolution order, mm/DPI sizing including the 300dpi-printer trap, the audit-trail split between `sticker_print_log` and `print_job_log`, and 52.7 named as the documented extension point.

**Three doc drifts found by verifying each claim against source rather than against the existing prose** — this is the part worth recording. (1) `ADMIN_GUIDE.md`'s printing bullet asserted "Nothing depends on this being set up" across every print screen; Sticker Printing hard-requires a Printer record even on the browser-fallback path, so the claim was scoped to QZ Tray with the exception stated. (2) A first draft of both new sections repeated that bullet's implication that the Printer needs **Default For** = `Sticker`; `resolveQZPrinter` honours an explicit `printer_code` ahead of the role, so stickers do not need it — corrected before shipping rather than after. (3) `QZ_PRINTING_SETUP.md`'s field-palette list omitted **Mfg Date**, which `STICKER_DESIGNER_FIELDS` has always offered; added, plus a Status note and cross-links to the two new sections so the three documents point at each other instead of drifting apart again.

**A real defect the documentation pass found (52.8).** Walking §7A.1's steps literally — rather than describing the code from the outside — surfaced a bug nobody had reported: the transaction print keyed its line selection on SKU alone, while the resolver deliberately keeps a SKU received on two lots as two separate lines. On a batch-tracked GRN the two rows therefore shared one checkbox and one copy count, and the per-row **Print** button printed both lots, contradicting the "print one line" behaviour the feature exists to provide. Stage 52.5's tests had covered the resolver's lot-splitting but never selected one of two same-SKU lines. Fixed at the choke point both print paths already share: `StickerLineSelection{SKU, BatchNo, Copies}` replaces the old parallel `selectedSKUs`/`copyOverrides` parameters, so matching and copy resolution happen once instead of twice, and a new `stickerSelections` helper resolves both endpoints' requests identically. The change is backward-compatible in both directions — the endpoints still accept `skus`/`copies_override`, and a SKU-only selection still means "every lot of this SKU" — so the manual scan flow and any existing client are untouched. The review table gained a **Batch/Lot** column that appears only when a document actually carries lots. `TestPrintStickersForDocumentSelectsOneLotOfASKU` pins the four behaviours involved.

Also documented as user-facing pitfalls, all three verified in `engines.ResolveStickerTemplate`: `Item.category` is unvalidated free text with no master behind it, so a mistyped category silently falls through to the default layout; `Status = Inactive` is the retire switch; and the default-template flag is effectively single-occupancy because the resolver's query carries no `ORDER BY`, so two defaults means an arbitrary winner.

**Live-verified**, not just unit-tested: a scratch build on port 8097 against a throwaway Item/Printer/two-lot GRN exercised the real HTTP path (which is where the new JSON field could have failed independently of the engine). `GET /api/v1/stickers/preview` returned both lots; `POST /api/v1/stickers/print` with `lines: [{LOT-B}]` returned exactly LOT-B at qty 6, with `[{LOT-A, copies: 11}, {LOT-B}]` returned 11 and 6 respectively, and the legacy `skus: ["…"]` and no-selection forms both still returned both lots at 4 and 6 — i.e. the old shape is genuinely unchanged. The QZ silent-print endpoint emitted 3 ZPL labels for `copies: 3` on one lot and 10 for the whole document, confirming both paths resolve the selection identically. Fixtures and their `sticker_print_log` rows were deleted afterwards and the scratch server stopped.

That live pass also turned up **52.9**, left open rather than fixed: `sticker_print_log` carries no batch column, so now that one lot can be printed alone the history cannot say which lot a run covered. The fix is small and additive but changes schema and deploy for an audit improvement nobody asked for, so it is recorded as a decision for the user.

`go build ./...`, `go vet`, `gofmt` on the touched Go files, `node --check` on both JS files, `go run ./cmd/doclint -root . -strict` (320 files, 0 findings) and the full Stage 52 engine suite (including the new regression test) are clean. **Three failures in the tree are not from this work**, each confirmed by what they name: `internal/kb`'s `TestDriftGuardsUnmappedScreensSummary` (a fixture-only test whose expectation another session's in-flight `drift.go` edits have outgrown); `TestModuleManifestCatalog` (unclassified route `POST /api/v1/security/csp-report`, from the concurrent Stage 47.8 CSP work); and `TestAttackSurfaceManifestIsCurrent`, which **passed during this session and then started failing** on `incremental_migrations: 166 -> 167` when a concurrent session wrote `db/migrations_stage53_1_location_sellable.sql` partway through. This Stage adds no route and no migration, so `attack_surface.json` was deliberately **not** regenerated - doing so would fold another session's in-flight Stage 53 work into a tracked security artifact.

The targeted payloads rendered inert (`xss=0`). A bounded CSP report endpoint and strict same-origin report-only candidate are active. **Not complete:** most source locations remain unclassified/unmigrated; enforced CSP still includes `'unsafe-inline'`, and the report-only findings plus full shared-renderer/session-revocation matrix remain open. The pentest scope now describes this as retirement-in-progress, not an accepted exception.

**BLD-046:** the job-handler contract now carries a cancellation context; operator cancellation signals a running handler, durable status watching covers cancellation from another process, and cancellation remains terminal rather than consuming a retry. Webhook delivery observes the context. This closes only the worker/operator leg: abandoned synchronous HTTP imports and reports still do not cancel every in-flight DB operation. Required normal/oversized/interrupted three-round acceptance and DB-backed cancellation tests were not run because no isolated DB was provisioned; BLD-046 remains open.

Logs/backups and retention are observations only because no approved numeric caps exist; database size was not measured. Three independent release artifacts and growth-baseline evidence remain open.

**BLD-052:** system/ops messages are redacted and byte-bounded; the tenant-aware queue monitor has configurable backlog/wait thresholds, a cooldown and recovery messages. Local unit tests passed. Correlation request→transaction→outbox, backup-drill wiring and delivery to an accountable responder are unproven; `OPS_ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` is unset, and no external alert was sent.

**BLD-055/50.6a:** archived the prior full handover content in `docs/archive/ai-handover-2026-10-03.txt` with a distinct archive ID and shortened the live handover. Reviewed/generated the 320-entry document register, registered the seven security documents and other unregistered artifacts, and removed portable-path findings. `go run ./cmd/doclint -strict` is clean. Brain pages were generated, checked and published only through the TEMP-stage/copy updater: 37 regions, 1,058 repository files, 100% coverage, zero unclaimed. **50.6a is closed; BLD-055/056 stay open** for clean-checkout, three-safety-round and remaining Stage 48 owner/content/migration/human acceptance.

**Verification:** Node syntax checks for shell/theme/QA harness/all view modules; Chromium payload harness; `go build ./...`; focused doclint/releasebudget/server/KB/log/alert tests; `go test ./internal/securityscan -count=1`; strict doclint; release-budget measurement; Brain generated check — all passed. Security scan initially exposed the intended new CSP route, queue monitor, `releasebudget` CLI and `RETAIN_DAYS` measurement; the reviewed surface was regenerated to TEMP and copied in, after which the full security-surface tests passed. No commit, deploy, migration, shared-database mutation, or external delivery was performed.

## 181. Stage 50 — BLD-041 completed: native ESM screen loading meets both cold budgets, preserves entitlement/error/help/print paths, no bundler or npm scripts (2026-10-03, code + tests + docs)

**This supersedes the intermediate classic-script implementation and open status recorded in §180.** Expanded the split to 18 native ES modules; `public/app.js` retains the app shell, shared services, navigation and the existing access checks. A route's module is fetched only after `/api/v1/me/modules` has returned and `isMenuModuleVisible` permits it. `loadViewModule()` caches the in-flight import/result to avoid duplicate requests and adds a cache-busting retry URL after a failed import; the existing retry panel handles failure. No bundler, build output, package install or npm script is required. POS, standalone Returns and RF traceability have distinct modules, so first paint on POS does not download the other screens. Help and cross-domain reports/PIM actions use explicit lazy bridges. Shared document-table, formatting, QZ and print-sheet helpers stay in the shell to avoid a dependency waterfall when entering those screens.

**Cold transfer proof (three independent Playwright/Edge-Chromium contexts over actual HTTP, `Accept-Encoding: gzip`, `Cache-Control: no-store`; all three runs identical):** initial JavaScript including the required authorized POS screen was **114,849 bytes (112.16 KiB)** against 120 KiB; cold-core including `index.html`, `styles.css`, `db.js`, `components/erp-typeahead.js`, `app.js`, `qz-print.js` and that POS module was **153,186 bytes (149.60 KiB)** against 180 KiB. The measured script parts were 858 B, 3,095 B, 90,022 B, 6,023 B and 14,851 B respectively. POS module fetch followed the entitlement response and occurred once. Unauthenticated and module-disabled deep links rendered the appropriate login/unavailable states without fetching view modules. Separate Returns and RF routes requested only their own chunk. A forced failed POS import displayed Retry, then succeeded with zero page errors; Help fetched its module only on click; the browser print fallback rendered a sticker and invoked `window.print()`. Physical QZ hardware/printer behavior is not claimed by this smoke test.

`node --check` passed for `public/app.js` and all 18 `public/view-*.js` files. `go build ./...` and `go vet ./...` passed. The first full suite identified only stale static-root inventory in the already-modified `docs/security/attack_surface.json` (22 recorded files versus 36); comparison to a temp-generated surface confirmed route registrations and all other inventory matched the shared source tree. Regenerated the artifact from the current tree, then ran `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` to completion on a second freshly migrated disposable PostgreSQL database (167 migrations); all packages passed, including `internal/securityscan`. The disposable server used loopback port 5542, isolated from the shared 5490 instance. `graphify update .` exited successfully with SQL-parser-unavailable and graph-HTML-size warnings; the graph/report were refreshed. Temporary extraction/measurement harnesses were removed. The curated brain updater completed at 100.0% region coverage with 0 unclaimed files. Nothing staged or committed; no API/schema/Go production change was part of BLD-041.

The disposable server is stopped; no process remains on its temporary fixture. Windows policy rejected removal of the verified TEMP targets, so the isolated data directory, log, and generated surface candidate remain inactive under `the workstation-local TEMP fixture directory named erp-bld041-postgres-20261002*` / `erp-bld041-attack-surface-20261002.json`. The shared Postgres on 5490 was not stopped or modified.

Files touched for this item: `public/app.js`, 18 native `public/view-*.js` chunks, `public/index.html` asset version, `package.json`, `README.md`, A-32 audit status, `docs/security/attack_surface.json` static-root inventory, and the BLD-041/micro-checklist/handover/brain records. Full checklist detail: `docs/product/erp-build-checklist.md` BLD-041; compact status: `docs/micro_checklist.md` 47.10.5 and 50.14.
