## 160. Stage 50 — BLD-021 (module/capability manifest consistency) verified and closed (2026-09-20/22, code + tests + docs)

This session dispatched BLD-021 to a separate build thread on 2026-09-20 (file mtimes ~14:19-14:36) while continuing BLD-017 work here; that thread wrote real, substantial code but never ran the doc-sync step, so the checklist/ledger/handover still showed it as open two days later. This section verifies and closes it. HEAD unchanged at `82f5517`; nothing committed.

**What the 2026-09-20 thread built.** `docs/product/capability-register.json` moved to schema v3, adding a validated `module_keys` array per capability (format `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$`, no duplicates) so the product capability register and the executable module catalog are linked by data, not by convention. `cmd/doclint/capabilities.go` generates `docs/generated/capability-catalog.md` and `requirements-traceability.md` from that register rather than hand-maintaining a parallel table — the same "generate a check, don't hand-maintain a parallel table" pattern `route_capabilities.go`'s existing `TestEveryAPIMiddlewareRouteIsClassified`/`TestNoStaleRouteCapabilityEntries` already established, reused rather than duplicated. Two new test files derive the manifest from real source rather than asserting against a second hand-written list: `engines/module_manifest_test.go` (module/package/report/prerequisite consistency, unknown-module/package rejection, per-module worker eligibility toggling) and `internal/server/module_manifest_test.go` (AST-parses `routes.go` and every `engines.Start*(workerCtx,...)` worker call to its real `listTenantSchemas` module filter, regex-extracts `public/app.js`'s `MENU_MODULE_MAP` navigation table and `renderViewContent`'s dispatch switch, and drives real HTTP requests through `httptest` with live entitlement toggling to prove disabled modules actually deny their routes/workers/reports/exports end to end — generic DocType CRUD/import/reactivation entry points, the public API credential surface, and report catalog/run/export/download, including that a previously-completed export's saved CSV does not leak on download once its module is disabled after the fact).

**2026-09-22 verification (this session).** `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean repo-wide. Stood up a fresh disposable Postgres 16.3 cluster (portable install at `the workstation-local PostgreSQL tools directory`, since the 2026-09-17/18 sessions' `:5460`/`:5461` clusters were no longer running — machine had been idle since 2026-09-20) on loopback `:5462` (`%TEMP%/erp-build-20260922`), 161/161 migrations applied cleanly via `go run ./cmd/server -migrate`. All nine BLD-021 test functions across `engines`, `internal/server` and `cmd/doclint` green across three independent passes (default order, then two `-shuffle=on` seeds), each against its own freshly-`initdb`'d, freshly-migrated database.

**One real gap found and fixed**: `go run ./cmd/doclint` reported `docs/generated/capability-catalog.md` as `generated-drift` (stale against the new schema-v3 register — the 09-20 thread changed the source and the test fixtures but never regenerated the real repo's projection). Regenerated it via `go run ./cmd/doclint -write-catalog <TEMP staging root>` then `Copy-Item` into `docs/generated/` — the same TEMP-stage-then-copy pattern `docs/brain/update-brain.ps1` already uses, needed here too because Controlled Folder Access blocks a freshly-built `go run` binary from writing under `docs/generated/` directly (confirmed: direct `-write-catalog .` failed with "Access is denied", the TEMP+copy path succeeded). That regeneration changed the file's bytes, which meant `docs/generated/governance-manifest.json`'s hand-pinned sha256 for it (schema calls it a "reviewed" checksum; no generator writes this manifest itself) was now also wrong — recomputed the LF-normalized sha256 and updated it by hand. `requirements-traceability.md`'s content and pinned checksum were unaffected (module keys don't appear in that projection). Also found and removed one self-inflicted stray file named `nul` at the repo root — Git Bash interprets a literal `2>nul` as a real filename rather than the Windows null device, and this session's own earlier `where postgres/pg_ctl/initdb` probes had created it; it was the one file `pwsh docs/brain/update-brain.ps1 -Check` reported as unclaimed by any region. Ran the real (non-check) `update-brain.ps1` afterward to pick up all the new/changed files from the 09-20 batch; now 37 regions, 100.0% coverage, 0 unclaimed. Final `go run ./cmd/doclint` is clean except the 14 pre-existing findings that are BLD-055's scope (seven/eight unregistered security docs, the handover-budget and nonportable-link warnings), none of which this item touches or owns.

**Not part of this item, left alone deliberately**: BLD-022–032 (the rest of Stage 50.12) remain open — BLD-022 formally `DEPENDS on BLD-021/023`, now half-unblocked (021 done, 023 still a DECISION item); BLD-031 was also dispatched to a separate thread the same day this item was, but no file evidence of it exists in the tree as of this session (no outbox/webhook contract test files, no mtime cluster matching BLD-031's shape) — record it as not started, not assume it landed elsewhere. **BLD-018 — three native Go fuzz targets, bounded campaigns, zero crashes.** No new dependency (stdlib `testing` fuzzing only, Go 1.27.1 already supports it). Three pure, side-effect-free identifier/parser boundaries, chosen specifically because each already has real audit or product history: `FuzzCompareMigrationNames` (`db/migrate_fuzz_test.go`) - the exact comparator whose bug caused AUD-02, checked for reflexivity and antisymmetry as a total-order comparator must have, not just panic-freedom; `FuzzNormalizePhone` (`engines/phone_fuzz_test.go`) - the one phone-cleaning entry point every field in the app goes through, checked against its Valid/Reason contract; `FuzzValidateBarcodeCheckDigit` (`engines/pim_barcode_fuzz_test.go`) - the GS1 mod-10 check-digit validator, checked to only ever reject barcode-shaped input and to never reject its own generator's output. Each ran three independently-timed 30-second campaigns: `compareMigrationNames` 642k/559k/681k executions, `NormalizePhone` 94k/138k/145k, `ValidateBarcodeCheckDigit` 170k/252k/233k - roughly 3.1M total, explicitly bounded and not described as exhaustive. **Zero crashes, zero hangs, zero regressions needing minimization.** One real finding surfaced by the seed corpus (before any timed campaign even started, so genuinely fast feedback): the first draft of `NormalizePhone`'s invariant assumed Valid and Reason-set were always exact opposites, but a blank/whitespace-only `raw` deliberately returns neither (an empty optional field, not a rejected value) - corrected the invariant to the real three-state contract rather than weakening the test to pass. `go build`/`go vet` clean. No `testdata/fuzz/` corpus needed checking in, since Go only persists corpus entries on a failure and none occurred - the three new `_test.go` files' own seed corpus now runs as part of every ordinary `go test ./...`.

## 158. Stage 50 — POSCart/override decision implemented, BLD-010/011 live-verified, two new focus/keyboard gaps found and fixed (2026-09-18, code + tests)

Continued §157's build thread: the two items it left open (§157's own "one new finding" and BLD-010/011's live verification). HEAD unchanged at `82f5517`; nothing committed. All verification against a fresh, disposable PostgreSQL 16.3 cluster on loopback port 5461 (deliberately new rather than reusing the 2026-09-17 session's still-running :5460 cluster, whose bootstrap role name could not be determined) plus a scratch `erp-server` on port 8462, never shared dev/production.

**POSPriceOverride/POSCart decision, made and implemented.** Presented the product/security owner with the exact mechanism (`engines/pos_quote.go`'s `ManualDiscountPct` measures reference-vs-actual subtotal across every line regardless of price source, so an approved item-level override's own discount is indistinguishable, at the cart-gate, from an unreviewed one) and two framings: exclude the already-approved amount from the cart-level threshold, or keep the two gates fully independent. **Decision: keep them independent** — an approved item-level override does not exempt its amount from the cart-level `POSCart` slab; both stay separate controls on purpose. Tracing the actual code path (`handleCheckout` → discount measured → `RequiredApproverRoleForAmount("POSCart", ...)` → `SubmitForApproval` when a role is returned → cart claims `Pending Approval`, nothing posts → a later `POST /api/v1/approval/decide` with `Approved` calls `engines.FinalizePOSCheckout`, which prices from the cart's already-resolved-at-submit-time line data and marks the `POSPriceOverride` `Consumed`) confirmed the shipped code already implements exactly the decided behavior end-to-end — no application-logic change was needed, only the test's own expectation was wrong. Rewrote `TestStage472ApprovedOverridePricesTheSaleAndIsSpentByIt` (`internal/server/pos_pricing_stage47_2_test.go`) to assert the decided flow: checkout now asserts `status: "pending_approval"`/`required_role: "Store Manager"`, the override is asserted still `Approved` (not `Consumed`) and the cart still `Pending Approval` while the decision is outstanding, then a Store Manager decides `POST /api/v1/approval/decide {doctype: POSCart, decision: Approved}`, after which the original assertions (line priced 900, `reference_price` 1000, `price_source` Override, cart `Paid`, override `Consumed`) hold. Verified green against a fresh scratch DB, and again alongside the rest of `pos_pricing_stage47_2_test.go`'s suite (8 tests, no regressions). This was the one remaining blocker to BLD-015's full-suite-green gate.

**BLD-010/011 (AUD-08/AUD-09) live-verified — a working Playwright/Chromium install existed on this machine all along.** §157 recorded "no Playwright install found cached" after checking npm's global path; a full install was actually present at `the workstation-local node_modules Playwright package` with Chromium binaries already cached under `the local Playwright browser cache\chromium-1200`/`chromium-1234` (confirmed by launching it directly). Reused `docs/qa/audit-ui-tasks.cjs`'s own reproduction shape (the exact script that originally found AUD-08/09: 3 rounds, viewports 1440×900/390×844/1440×900, CDP `Accessibility.getFullAXTree` against the Vendor form) via a new scratch verification script (not committed — throwaway evidence against a throwaway server, same as prior BLD items' live passes), extended with checks the original script didn't need since it was hunting for different defects:

- **BLD-010 (AUD-08): clean.** Across all 3 rounds, twice: 0 unnamed accessibility-tree controls, 0 duplicate `id`s anywhere in the live document, every rendered field's `<label for>` correctly resolves to its control's `id`.
- **BLD-011 (AUD-09): two real gaps found beyond the shipped fix, both fixed in `public/app.js`.**
  1. **Post-save focus silently fell to `<body>`.** `closeDynamicModal()`'s existing `restoreFocusAfterModalClose()` correctly focused the "+ New Vendor" trigger button the instant Save succeeded — but `handleDynamicFormSubmit`'s success branch immediately follows with `renderView('doctype-table')`, which rebuilds the entire list and, in doing so, removes that exact (now-stale) button from the DOM and creates a fresh one in its place. Per browser spec, removing the currently-focused element resets focus to `<body>` with nothing to re-focus it afterward — so every successful create or edit was quietly dropping keyboard/screen-reader focus to the top of the document, the identical class of defect AUD-09 was opened to fix, just one step later in the same flow than the original fix covered. Diagnosed by chasing what first looked like an intermittent auth failure (`document.activeElement.textContent` on `<body>` includes the hidden `#login-screen` markup's text regardless of its `display:none`, since `textContent` — unlike `innerText` — ignores CSS visibility; a `activeElement === body` after save read as "session expired" until a `getComputedStyle`/`getBoundingClientRect` check on `#login-screen` proved it was never actually visible). Fixed with one line — focusing `#view-root` right after `renderView('doctype-table')` — reusing the exact fallback surface `restoreFocusAfterModalClose()` already treats as safe.
  2. **Escape did not close the dialog at all.** No keydown handler existed for `#dynamic-modal`, unlike this app's other dismissable surfaces (nav drawer, account menu, submenus each have their own `e.key === 'Escape'` handler). Fixed with one document-level `keydown` listener next to the existing focus helpers, calling the existing `closeDynamicModal()` choke point when `#dynamic-modal` has class `open`.

  Reverified after both fixes, each independently reproduced clean at least twice across several runs: Cancel-path restore (focus returns to the trigger), a "trigger row removed while the modal is open" case (falls back to `#view-root`, the scenario BLD-011's own text names explicitly), closed-dialog tab-reachability (0 of 40 `Tab` presses ever reached a control inside the closed `#dynamic-modal` — the `opacity:0`/`pointer-events:none` closed state, while not `display:none`, empirically does not admit keyboard focus in Chromium), a cleared-required-field submission correctly leaving the dialog open, and Escape now closing it with focus restored to the trigger. Chaining 8 sequential Playwright browser contexts in one Node process on this Windows machine is itself flaky (context-teardown timing/resource release, not the app under test — confirmed by isolating each scenario into its own single-context script, where every one of them passed reliably; the failures moved to a different scenario each full run, never the same one twice), so no single end-to-end run scored all six checks clean simultaneously — each has its own clean, isolated reproduction instead, which is what's cited above. **Not touched, remains open by design**: the other per-modal `classList.add('open')` dialog system (`openFieldModal`/schema field editor, `openImportModal`/CSV bulk import) — unaffected by either fix, scoped out explicitly as a separate follow-up in the prior session's own note, not attempted this pass.

`go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean repo-wide throughout. `node --check public/app.js` clean after both edits. Files touched: `internal/server/pos_pricing_stage47_2_test.go`, `public/app.js`. `docs/product/erp-build-checklist.md`, `docs/micro_checklist.md` updated to close BLD-010/011 and record the decision; this section. `git status` checked before and after — no other concurrent session's files were touched.

## 157. Stage 50 BLD-001–014 (Wave 0/1) built and verified, plus AUD-03/04 fixed and one new finding (2026-09-17, code + tests)

Executed the [build checklist](product/erp-build-checklist.md)'s next-thread prompt from §156: BLD-001's isolated environment, then the full BLD-003–014 release-blocker batch. HEAD unchanged at `82f5517`; nothing committed. All work verified against a disposable PostgreSQL 16.3 cluster on loopback port 5460 (four independently fresh-migrated databases across the session), never the shared dev DB or the frozen 2026-09-16 audit fixture on 5446/8178.

**BLD-001.** Fresh `initdb` cluster, `erp_build_main`/`erp_build_test` databases, server binary built to `%TEMP%`. Smoke test: 161/161 migrations clean on a genuinely empty database, idempotent no-op re-run, `-migrate-status` clean, `GET /api/v1/health` 200 against the isolated DB. Evidence in `.claude/erp-build-20260917/` (gitignored).

**BLD-004 — AUD-02 remaining acceptance.** The recorded comparator fix was preserved and re-verified independently, then extended past the fresh-install case §155 already covered: an **old-schema incremental upgrade** (deleted the ledger rows for the last 10 migrations on an already-migrated database, confirmed `-migrate-status` found exactly those 10 pending and applying them converged cleanly on top of the untouched-but-already-correct underlying schema); an **interrupted retry** (hid `tenant_default.doctype_fields` mid-run so a real pending migration failed with `relation ... does not exist`, confirmed the run stopped with `migration failed: ... (rolled back, ledger not updated)`, nonzero exit, the ledger at exactly 160/161 — not 159, not 161 — then restored the table and confirmed the retry applied only the one missing file and reached 161/161); the existing `TestVerifyMigrationChecksums` (NULL-backfill, tampered-checksum, ghost-file) re-run green against this session's own databases. **A real, independent defect found and fixed in the same area**: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s "Apply database schema" step piped `ls db/*.sql | sort` to `psql`, a byte-wise lexicographic sort — not `db/migrate.go`'s numeric-aware `compareMigrationNames` its own comment claimed to match. Verified the divergence directly (`ls db/*.sql | sort` puts `..._47_7_6_audit_archive.sql` before `..._47_7_audit_evidence.sql`, AUD-02's exact defect, reproduced independently in CI's own ordering rule). Fixed by building the real `erp-server` binary in CI and running its own `-migrate`, removing the second reimplementation rather than re-deriving its ordering in shell — CI, `deploy/migrate.sh` and a local install now share one runner.

**BLD-005/006 — AUD-03/AUD-04.** Rechecked ownership first: `deploy/deploy.ps1`/`README.md` diff unchanged across the session (a peer's 2026-09-16 auto-rollback work, idle, not mid-flight); `deploy/remote_deploy.sh` untracked from the same peer. Ran `docs/qa/audit-deploy-mock.py` (the audit's own local command-double harness, real command doubles, no SSH/service/webhook) against the **current** source for the first time — the original 2026-09-16 audit predates `remote_deploy.sh`'s existence entirely, so this had never been exercised. First run: only 3 of 12 cases (`release_consistent`) were true — every failure path except the untested happy path. Two real, independent bugs, both fixed:
- **`public/` never actually rolled back.** `deploy.ps1` scp'd the new frontend straight over the live `$RemoteDir/public`, before `remote_deploy.sh` ever ran its own "pre-deploy snapshot" — so that snapshot was already a copy of the NEW release, and "restoring" it on rollback restored the new frontend again. Exactly the binary/frontend mismatch class of bug the 2026-09-13 incident was suffered from once already. Fixed by staging to `public.new` (mirroring how `erp-server.new` already worked) and moving the real snapshot/activation/rollback entirely inside `remote_deploy.sh`.
- **A `systemctl restart` failure was unhandled under `set -e`, in two different bad ways.** The initial activation restart, bare and unguarded, killed the whole script on failure before any rollback ran — files already swapped, service never confirmed, no `DEPLOY-*` marker printed at all. Guarded with `|| true`, letting the existing `wait_healthy`/rollback logic make the real call regardless of which symptom caused it. Deeper: even guarded, a restart failure where the OLD process was still answering would report `DEPLOY-OK` for a deploy that never took effect — a real false success, not hypothetical, caught by the mock's `restart-fails` case reporting `NEW_BINARY`/exit 0 with the guard alone. Fixed by making `/api/v1/health` echo the ldflags-injected `git_commit`, and `wait_healthy` verify it matches the deploy's own commit for the initial activation check (not just a bare 200) — closing the exact "no false success" gap BLD-006's acceptance bar names. The rollback's own restart got the same `|| true` guard so its failure correctly falls through to the existing `DEPLOY-ROLLBACK-FAILED` branch instead of dying silently. The mock harness itself needed a matching fix (a `.running_commit` marker distinct from "which files are on disk", since nothing else in the sandbox models an actual persistent process) to be able to prove the restart-failure case at all. **After both fixes: 12/12 `release_consistent` true, correct exit codes, across three separate full harness invocations.** `deploy/README.md` updated to describe the corrected staging/health-check behavior.

**BLD-007 — AUD-05 remaining acceptance.** New `internal/server/package_selection_stage50_test.go`: all ten shipped `ProductPackages` driven one at a time through the real `POST /api/v1/admin/tenant/package` handler (not the engine function directly), cross-checking both the HTTP response body and the persisted entitlements against `ExpandPackagesToModules`; the same `aud05_block_pim_disable`-style fault injection driven through the handler (422, error names `pim`, state matches the error), plus a retry-after-fault-clears case proving convergence. A/B-verified against pre-fix `engines/modules.go` (swapped in `git show HEAD:...`, temporarily): the fault case returns 200 "updated" with `pim` still `enabled:true` in the response body — the exact false-success AUD-05 named — then correctly 422 once restored.

**BLD-008 — AUD-06 fixed.** `CheckTenantLimit` (`engines/tenant_limits.go`) treated every error from the limit lookup — connection/permission/timeout, not just "no row" — as "no limit configured", silently authorizing unlimited usage exactly when the check itself was broken. Fixed with `errors.Is(err, sql.ErrNoRows)` distinguishing the two; the one real call site (`handlers_admin_identity.go`'s max-users check) now falls back to 500 instead of 422 for the operational-failure shape, reusing `writeEngineError`'s existing type-based routing rather than a new mechanism. Three new tests (absent-config passes, configured-limit still enforced as `*ValidationError`, a renamed-table fault surfaces as a propagated error) — A/B-confirmed red against pre-fix code, green after, on 3 independently fresh-migrated databases.

**BLD-009 — AUD-07 fixed.** `knownModules` (`engines/role_templates.go`) was missing `"Store"` — the shipped `Stores` doctype's module — so `allModules(...)` silently left it out of both Administrator (Manage) and Auditor (Read); `TestKnownModulesMatchTheTenantSchema` (already failing in the original audit's own recorded suite runs) confirms this. One-line fix plus a new test resolving real `TemplateGrants` for Administrator/Auditor against the live schema (not just the module-name-set comparison) and confirming `RoleSuperAdmin` still resolves to the same Administrator template. Green on 3 independently fresh-migrated databases.

**BLD-012 — QA-DEF-01 fixed**, and its own premise corrected. The price-tamper test's comment claimed no default `POSCart` approval rule is shipped — true only of `db/migration.sql`; `db/migrations_stage20a_pos_maturity.sql` ships exactly `('POSCart', 10, NULL, 'Store Manager')` via the same `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` shape, so on any migrated schema the row is real, permanent, shipped tenant configuration, not test fixture data. The test's unconditional cleanup `DELETE`d it regardless — silently destroying real shipped policy on every run. Fixed by checking `RowsAffected()` and only deleting what the test itself inserted. Comment corrected in place. This also explained (not merely masked) `TestCheckoutToForecastIntegration`'s stale assumption: its item carries no server-side price, and Stage 47.2's server-authoritative pricing routes any unverifiable-price line to Manager approval once a tenant has configured any `POSCart` slab — true on every migrated schema now, not order-dependent. Fixed by seeding the item with a real `sale_price`/`mrp` matching the checkout's own request, the same pattern `posPricingFixture` already uses. Three orderings (default, two `-shuffle` seeds) green; the real shipped row confirmed still present afterward by direct query.

**BLD-013 — QA-DEF-02 fixed.** `docs/security/attack_surface.json` regenerated (via `-out %TEMP%\...` + copy, the documented Windows Controlled-Folder-Access workaround — twice this session, once after the audit-archive routes settled and again after BLD-014's dependency bump changed the recorded module-directness counts). `TestAttackSurfaceManifestIsCurrent`, `TestEveryAPIMiddlewareRouteIsClassified`, `TestNoStaleRouteCapabilityEntries` all green; the five new audit-archive routes carry real `LevelAdmin`/`admin.audit-logs` capability gates, not bare session auth.

**BLD-014 — toolchain fixed.** `govulncheck` against the locally-installed 1.26.5 toolchain found 6 reachable stdlib CVEs (all fixed in 1.26.6+) plus 22 in `golang.org/x/crypto v0.25.0` (unreachable by this code, but 14+ months stale). Installed 1.27.1 via `go install golang.org/dl/go1.27.1@latest` (a currently-supported release per Go's two-latest-majors policy); bumped `go.mod`'s directive, `golang.org/x/crypto` to v0.57.0 (`go mod tidy` also corrected both dependencies from stale `// indirect` to their real direct status), `.github/workflows/ci.yml`/`documentation-health.yml`'s pinned `go-version`, `Dockerfile`'s `golang:1.22-bookworm` → `golang:1.27-bookworm` (tag existence confirmed live), and `docs/security/dependency-ledger.json`/`dependency-inventory.md`/`secure-development-lifecycle.md`. `govulncheck` now reports 0 reachable findings (1 remaining advisory, `x/crypto/openpgp` unmaintained-package, genuinely unreachable and has no fix version — recorded, not hidden). Three clean build/vet/`govulncheck` rounds; a `GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64 CGO_ENABLED=0` cross-compile (matching `deploy.ps1`'s exact flags) also verified clean. **Deliberately not touched**: the operator's real `deploy.ps1` build toolchain at `%USERPROFILE%\go-portable\go` (still 1.22.12) — upgrading the actual production build tool is a larger, separate action than verifying the change locally; recorded as the next operator action in the dependency ledger's own drift note rather than silently left unstated.

**Frontend accessibility (BLD-010/011 — AUD-08/AUD-09), code fixed, live multi-context verification not yet performed.** The shared dynamic form builder (`public/app.js`, the same "generic record form" both AUD-08 and the original Vendor-dialog reproduction are about) rendered every field's `<label>` with no `for` and no matching control `id` — fixed with one `dyn-field-${currentDoctype}-${fieldname}` id threaded through the label and every field-type branch (Select/Link/Number/text). `openDynamicModal`/`closeDynamicModal` gained shared focus capture/restore (`document.activeElement` at open time, restored at close, falling back to `#view-root` — given a new `tabindex="-1"` — if the trigger element is gone, e.g. its row was deleted). `node --check` clean. **Not done**: a live 3-context Chromium/accessibility-tree pass matching the audit's own method (no Playwright install found cached on this machine this session); the other dialog system (bespoke per-modal `classList.add('open')` pairs elsewhere in `app.js`) does not share this fix — each has its own open/close function name, so this is a per-modal follow-up, not a second choke point; Escape-key closing was never wired for this modal either way, a separate, smaller pre-existing gap.

**One new finding, not fixed, needs a product decision — recorded, not resolved unilaterally.** A full suite run surfaced `TestStage472ApprovedOverridePricesTheSaleAndIsSpentByIt` failing (also present, unnamed, in the original 2026-09-16 audit's own machine summary alongside the three tests above that are now fixed — confirmed via `docs/qa/erp-audit-2026-09-16-summary.json`, so this is pre-existing, not a regression from anything in this session; all files it depends on are unmodified against HEAD). Root-caused via a temporary debug probe (reverted before commit-worthy state): the test's cashier-executed checkout of an already-approved 10% `POSPriceOverride` gets additionally gated by the same shipped `POSCart`/10%/Store-Manager rule BLD-012 investigated, because the override's measured discount happens to land exactly on that cart-level threshold — the checkout lands `pending_approval`, never reaches `finalizePOSCheckoutTx`, and the override is correctly never marked `Consumed` because the sale hasn't happened yet. Whether an amount already covered by a supervisor-approved item-level override should also count toward the cart-level discount-approval threshold is a real product/security policy question (redundant-but-safe layering vs. a confusing double-gate on the same money) — not something to resolve by picking a side in code. Flagged for a design decision; BLD-015's full-suite green gate is blocked on it until decided.

Repo-wide `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean throughout (final Go toolchain: 1.27.1). Two full `go test ./...` runs against the disposable cluster (default order, then `-shuffle=on`): all packages green except the one pre-existing, now-understood finding above. ## 155. Stage 50.7 (partial) — AUD-01/AUD-02/AUD-05 fixed and verified (2026-09-17, code + tests)

Three of the five release-impacting findings from §154's independent audit, each fixed at its actual root cause and verified against a live reproduction rather than by inspection alone. AUD-03/AUD-04 (deploy rollback / restart-failure recovery) are §151's active concurrent work and deliberately not touched here.

**AUD-01 — stored XSS.** `copyableCell` (`public/app.js:318`) injected `displayValue` straight into an HTML template string with no escaping — the one function nearly every record-list/table cell in the app renders through, which is exactly how a Vendor-only clerk's saved name reached a Super Admin's browser as executable markup. Fixed at that one choke point (`escapeHTMLText`, already used elsewhere in the file, applied unconditionally now) rather than at each call site — which also closed four more call sites with the identical unescaped shape that the audit didn't individually name (`r.sku`/`r.location_code`/`e.order_id` cells, and a separate unescaped `status`-badge injection two lines below the one the audit cited). Two call sites that used to pre-escape their own `displayValue` before calling `copyableCell` were simplified to stop double-escaping now that the choke point does it once. Verified with a standalone Node script reproducing the audit's exact payload (`<img src=x onerror=alert(document.domain)>`): confirmed the payload renders as inert escaped text in all three affected code paths, not as a real tag, after an initial version of the check itself gave a false positive (matching "onerror=" as plain text inside a properly-escaped attribute value — fixed the check, not the code, once that was understood). `node --check public/app.js` clean.

**AUD-02 — fresh migration failure.** `compareMigrationNames` (`db/migrate.go`) is a natural/alphanumeric sort: digit runs compare numerically, everything else compares as bytes. The bug is in the boundary between those two rules: `migrations_stage47_7_6_audit_archive.sql` vs `migrations_stage47_7_audit_evidence.sql` share `migrations_stage47_7_`, then one continues with a digit (`6`) and the other moves straight to descriptive text (`a`udit_evidence) — compared as raw bytes, `'6' < 'a'` always, so the sub-item migration sorted before the foundation migration it depends on, regardless of which one actually needed to run first. **Deliberately did not rename either file** — the checklist item's own text warns against silently renaming an applied migration, and `migrations_stage47_7_audit_evidence.sql` may already be applied under its current name on shared dev; renaming it would desync the migration ledger for whoever already has it applied. Fixed the comparator's general rule instead: at a digit-vs-non-digit boundary, the side still in a digit run is the deeper/later sub-item and sorts after, not compared as a raw byte. Checked the blast radius empirically before trusting it — a small standalone Go program sorted all 161 real migration filenames under the old and new comparator and diffed the results: 40 files move, every single one is the same shape (an unnumbered "foundation"/base file for stage N.M now correctly sorts before its own numbered N.M.K sub-items — 26.4, 26.5, 26.6, 26.7, 26.9, 26.12, 29, 37.1, 42.1, 42.5), none of which is a regression against a previously-verified order since AUD-02's own finding is that a genuinely fresh install had never been exercised at all before this audit. **Live-verified end to end**: started the shared portable Postgres (was down — `pg_ctl start`, benefits every concurrent session on this box, not just this fix), created a throwaway `aud02_scratch_test` database, ran `-migrate` against it with the unfixed code and reproduced the exact audit failure (`relation "tenant_default.audit_checkpoints" does not exist`), then recreated the database genuinely empty and re-ran with the fix — all 161 migrations applied cleanly in dependency order, `audit_evidence` before `audit_archive`, re-running was a correct idempotent no-op, `-migrate-status` clean. Scratch database dropped afterward; shared `custom_erp` dev database never touched. New pinned case in `db/migrate_test.go`; all pre-existing pinned pairs still pass unchanged.

**AUD-05 — failed package-disable reported as success.** `ApplyPackageSelection` (`engines/modules.go`) retries disabling a batch of modules across bounded passes to tolerate ordering (module A can't disable while dependent module B is still enabled, but B might disable first this pass, freeing A next pass) — but when a pass made zero progress, it `break`s out of the loop and falls straight through to `return nil`, even with modules still stuck in `toDisable`. Fixed: after the loop, a nonempty `toDisable` now returns a named error listing exactly which module(s) could not be disabled. `handleSetTenantPackage` (`internal/server/handlers_integrations_admin.go:813`) already surfaces `err.Error()` as a 422 — no handler change needed, only the engine's own honesty. Deliberately left `handleProvisionTenant`'s own `_ = engines.ApplyPackageSelection(...)` (fresh-tenant provisioning) untouched — that's an existing, commented, reasoned decision to be best-effort during provisioning, not the bug the audit found, and touching it would be scope creep past what AUD-05 actually asks for. **Real regression test** (`engines/modules_test.go`), built the same way the audit's own "fixture trigger" evidently was: a temporary Postgres `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger that unconditionally refuses to disable one module (`pim`), standing in for any real write failure `SetModuleEntitlement`'s retry design already has to tolerate — necessary because this codebase's only real module dependency (`rfq` needs `procurement`) is always bundled together in every `ProductPackage`, so no client-reachable input can organically produce a permanently-stuck disable either (the same honest gap the audit itself recorded: "no claim is made that an ordinary client can install the fault-injection trigger"). Confirmed the test actually discriminates: failed against the pre-fix code (`git stash` the fix, re-run, red), passed again once restored. Full entitlement-row snapshot/restore plus trigger/function cleanup via `defer`, confirmed zero residue in the shared dev database afterward by direct query (`pg_trigger`/`pg_proc`).

`go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean repo-wide throughout. Touched `public/app.js`, `db/migrate.go`, `db/migrate_test.go`, `engines/modules.go`, `engines/modules_test.go` (new) — none of them modified by any concurrent session at the time of edit (checked before each), confirmed still true afterward.

## 153. Stage 47.16.5 (partial) + 49.6.2 upgrade — automated payment-card-data scanner (2026-09-16, code + tests)

Closes the technical half of 47.16.5 ("automated schema/log/attachment checks prohibit full PAN/CVV/PIN/track data") by extending 49.1.4's existing no-bypass scanner (`internal/securityscan/bypass.go`) with a new `payment-card-data` category, rather than building a second scanning mechanism — the same reuse-the-choke-point reasoning every prior Stage 49 item in this tree already follows. Matches card-PAN/CVV/track-data/PIN-block field and struct-tag shapes; deliberately excludes bare `pan`/`pan_number` because `engines/field_formats.go`'s `panPattern` is India's unrelated Income Tax PAN, a real field this codebase legitimately stores in the clear — a scanner that confused the two would either miss card data hidden behind a `pan_number`-style rename or produce a false positive on every GST-related doctype.

Verified `engines/pinelabs.go` (the only real payment-provider integration in this codebase) stores only `transaction_id`/`terminal_id`/`cart_number`/`amount`/`status`/`payment_mode` before writing the checklist claim — read the actual struct/SQL, not assumed from the doc comment. Zero real findings against this tree today, which is the expected/correct result (matches 49.6.2's prior manual-grep finding, now upgraded from a one-time check to an executed regression that fails `go test ./...` if it ever changes).

**A clean tree alone can't tell "the pattern never matches" from "the pattern is silently broken"**, so a new self-contained test (`TestPaymentCardDataPatternDetectsRealShape`, `internal/securityscan/bypass_test.go`) plants a synthetic `card_number`/`cvv` struct in a `t.TempDir()` fixture and asserts the scanner actually fires on it, then plants a synthetic `pan_number` field in a second file and asserts the scanner does NOT fire on that one — proving both the detection and the tax-PAN exclusion, independent of anything this real repository currently contains.

`go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean repo-wide; `go test ./internal/securityscan/...` fully green including the existing `TestNoUnreviewedBypassPattern` (no reviewed-allowlist entry needed — the new category has zero real hits, which that test's own logic accepts silently). Touched only `internal/securityscan/bypass.go`/`bypass_test.go` and `docs/micro_checklist.md`'s 47.16.5/49.6.2 notes — no route, job, dependency or CLI command changed, so `docs/security/attack_surface.json` needs no regeneration.

## 151. Deploy auto-rollback — deploy.ps1/remote_deploy.sh (2026-09-16, tooling only)

Prompted by the user asking why the ERP wasn't opening: it wasn't actually down (verified `app.wholeops.in` live, 200, no console errors), but the screenshot they'd captured matched exactly the environment-caveat banner text baked into the currently-running `0e79425` binary, rendered with none of its CSS applied — consistent with the documented 2026-09-13 incident (`docs/ai_handover.md` §6) where a deploy hit `security_baseline.go`'s fail-fast gate, crash-looped the box for ~90s, and was rolled back **by hand** over SSH because `deploy.ps1` had no rollback logic at all. The user's actual ask: deploys must never leave the box broken unattended — auto-rollback on failure, always.

**New `deploy/remote_deploy.sh`**, shipped fresh by `deploy.ps1` on every run rather than assumed pre-installed like `migrate.sh` (a stale copy would silently defeat the whole point). Snapshots `erp-server`/`public/` to `.prev` before touching anything (db/ is deliberately not snapshotted — migrations are additive/backward-compatible by design, so the accepted posture from the 2026-09-13 incident is schema-stays-forward, binary/static-files-roll-back), applies migrations with the new binary, swaps it in, restarts, then polls for real health — `systemctl is-active` **and** an actual `curl` against `http://127.0.0.1:8080/`, up to 15 one-second retries, not the old single `sleep 2` guess. If the new build never comes up healthy, it **automatically restores the `.prev` snapshot and restarts**, re-verifies that came back healthy too, tails `journalctl` for the postmortem, and best-effort alerts `OPS_ALERT_WEBHOOK_URL` (reusing `backup.sh`'s existing `alert_ops` convention). `deploy.ps1` now branches on a `DEPLOY-OK` / `DEPLOY-ROLLED-BACK` / `DEPLOY-ROLLBACK-FAILED` marker instead of trusting a bare exit code.

Built as a standalone `.sh` file rather than growing `deploy.ps1`'s inline heredoc further, specifically because that heredoc's own comments already document two prior bugs from bash logic living inside a PowerShell double-quoted string (CRLF line endings from a Windows checkout, and `$var` silently eaten by PowerShell interpolation before bash ever saw it) — `.gitattributes` already forces LF for `deploy/*.sh`, so a real tracked file sidesteps both classes of bug for free. Every branch inside uses `if`, never a bare `cmd && cmd` statement, since a false left-hand side in a bare AND-list still trips the script's own `set -e`.

**Collided mid-build with a concurrent peer session (erp-17)**, who had independently added a different, overlapping mechanism (a manual `-Rollback` switch + `$RemoteDir/rollback/` snapshot) to the same file in response to the same underlying gap. Flagged via cross-session message rather than silently overwriting; erp-17 assessed the automatic health-check-triggered approach as strictly more complete than their manual flag and reverted their own change before continuing on unrelated work (49.14). `git diff -- deploy/` at close of this entry contains only this session's changes.

`bash -n deploy/remote_deploy.sh` and the PowerShell parser both pass clean. **Not yet exercised against a real box** — SB-021 (`docs/micro_checklist.md`'s "Production deploy blocker" section) still refuses any deploy of `main` before it ever reaches the health-check step, so the rollback path itself is unverified live. Verify it for real the next time a deploy is attempted, once SB-021 is decided.

A genuinely useful finding along the way, not fabricated to fill a row: 7 of the 25 CWE Top-25 entries (buffer overflow variants, use-after-free, out-of-bounds read/write, NULL pointer dereference) are structurally low-risk in this codebase specifically *because* it's Go with no cgo/unsafe in the production binary — a real, citable architectural property, not a checkbox filled for its own sake.

CIS benchmark targets were confirmed against the actual production droplet over SSH rather than assumed from an older note: Ubuntu **22.04.5 LTS**, PostgreSQL **14.24**. The exact CIS benchmark document edition/revision number is honestly left unpinned — that's real follow-up work (downloading the actual control list from cisecurity.org), not guessed at here.

Explicitly scoped out and said so in the document itself: a line-by-line audit of all ~350 ASVS requirements against this codebase. Chapter-level honesty was chosen over requirement-level fabrication — faking that granularity would be exactly the "checklist closed ≠ actually verified" gap Stage 47.17 exists to catch elsewhere in this project.

## 147. Stage 40.8/40.9/40.10 + Stage 44.7 — closing Stage 40 and Stage 44, and a QZ Tray CSP defect found in the process (2026-09-13, code + schema + tests + prod verification)

Closes Stage 40 (`docs/micro_checklist.md`) completely: the three items left open since the 2026-08-10 batch (real-browser verification, `customer_phone` live-verification, and a QZ silent-print builder for a Purchase Order) were all buildable now, not blocked as first thought.

**40.10 (build).** `engines.BuildPurchaseOrderQZPayload` gives `handlers_qz_print.go`'s `job_type` switch a `"Purchase Order"` case, reusing `BuildPurchaseOrderPrint` (Stage 40.1.5) unchanged for pricing/party resolution so the silent copy and the existing browser fallback sheet can never disagree on a total — only the HTML/ESC-POS rendering is new. `printPurchaseOrder` in `app.js` now tries QZ first, quietly, exactly like `printSalesInvoice` already does. One additive migration appends `'Purchase Order'` to the `Printer.print_role` Select list. Live-verified over real HTTP against a real PO with real interstate GST data (both render branches, printer_code override, the error paths) on a scratch server; fixture removed after.

**40.8 (live-verify) — and a real bug found doing it.** A Playwright pass against a real Chromium instance (not the HTTP-only/unit-test verification this had before) confirmed the PO composer's live server-priced preview and the Stage 40.5 motion system both work correctly, including `prefers-reduced-motion`. In the course of testing the fallback path with a real (rather than CSP-blocked) connection attempt, found that **`internal/server/middleware.go`'s CSP has had `connect-src 'self'` with no exception for QZ Tray's WebSocket endpoints since Stage 31.1 shipped** — every `qzTryPrint` call anywhere in the app (receipts, invoices, shipping labels, and now POs) has been silently blocked from ever reaching QZ Tray in any CSP-enforcing browser, always falling straight to the browser fallback with no visible error. This had never surfaced because every prior live-browser QZ pass (Stage 45, and this one until the fix) deliberately tested only the "QZ Tray absent" fallback path, never a real connection attempt. Fixed by scoping `connect-src` to add exactly the two loopback hosts QZ needs (`ws://localhost:*`, `wss://localhost.qz.io:*`), both of which resolve to `127.0.0.1` per `qz-print.js`'s own documented reasoning for using `localhost.qz.io` — not a new attack surface. Stage 31.1.8's physical-hardware verification is still genuinely blocked (no printer/QZ install on this machine), but this removes a defect that would have silently defeated it even once that hardware is available.

**40.9 (live-verify).** The dev DB's "no stock for the test SKU" blocker no longer applies (a PO since restocked it). `POST /api/v1/orders` with a channel-shaped phone number stored it cleaned to national format plus the derived `customer_country`, exactly as `engines/orders.go`'s Stage 41 phone-cleaning path is written to do; the order landed on its intended non-blocking Hold over an unrelated free-text address, not over the phone number. Confirmed there was no `ReasonCode` of category `Cancellation` anywhere in this dev DB (needed to properly cancel-and-release the test order rather than leave a dangling reservation behind a soft-deleted document) — added one, used it, removed it again.

**Verified.** `go build`/`go vet`/`node --check` clean repo-wide; 4 new Go tests (`engines/qz_po_print_test.go`) plus the existing PO/QZ/route-capability suites all green. `docs/guides/QZ_PRINTING_SETUP.md` updated for the new `Purchase Order` print role.

**Stage 44.7 also closed this session — and needed no deploy.** Asked to deploy Stage 44 to production, checked the box first rather than assuming the checklist's "not deployed" note was current: the running binary (`0e79425`, 2026-09-04) already has Stage 44's commits as an ancestor, `-migrate-status` showed nothing pending, `TENANT_BASE_DOMAIN=wholeops.in` was already set, `/etc/caddy/Caddyfile` was already the full on-demand-TLS config, and both real tenants already carry a `host_slug`. Some earlier pass — most likely 2026-09-04's "push all and deploy" (§ above referenced in `ai_handover.md`) — had already finished every prerequisite without anyone updating the tracker. Live-verified from the dev machine instead of redeploying anything: `https://minn.wholeops.in` completes its own separately-issued on-demand TLS certificate and returns 200 (Caddy's access log confirms the proxied Host header); a made-up hostname under the same wildcard DNS record fails the TLS handshake itself (`SEC_E_ILLEGAL_MESSAGE`) rather than reaching the app, confirming the `ask` gate refuses issuance for unrecognised names before any certificate is requested — a stronger guarantee than the app-level 404 fallback. Did not attempt a real login as the `minn` tenant (no credentials for a real customer, none guessed at). **Production therefore remains on `0e79425`; the Stage 40.8/40.9/40.10 + CSP fix above are not live until a separate, explicitly-approved deploy ships them.**

**A deploy of that work was then attempted (user-approved) and rolled back after a ~90s partial outage — root cause pre-existing on `main`, not this session's code.** Built from an isolated worktree (HEAD + only this session's files, deliberately excluding the concurrent uncommitted 47.7.6 work). `deploy.ps1` cross-compiled, shipped, and applied 12 pending migrations cleanly (Stage 47.1 through 49.7.5 — additive, left applied, genuinely useful progress), then the new binary refused to boot: `engines/security_baseline.go`'s Stage 49.1.3 production fail-fast gate found two long-standing, previously-unenforced findings on this box — **SB-012** (`TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS` empty despite `TRUST_PROXY=1`) and **SB-021** (`cashier1`/`system` still carry the shipped bootstrap password). systemd crash-looped for ~90 seconds before the binary (then `public/`+`db/`, to keep frontend/backend in lockstep — the first pass restored only the binary and briefly mismatched them) was rolled back to the pre-deploy `0e79425` snapshot, confirmed `active (running)` both locally and via both public hostnames. Net effect: production is byte-identical to before the attempt except its database schema, which stayed forward-migrated (safe). **This blocks every future deploy of `main` until fixed** — SB-012 is a mechanical env-var fix; SB-021 needs a product decision for `cashier1` specifically (a real POS login, not something to rotate blind) — tracked as a new blocker section in `micro_checklist.md`.

## 146. Stage 47.7.6 — the audit archive, and the incident it caused while being built (2026-09-12, code + schema + tests)

Closes the one piece §142 left open: 47.7 (A-07/A-30) now reads **fully done**. `engines/audit_archive.go` adds the archive itself on top of §142's signed-events-plus-checkpoints model — AES-256-GCM-encrypted, gzip-compressed archive files (same key-management pattern as `channel_credentials.go`), one archive per checkpoint window (never partial — a single held row under legal hold blocks the whole window), manifest-then-delete taken literally (the file is written and self-verified by re-decrypting and re-checking its digest *before* the archive row is recorded and the source rows deleted, all inside one transaction). The subtler half: checkpoint verification is now archive-aware, redirecting to the archive file instead of the (now-empty) live table for an archived window, so a legitimate archive is never mistaken for the deletion attack checkpoints exist to catch — while still catching the archive file itself being deleted, corrupted or swapped. Five new HTTP endpoints and a daily scheduler entry round it out. Full mechanics in `micro_checklist.md`'s 47.7.6 entry.

**The incident, in one paragraph.** An early test version isolated its own rows by sealing a "boundary checkpoint" over everything already in the table, then disabled the retention policy to make its own rows archive-eligible immediately — which made the boundary checkpoint eligible too, and `RunAuditArchive` (correctly tenant-wide, by design) archived the *entire pre-existing history* into a `t.TempDir()` that Go deletes at test exit. `tenant_default.audit_logs` dropped from roughly 389,000 rows to 334, found and reported by a peer session mid-review, confirmed independently by two more. No usable recovery existed — the nearest dev-DB backup was two months stale, and restoring it would have rolled back every other concurrent session's dev data, which is worse than the loss itself — so the row count is accepted as an unrecoverable dev-only loss.

**What actually closed it, all peer-reviewed in real time across five concurrent sessions**: tests now provision an isolated, single-use scratch tenant schema and never touch `tenant_default`; `RunAuditArchive` refuses `archive_after_days` below a 30-day floor as a coded error, so the same misconfiguration can't reproduce this in production; a 50,000-row ceiling per window (`maxRowsPerArchiveWindow`) bounds what the *scheduled* pass will process unattended, overridable only by an explicit flag on the manual trigger; and the archive directory is validated (absolute, existing, writable) before any row is touched, rather than discovered mid-pass. Peer review then found a sharper, related gap in the restore guard itself — a checkpoint's `archive_id` isn't covered by its own signature, so a DB-write attacker could delete an archive's *already-restored* rows again and repoint the checkpoint back at that same, still-validly-signed archive — closed by having restore **delete** the archive manifest row (inside the same transaction as restoring the rows, with the restore itself logged as a signed audit event) rather than flag it, so the repoint is refused by a foreign key the database itself enforces, not by an app-level check a second `UPDATE` could bypass.

13 tests in `engines/audit_archive_test.go`, all green in isolation; `go build`/`vet` clean repo-wide. Migration and the three new Go files are deliberately kept **uncommitted**, separate from `7f5fadf`'s Stage 47.5/47.7-core/48/49 commit — a concurrent session's decision, made independently, to exclude this item from that round while the incident was still being worked. 21 orphaned `audit_archives` rows (dead references to the deleted temp files, `tenant_default` only) are left for a deliberate operator cleanup rather than an agent-run `DELETE` against the shared dev database — the permission classifier itself refused that action mid-session, appropriately.

## 145. Stage 49.9 — Secure development lifecycle, dependency and release-artifact supply chain (2026-09-11, CI tooling + tests + docs)

Built the CI-only assurance layer 49.9 asks for, with zero mandatory runtime
dependency: a hand-maintained, test-cross-checked dependency ledger
(`docs/security/dependency-ledger.json`, package `internal/supplychain`)
covering direct/transitive Go modules, CI actions, CI tools and OS/runner
images with owner/purpose/license/provenance/version/checksum; a release
manifest generator and verifier (`cmd/releasemanifest`) producing an SBOM,
artifact checksums and a provenance record, with a `-verify` mode a
deployment step can run to refuse a tampered binary; every third-party
GitHub Action in every workflow pinned to a full commit SHA and enforced by
a generic test (`TestNoWorkflowActionIsUnpinned`) rather than a one-time
fix; a new `release-artifact` CI job building hermetically from a clean,
already-tested checkout; and `.github/CODEOWNERS` plus an expanded PR
template giving review ownership and the 49.9.1 change-trigger a reviewable
shape. Signing-key provisioning, DAST-against-a-release-candidate, and
wiring `deploy.ps1` to consume the new manifest are named explicitly as
`[needs decision]`/follow-on work rather than left implicit. Full accounting
in `docs/security/secure-development-lifecycle.md`; new risk R-13
(no enforced branch protection) recorded in `docs/security/risk_register.md`.

Built in an isolated worktree in parallel with 49.6 and 49.7 below (all
three branched from the same post-47.5/47.7/48 commit), then merged
forward through each other's landings in turn: resolved a risk-register
ID collision against 49.6's own new entry (its R-10 duplicated an
already-open R-10 from the base commit; renumbered 49.6's to R-14) and a
pre-existing Stage-48 generated-content drift (`dictionary.{json,md}`,
`guides-manifest.json`) surfaced by re-running the content-check gate
after merging. Full per-item mechanics in `micro_checklist.md`'s 49.9
entry — this section is the index pointer, not a duplicate.

## 144. Stage 49.7 — Database, host, network and deployment hardening (2026-09-11, code + schema + tests + docs)

Built the least-privilege split `engines/tenant_lifecycle.go`'s own
`db-privilege` reporter had only been able to name, not fix:
`deploy/postgres_harden.sql` splits the shared `erp` role into
`erp_app`(DML)/`erp_migrate`(schema owner)/`erp_backup`(SELECT), verified
end-to-end against an isolated scratch database and fully cleaned up
afterward. Added a SHA-256 migration-ledger checksum system (`db/migrate.go`,
SB-024) and a static scanner proving every migration in the tree is
additive-only. Hardened `deploy/erp.service` (capability/namespace/kernel
isolation, explicit `ReadWritePaths` instead of all of `/opt`).

Found and fixed **four** real sandbox-tenant side-effect leaks — not the one
first found, but three more surfaced while reconciling this stage's rebase
against Stage 49.2's and 49.6's parallel work on the same files:
`sendPasswordResetEmail`, `SendRecoveryEmailChangedNotice` and
`SendPasswordChangedNotice` (all `engines/password_reset.go`) never checked
`IsSandboxTenant`, and `DispatchNotification` (`engines/notifications.go`)
had neither that check nor the `ExternalSideEffectsEnabled()` kill switch
every other outbound call site is supposed to honor. All four now match
`engines/webhook.go`'s existing pattern, each proven with a real TCP/HTTP
canary test, not a mock. Also found and fixed an `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES`
gap that would have broken every tenant provisioned *after* hardening ran —
default privileges can't cover a schema that didn't exist yet; `saas.go`'s
`ProvisionTenantSchema` now grants it explicitly inside its existing
transaction (a no-op on an unhardened database).

Found and **documented, not fixed**: two Super-Admin HTTP routes
(`POST /api/v1/admin/tenant/provision`, `POST /api/v1/admin/sandbox-tenants`)
call `ProvisionTenantSchema` — a `CREATE SCHEMA` — through the server's own
DB connection, contradicting `docs/security/README.md`'s prior claim that
provisioning "has no HTTP surface." This blocks moving the server's runtime
identity to the new least-privilege `erp_app` role until either those routes
are refactored onto a separate elevated connection or retired in favor of
`tenantctl`-only provisioning — a real scope decision, not infrastructure
config. Recorded as risk R-07. 49.7.2/49.7.3/49.7.8 shipped as reviewable
artifacts/runbooks only (`deploy/erp.service`, `deploy/README.md` Parts
A2.5/G) — their acceptance needs a real host. Full per-item mechanics in
`micro_checklist.md`'s 49.7 entry — this section is the index pointer, not
a duplicate.

## 142. Stage 47.5 + 47.7 — the two decision-blocked audit findings, closed (2026-09-09, code + schema + tests)

Both items had sat open on a *decision* rather than on effort, and both decisions were taken by the user this session from measured evidence rather than in the abstract.

**47.5 (A-05, 3PL isolation).** The choice the item posed was "prove mixed-owner isolation at every transition" or "make the unsupported configuration impossible". The data settled it: the entire development database held **two** `bin_stock_owner` rows and both were the A-05 red-team fixture itself. Nobody was running mixed-owner 3PL, so building owner-awareness through receive → allocate → pick → pack → ship → adjust → count → bill would have been a multi-session feature for zero users, while the *claim* of isolation stood unproven the whole time. The claim was the risk, not the missing feature.

So: one owner per warehouse, enforced structurally — a `warehouse_owner` table whose PRIMARY KEY *is* `location_code`, checked at `RecordOwnerStock`, the one API in the codebase that can put a second owner in a building. An unassigned warehouse adopts its first owner, so a tenant that never thought about ownership is unaffected; it is the *second* owner that becomes impossible. The migration deliberately refuses to "fix" pre-existing mixed data — aborting would block every later migration on such a database, and silently picking a winner would re-attribute somebody's stock — so it seeds only unambiguous locations and warns about the rest. Verified on the dev database, where it correctly found the one mixed location, refused to guess, and said so. The ERP_BLUEPRINT's "multi-owner (3PL) stock segregation" claim is corrected to what the build actually supports.

**47.7 (A-07/A-30, audit evidence).** The choice was a serialized tenant chain or independently signed events plus checkpoints. What existed was the worst of both: each row's checksum hashed from the previous row's — chain semantics — with no lock, and the code said so explicitly, concluding that sibling rows were harmless. They were not: siblings are precisely what made the verifier report a break on clean data, and a verifier that cries wolf under normal load is worse than none, because it teaches an operator to ignore it. Taking the lock instead would have serialized every audit write per tenant, on a table that takes a row on nearly every request — immediately after Stage 47.3 had made checkout deliberately concurrent.

So rows are now signed over their own content with no ordering dependency, and periodic checkpoints cover the one thing per-row signatures cannot: deletion. The 90.1% of rows carrying no checksum are **sealed, not backfilled** — a signature computed today proves only that a row exists today, and asserting more would convert a known gap into a false assurance an auditor would rely on; the verifier states that limitation in words every time it runs. Verification and checkpointing ride Stage 38.6's durable runner rather than a new ticker, and the alerting covers verification that did not *happen*, not only verification that *failed*.

**A real bug surfaced from the new tests**: Postgres `TIMESTAMP` stores microseconds while `time.Now()` carries nanoseconds, so the value signed differed from the value stored and every freshly written row failed its own signature — caught because the tamper test reported a row as tampered before anything had touched it.

**A-05's red-team test now passes and was promoted out of the build tag**, joining A-02/A-03/A-04: all four originally-red findings with buildable closures are now green on the ordinary test path. 47.7 stays open on 47.7.6's archive/restore drill alone. Full per-item mechanics are in `micro_checklist.md`'s Stage 47.5/47.7 entries.

## 141. Stage 49.2.4 — Closed: recovery-destination reauthentication, and dual-control helpdesk password reset (2026-09-09, code + schema + tests + docs)

49.2.4 (recovery cannot bypass authentication) is now fully closed — the two clauses 49.2.2's session left open. Full detail is in `docs/micro_checklist.md`'s own 49.2.4 entry; this is the index.

**Reauthentication for destination change.** `handleUpdateProfile` (`PUT /api/v1/me`) changed the account's registered email with no reauthentication at all — a real gap: a hijacked session (a stolen bearer token, an unattended workstation) could silently redirect "Forgot Password" to an attacker-controlled address, with nothing else in the flow to stop it. It now requires the current password, the same check `handleChangePassword` already runs, whenever the email is actually changing; a same-value resubmission (a form that always sends every field) is exempt. `engines.SendRecoveryEmailChangedNotice` (new, sibling to `SendPasswordChangedNotice`) alerts the OLD address only — a risk notice, not an email-verification flow, which stays out of scope. MFA-change reauthentication turned out to already be true: the one self-service MFA mutation that exists (`/me/mfa/reenroll`) has required the account password since Stage 32.5, and there is still no self-service "disable MFA" path at all for it to matter for.

**Auditable dual-control helpdesk recovery for privileged users** was the real missing piece: there was no admin-driven "reset another user's password" endpoint at all before this — only self-service change/reset and initial creation. It now exists (`POST /api/v1/admin/users/reset-password`, Users screen → **Reset Password**), built on the same generic maker-checker engine (`approval_rules` + `SubmitForApproval`/`DecideApproval`) every other high-risk doctype in this codebase already rides, via a new `PasswordResetRequest` doctype seeded exactly like `HoldReleaseRequest`/`LoyaltyRedemptionRequest` before it. An ordinary target is reset in one call — a 20-character one-time password (same alphabet as MFA recovery codes) is shown once, `credential_version` bumps (revoking every open session on the account), and the existing risk-notification email fires — the same single-admin shape `handleAdminResetUserMFA` already established for MFA. **A Super Admin target cannot be reset by one admin acting alone**: the request instead requires a second, different Super Admin's approval (maker-checker already refuses the same actor as both requester and approver), and — because this is the one approval hook in the codebase whose result must reach the approver and never the requester — `handleDecideApproval` gained its own doctype branch to surface the generated password only in the approving admin's own decide response, rather than one of `engines.DecideApproval`'s ordinary result-less hooks. A reason is mandatory once the target is privileged.

**A real, closely related gap was found and deliberately not fixed in the same pass**: `handleAdminResetUserMFA` has the identical single-admin-clears-a-Super-Admin's-second-factor shape, and this session did not retrofit dual control onto it — its existing test seeds both accounts with the same privileged role by construction, so the fix is a genuine behavior change earning its own reviewed session, not a drop-in alongside this one. Recorded as risk register **R-12** (Medium) with the exact treatment named, rather than silently left for someone to rediscover.

Verified live, not just by unit test: after fixing a real omission the tests caught immediately (the new route was missing from `route_capabilities.go`'s admin-capability map, so every call 403'd until it was added — the same gate `/admin/users/reset-mfa` and its siblings already require), the full flow was replayed against a scratch server on port 8099 over real HTTP through `routes.go`'s actual dispatch table: login-minted admin token → reset a throwaway Cashier's password → confirm the old hash stopped verifying, the new one-time password verified, `credential_version` incremented, and an `Approved` evidence row was written. `go build`/`go vet` clean; full `go test ./internal/server/... ./engines/...` green; four new tests in `internal/server/stage49_2_4_admin_password_reset_test.go` cover the immediate path, the dual-control path (blank-reason refusal, self-approval refusal, a different approver succeeding), the self-target refusal, and the profile reauthentication gate. `docs/security/attack_surface.json` regenerated. Migration applied to the local dev DB for the test run — **production has not had it applied**. Not committed — review `git status` before staging; this tree holds the concurrent Stage 48 documentation-governance session's files as well (untouched by this session).

---

## 140. Stage 49.2.2 — Password baseline, and the session revocation it unlocks for 49.2.4 (2026-09-08, code + schema + tests + docs)

Stage 49 moved into Phase S1 (identity/authorization/data integrity) with 49.2 (identity, authentication, recovery, machine identity) — its first buildable sub-item, 49.2.2, is closed; the rest of 49.2 stays open. `engines/password_policy.go` + an embedded, curated `common_passwords.txt` (~300 entries, including the `Word@123`-shaped patterns this India-first ERP's own corporate password culture produces) is the one choke point `handleChangePassword`, `engines.CompletePasswordReset` and `handleCreateUser` all call now: minimum length (new `security.password_min_length` setting, default 12, following Stage 30.7's registry pattern), a denylist match, username-equality, and a cheap ascending/descending/repeated-character sequence check nothing else would catch.

A real pre-existing vulnerability surfaced while closing this out: `handleLogin`'s bcrypt check carried a plaintext fallback (`u.PasswordHash != req.Password`, described as support for "local seed configs") that every actual seeded account had no need of — every hash in `db/migration.sql` is already bcrypt — but whose failure mode ran backwards for a security control. If `password_hash` were ever a plaintext value (a bad migration, a manual SQL fix, test debris), that comparison would authenticate anyone who typed the literal stored string, via a non-constant-time `!=`. Removed; bcrypt is now the only accepted credential form. Adaptive-hashing cost migration was added alongside it — `handleLogin` transparently re-hashes at `bcrypt.DefaultCost` on a successful login if the stored hash's cost has fallen behind (best-effort, and deliberately does not touch `credential_version` since re-hashing the same password is not a credential change).

That last field is the bigger structural piece, and it also closes half of 49.2.4 (recovery cannot bypass authentication) at the same time: `db/migrations_stage49_2_2_password_hardening.sql` adds `users.credential_version`; `SignToken` now embeds it as a `cv` claim; and `apiMiddleware`'s existing Stage 29.8 live-state re-check (the same code path that already re-reads role/status on every request) rejects a full session token — never an MFA/extension purpose token — whose `cv` no longer matches the row. `engines.CompletePasswordReset` and `handleChangePassword` both bump the counter and call `engines.InvalidateLiveUserState` immediately (the same mechanism 49.1.5's tenant-suspend path already uses) rather than waiting out the ~30s cache window. A password change/reset now revokes every *other* session on the account the moment it happens — something 24.28's original reset flow never did — while `handleChangePassword` reissues a fresh token in its own response so the caller's own session survives its own change. Both paths also send a new "your password was changed" risk notification (`engines.SendPasswordChangedNotice`, sibling to the existing reset-link email, same SMTP/`ExternalSideEffectsEnabled` gating). **Deliberate one-time side effect, not a bug**: a token minted before this ships carries no `cv` claim and is rejected the same way a mismatched one is, so every session active at deploy time is asked to sign in again once.

Not built in 49.2.4: reauthentication for a destination/MFA change, and auditable dual-control helpdesk recovery for privileged users — there is no admin-driven "reset another user's password" endpoint at all yet (today's paths are self-service change/reset and initial creation only), so that clause has nothing to gate. 49.2.1 (identity lifecycle), 49.2.3 (MFA assurance tiers/WebAuthn), 49.2.5 (login-abuse defense beyond the existing account/IP lockout), 49.2.6/49.2.7 (machine identity, OAuth2 convergence) and 49.2.8 (the full authentication test matrix) remain open for a future session.

Stale client-facing claims fixed alongside the code: `ADMIN_GUIDE.md` and `USER_SOP.md` both described an 8-character minimum; `public/app.js`'s three password `minlength` attributes were still `8` as well (the server would have silently bounced anything the old hint let through). All four now read 12. New tests: `engines/password_policy_test.go` (the baseline function itself), `engines/password_reset_test.go` (24.28's reset flow had no dedicated test before this at all — end-to-end mint/validate/replay, plus the credential_version bump and policy enforcement this stage adds), and `internal/server/stage49_2_2_session_revocation_test.go` (HTTP-level: the pre-change token 401s, the change's own returned token keeps working, a rejected weak password mutates nothing). `go build`/`go vet` clean; `go test ./engines/... ./internal/server/...` green. `docs/security/attack_surface.json` regenerated for the new migration (153rd). Migration applied to the local dev DB by hand for the test run — **production has NOT had it applied**; deploying it will force every session active at that moment to sign in again, which is expected. Not committed — review `git status` before staging; this tree holds the concurrent Stage 48 documentation-governance session's files as well (untouched by this session).

## 139. Stage 48 — Documentation governance and canonical manuals (2026-09-10, implementation and review package)

The documentation portal now leads to owned product, process, architecture, data/API, security/legal, engineering, operations, implementation and QA drafts. User and tenant-administrator manuals are generated from canonical Knowledge Center topics. All 56 registered screens have help mappings across 49 topics; current returns, RF, warehouse-depth, asset and expense instructions replace the previously missing or stale guidance.

The safe generation pipeline stages all outputs, validates manifests and concurrent source identity, and compares without repository writes. Ten registry projections include the explicitly scoped data dictionary (199 doctypes, 1,542 fields) and canonical OpenAPI with a retained identical legacy JSON projection. Forward/reverse traceability covers 14 capability domains and 42 functional requirements, business needs, personas/processes and security/data/operations controls. Strict offline CI now enforces repaired metadata, ownership, links, help and evidence structure. Public external-link checks run separately with bounded requests and a seven-day cache.

The repaired screenshot tool waits for editor visibility/animation, preserves server rate limits and rejects partial/error/unauthorized capture sets. All 14 live screens pass on an isolated development database with external delivery disabled; manifest status remains pending human review. Keyboard and narrow-screen manual checks found a long inline reference overflow, repaired in the shared manual stylesheet. Actionable `GOODSR-0096`/`GOODSR-0097` companion catalog entries close the actual engine-to-help gap without hand-editing generated catalog source.

Validation and remaining findings are recorded in [the dated verification record](assurance/stage48-verification-2026-09-10.md). Application build, vet and the full serialized Go suite passed; focused generator/linter/KB/docgen tests, screenshot harness regressions and 13 real-command check-purity cases passed. Final source/projection and migration-preview checks are recorded there as they finish. The first external run found 43/48 reachable links; five historical citations need an owner addendum. No historical finding was overwritten to conceal a failure.

The handover is now below 150 lines; prior content is preserved in an archive and its exact predecessor in the recovery checkout. A detached baseline preserves 889 hash-verified files from `fed51b4` plus concurrent work. A second isolated checkout prepares 24 explicit moves across five batches, with retained published-path headings and a [concrete review table](governance/migration-review.md). Neither is an approved baseline/batch commit. No main-workspace folder cutover, evidence deletion, deployment or commit occurred. This verification did create/migrate an isolated local fixture database; the dictionary capture of the development registry was read-only.

Stage 48 remains open for qualified product/process/security/legal approval, legacy unique-content parity, representative user/screen-reader/physical-device walkthroughs and the approved baseline/per-batch migration gates. Drafts and local automated passes do not authorize Production/Certified capability claims. The later Stage 47.5 scope decision now governs the drafts: one stock owner per warehouse, with mixed-owner mode explicitly unsupported. Physical-device and 47.7.6 archive/restore acceptance remain open. Concurrent Stage 49.2.2 and 49.2.4 work is preserved; see §140–141 and the live checklist for its separate verification and deployment constraints.

Continuation on September 10 reconciled the later Stage 47.5 single-owner decision and Stage 47.7 signed-event/checkpoint implementation across canonical drafts. Current build/vet and focused documentation tests pass. The broader suite is not green: an isolated fresh database applied all 157 migrations and exposed the reproducible `Store` module/role-registry mismatch; shared-fixture/time and server-order behavior remain QA follow-up (DOC48-07). The earlier full-suite pass is retained as earlier evidence, not repeated as current acceptance.
