## 138. Stage 49.1.7 — Outside-in verification, and a real static-server method gap it found (2026-09-08, code + tool + tests + docs)

The last open sub-item of 49.1 needed something none of the rest of Stage 49 S0 could provide from inside this tree: a genuinely external vantage point. `cmd/edgecheck` is a new, reusable CLI — not a one-off script — that probes a deployed instance the way an anonymous internet client would: declared public routes answer as declared, `/internal/*` never reaches the edge, alternate HTTP methods and encoded/traversal paths fail safely, the app's own bind port and a handful of common service ports are unreachable directly, and (when `TENANT_BASE_DOMAIN` is live) an unknown tenant subdomain is refused rather than silently falling through. Run from the developer's own machine over the ordinary internet — not the SSH tunnel, not `127.0.0.1`, the exact distinction the 2026-08-14 TLS bring-up already learned the hard way when a firewall problem was invisible from inside the box.

It found a real gap on the first run. `routes.go` registered the static asset tree at the bare `http.Handle("/", fs)`, a pattern with no method verb in Go's `net/http.ServeMux` syntax — which therefore matches every method — and `http.FileServer` only special-cases `HEAD` internally, never otherwise checking `r.Method`. `TRACE /app.js`, `PUT /styles.css` and `TRACE /` all returned `200` with the ordinary file body, confirmed against production itself before anything was changed. Not an XST/reflection finding (nothing echoes back), but a real violation of "only declared methods respond" and exactly the kind of gap an external scanner flags on sight. Every other route in the codebase already refuses a wrong method, either through an explicit method in its `ServeMux` pattern or through `apiMiddleware`; the static file server had neither, because a file server has no natural place to declare one.

Fixed at the one place it needed to be: `onlyReadMethods` in `internal/server/static_fileserver.go` wraps the file server and refuses anything but GET/HEAD with a `405` and an `Allow: GET, HEAD` header before `http.FileServer` ever runs, and `routes.go`'s single registration site now wraps it. Verified on a local scratch instance (`TRACE`/`PUT`/`DELETE`/`PATCH`/`POST` all `405`, `GET`/`HEAD` still `200`) and locked by a new regression test. **Not yet deployed** — recorded as risk-register row R-10, open rather than closed, because the checklist item (the verification ran, and it found and fixed the gap) is a different thing from the production exposure (still live until the next deploy). Full evidence, including the complete 27-PASS/1-SKIP/1-FAIL run against `https://app.wholeops.in`, is `docs/security/outside_in_verification_2026-09-08.md`. With 49.1.7 closed, Stage 49.1 (attack-surface inventory and fail-closed baseline) is now fully closed; Stage 49.0 stays open on 49.0.6 alone, which needs an appointed acceptance authority — a decision, not a build step.

## 137. Stage 49.1.5 — Secure tenant provisioning, offboarding and proof of removal (2026-09-06, code + schema + tests + docs)

The tenant lifecycle had exactly two states: it existed, or somebody ran `DROP SCHEMA CASCADE` by hand. Everything in between — a credential that stops working, a tenant that stops being served without being destroyed, a deletion that has to wait for a backup and a retention period, a way to show afterwards that nothing of it remains — did not exist. Provisioning itself was better than its reputation (it already minted a cryptographically random one-time password and refused to clone `tenant_default`'s users), but nothing governed what happened to that password after it was handed over.

Four real defects in the existing path, found by reading it rather than by adding to it. The registry `INSERT` and `CREATE SCHEMA` ran as two autocommitted statements *ahead* of the clone/seed transaction, so a failure in the middle left a registry row resolving to a half-built schema — a tenant that authenticates and then 500s on every request, with no state anywhere saying it was never finished; all of it is now one transaction, and a test forces that failure and asserts the row is gone. The one-time password never expired and nothing recorded whether it had ever been rotated. Re-running provisioning over an existing tenant silently reset a rotated, in-use admin password through step 5's `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` — the same hazard as R-01, in Go instead of SQL. And there was no reversible state between serving and destruction, so the only answer to non-payment or a suspected compromise was to do nothing or to delete the tenant.

The credential is now a credential with a lifetime: 72 hours by default, refused at login afterwards with the same generic failure as a wrong password, reissued only by an operator and only with a recorded reason. Rotation is detected without a flag anyone has to remember to set — bcrypt salts every hash, so the account's stored hash still being byte for byte the one issued is itself the proof it was never changed, and a rotation done in raw SQL is caught exactly like one done through the app. MFA was already mandatory for the provisioned admin's role; a test now pins that rather than the design assuming it.

Suspension and deprovisioning cut off **sessions already in flight**, not just new logins, through a tenant gate placed in `ResolveLiveUserState` — the same choke point Stage 29.8 built for a single user's deactivation, which every authenticated request already passes through, so a suspension is felt within the same ~30s SLO with no new middleware and no per-route change. A purge refuses four ways (never deprovisioned, legal hold, retention window, no backup reference), records each refusal, and then proves the removal: no registry row, no schema, no hostname mapping, no row in any `public.*` table carrying a `tenant_id` — discovered from `information_schema` rather than hardcoded, so a table some later stage adds is covered without anyone remembering this file — and no cached session in the running process. The evidence trail is deliberately not foreign-keyed to the tenant registry, because after a purge it is the only remaining record that the tenant ever existed.

The operator surface is `cmd/tenantctl`, a CLI and not an admin route. Platform-level authority to create and destroy tenants is not something any role inside the product should be able to reach, and an offline tool cannot be reached from the internet at all; it costs the 49.1.1 inventory one CLI command instead of an authenticated path in front of `DROP SCHEMA`. It follows `cmd/reset_mfa`'s conventions — dry run by default, `-yes` to act, a required reason recorded permanently. Least-privilege database ownership is **reported, not fixed**: `tenantctl db-privilege` raises a finding when the app connects as a superuser, because creating and granting roles is deployment state and belongs to 49.7.4 — naming the gap is what keeps it from being assumed away. Full per-item mechanics are in `micro_checklist.md`'s 49.1.5 entry; the operator procedure is `docs/guides/ADMIN_GUIDE.md` §C.6.

## 136. Stage 47.4 + 47.6 — Atomic replay-safe returns, and the RF task shell (2026-09-06, code + schema + tests)

**47.4 (A-04).** Stage 35.9 had already built the right shape — one `ReturnRequest` aggregate with a real state machine, prices resolved from the original sale — and its own entry recorded that it had no management UI. What it lacked was the four properties that make a returns model safe, and what it sat beside was a legacy instant-return path that had none of them. That path took price and cost from the caller, incremented stock with an unlocked upsert, committed, and only then posted two GL reversals with no idempotency key; its "already returned" pool came from a single `SalesReturn` document whose repeat INSERT conflicted and whose error the HTTP handler discarded, so the recorded total never advanced past the first call while stock and GL for every later call still went through. Four replayed calls of 3 against a sale of 10 returned 12.

It is now retired twice over — the route is a 410 that names its replacement, and `ProcessReturnAnywhere` itself is a coded refusal, because it was an exported engine function as well as a route. In its place: cumulative eligibility checked inside a transaction holding the original sale's own row locked (every return against one sale contends on exactly one row; returns against different sales never contend), a tenant-scoped idempotency key through the same `command_idempotency` mechanism 47.3 built for checkout, both QC GL legs moved inside the QC transaction with real posting keys, refund processing collapsed into one transaction with a row-locked status re-check, and — new, because nothing did it before — **output-GST reversal on returned goods**, which a refund had been leaving on the books as taxable turnover.

Two workflows genuinely missing from 47.4.5 were built as named, evidenced, capability-gated exceptions rather than as relaxed validations: **No Receipt** (no bill; priced from the item master and labelled as such) and **Goodwill** (past the window — and only the window; cumulative eligibility still holds, because "we will take this back as a gesture" is not "you may return more than you bought"). And the missing UI is now two surfaces: the POS return panel rebuilt so the clerk types a bill number and the server answers with what is returnable and at what price, and a Returns screen whose per-row actions are derived from the aggregate's own state machine.

**47.6 (A-06/A-39), partially.** The named bug is small and nasty: the RF picking screen's Wave ID input is called `mobile-pick-wave-id`, the substring inference matched "mobile" against the phone-format tokens, and the phone keystroke filter stripped the letters out of a wave id as the operator typed it — silently, on a device where nobody could see the rewrite. The server-side half was worse: `IsPhoneField` feeds `NormalizeDocumentPhones`, so the same inference would have rewritten a stored wave id or lot number into E.164 on save. An explicit semantic registry now wins over the inference, is served to the browser so both sides agree, and the inference itself is deliberately kept — it covers every phone field in the schema today and every one added later, and removing it would have silently stopped normalising all of them.

On top of that, the RF task shell 47.6.2 asked for, carrying the four traceability endpoints 47.6.7 found orphaned (`batch/putaway`, `batch/consume`, `batch/expiry-sweep`, `serial/status` — all real, tested, registered, and with **zero caller anywhere in `app.js`**, so the operation that makes a lot visible to FEFO was reachable only by a developer with curl). The shell is not the desktop layout at 390px: no chrome, one instruction, one scan target that keeps focus, Enter advancing between fields because that is what a hardware scanner sends, and four outcomes distinguished by sound, vibration, colour **and** text. Offline behaviour is a deliberate decision recorded in the code — these four operations are **not** queued, because each mutates stock against server state the device cannot evaluate, which is the opposite of the POS decision (20.13) for the reason that a sale has already physically happened when it queues and a putaway has not.

**47.6.3 and 47.6.4 were then built too.** The responsive work is one layer rather than six screen patches, because all six screens are assembled from the same vocabulary - and the measurement had to be corrected before it meant anything: `documentElement.scrollWidth` reported zero overflow even BEFORE the fix, because `.app-container`/`.main-content` are `overflow: hidden`, so this application never scrolls sideways, it clips. Measured against the right instrument at 390px, all seven screens were clipping content beyond reach (120px of usable width out of 390, the sidebar taking 270; up to 553px of a table unreachable) and all seven now clip nothing. The accessibility pass found four real AA failures by computation rather than by eye - a 2.86:1 warning token, a 3.90:1 on-primary in both dark blocks, six labels with no `for=`, and two controls with no accessible name - and is guarded going forward by a test that parses the stylesheet and the shell, the same self-checking shape three other subsystems here already use, because a one-time accessibility sweep lasts until the next screen. **47.6 stays open on 47.6.6 alone**, which needs physical hardware. **The A-04 red-team test now passes and was promoted out of the build tag**, joining A-02 and A-03. A-05 and A-07 stay red and tagged, correctly — 47.5 and 47.7 are blocked on the decisions their checklist entries name. Full per-item mechanics and the two pre-existing tests that needed real adjustment are in `micro_checklist.md`'s Stage 47.4/47.6 entries.

## 135. Stage 49 Phase S0 — Security charter, attack-surface inventory and fail-closed production baseline (2026-09-06, code + docs + tests)

The first phase of Stage 49's security program: establish what is being protected and from whom (49.0), then make the surface knowable and the production baseline fail closed (49.1). Built in parallel with the Stage 47 session and deliberately scoped to files that session was not holding — two narrow edits to `internal/server/routes.go`, everything else new.

**49.0 — the charter.** `docs/security/threat_model.md` and `docs/security/risk_register.md`, written from the code and the deployment files rather than a template: four supported configurations (single-box hosted, per-tenant hostnames, dev, sandbox) with the unsupported ones named; eight security objectives each written so it can fail a test, with today's honest status; ten crown jewels ranked by what an attacker gains, each with the protection that actually exists in this tree; the trust-boundary chain B1–B8 from Caddy to egress; eleven adversary personas and the three collusion pairs; and fourteen misuse cases of which eight are the 2026-09-01 deep persona audit's proven findings restated in the same register, so proven and modelled abuse live in one place. Review triggers are split into the six that are now a failing test and the six no test can detect. The register carries eight open risks in a full schema (exposure, detectability, treatment, owner, evidence, review expiry) plus the twelve audit findings indexed in. **What is deliberately not done:** no risk is marked Accepted, because 49.0.6 forbids an implementer accepting their own critical risk and no approver role exists — that is flagged in `micro_checklist.md` as needing a decision.

**49.1 — the surface.** `internal/securityscan` derives the inventory from source (no runtime registry, nothing added to the production binary, no infrastructure needed to regenerate from a clean checkout); `go run ./cmd/surfacescan` writes `docs/security/attack_surface.json` — 475 routes classified by the gate they pass through, 28 background jobs, 9 CLI commands, 26 environment flags of which 12 are security-critical, 150 migrations, 6 outbound call sites, 2 dependencies. That file is then the approved profile a drift test compares against, so any change to a route, an auth class, a job, a flag, an outbound call site or a dependency fails `go test ./...` until the inventory is regenerated and the diff read. `engines/security_baseline.go` is the fail-fast configuration validator: 22 checks that log at every ENV and refuse to start only on a blocking finding under `ENV=production`, with a canary test enforcing that no finding ever echoes the value it is complaining about.

**Four real findings came out of building it**, which is the point of doing the inventory rather than assuming it:

- **An unauthenticated directory listing.** `http.FileServer(http.Dir("./public"))` generated an HTML index for any directory without an `index.html`; `public/components` and `public/profiles` both qualify, so `GET /components/` returned a complete linked map of the front-end before login. Fixed in `internal/server/static_fileserver.go` — such a directory now 404s identically to one that does not exist, assets still load by exact path — and verified live.
- **Three of four shipped bootstrap credentials were unguarded.** 24.27 checked `tenant_default.admin` only; `db/migration.sql` seeds four accounts, and `system` carries the Super Admin role. SB-021 now checks all four across every tenant schema, and reported three still active on the dev database the moment it was switched on.
- **Re-running the base schema resets rotated passwords.** That INSERT ends `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET password_hash = EXCLUDED.password_hash` (risk R-01).
- **Two entries on the unauthenticated route allowlist name routes that do not exist** (risk R-05) — harmless today, a pre-authorisation for whatever is registered there next.

The no-bypass scan (49.1.4) came back genuinely clean: zero magic identity comparisons, zero impersonation or back-door paths, one governed kill switch. Its twelve block-severity hits are the four bootstrap credentials and the code that exists to refuse them, each written up in a reviewed allowlist that lives next to the scanner so it is executed rather than aspirational. Full per-item mechanics are in `micro_checklist.md`'s Stage 49 entries; `docs/security/README.md` is the operator-facing index and regeneration guide.

## 134. Stage 47.2 + 47.3 — Server-authoritative POS pricing and exactly-once atomic checkout (2026-09-05/06, code + schema + tests)

Two audit findings closed together because they are two halves of the same transaction. **47.2 (A-02/A-38)**: until this stage the only source of a POS line's sale price *and* its cost price was the browser — `public/app.js` pushed every cart line as `{salePrice: 0, costPrice: 0}` and let the cashier type both — while the discount-approval gate keyed on a third, unrelated, equally client-supplied `discount_pct`. The same money could therefore be rung up two ways: declared honestly as a discount (gated on approval) or hidden in a low unit price with `discount_pct=0` (not gated), delivering identical figures to the till, to inventory and to the GL. **47.3 (A-03)**: the existing "idempotency guard" claimed the POSCart *row*, but everything after it ran as five independently-committed transactions, unwound by hand-written compensations that could themselves fail and only log — so a crash between any two left a sale genuinely half-posted, and a retry under a *new* cart number bypassed the guard entirely.

The 47.2 answer is one engine, `engines/pos_quote.go`, that resolves every line from tenant master data (approved `PriceListVersion` — Stage 37.6.4's resolver, which had *no production caller at all* before this — then `Item.sale_price`/`mrp`, or an approved `POSPriceOverride`), computes GST and offers from those resolved prices, and returns an HMAC-digest `quote_version` that is simultaneously the rule version and the tamper-evident identity. Checkout re-resolves it and charges that, so a stale or tampered browser payload cannot influence the receipt, the GST return, the revenue posting or the approval decision. The approval gate now keys on the larger of the declared discount and the *server-measured* deviation from master price, which is what actually closes the bypass. Cost went the other way entirely: `cost_price` is removed from request, cart, response and UI, cost resolves server-side only, and a line with no cost basis posts no COGS leg and records a `POSCostingGap` rather than letting the till invent a number.

The 47.3 answer is a tenant-scoped `command_idempotency` record claimed before any mutation and completed *inside the sale's own transaction* — which is what makes taking over an expired claim provably safe rather than a gamble — plus `FinalizePOSCheckout` collapsed into one transaction covering the cart lock, availability, loyalty burn, revenue/COGS/GST/exempt reclass, override consumption and the outbox event. That required splitting six engines into `…Tx` forms; every existing caller keeps the original function, now a wrapper. A separate `Initiated → Authorized → Posted / Failed / Voided` path (`engines/pos_payment.go`) serves the payment-terminal case so no database transaction is ever held across a network round trip, and `engines/pos_sale_reconciliation.go` gives an operator the report that proves all of it per sale rather than asking them to trust the code.

Both findings' frozen red-team tests (`A-02`, `A-03`) now pass and were **promoted out of the `stage47redteam` build tag** onto the ordinary `go test ./...` path, per 47.0.1's own closure note. A-03's sanity check had to be rewritten in the process: it previously *required* availability to have dropped after a failed attempt, because that was the bug it was freezing — asserting the fixed behaviour there is strictly stronger, since it now fails if the transaction is ever broken back apart. Two pre-existing tests needed adjusting for real reasons, both recorded in `micro_checklist.md`. Full per-item mechanics, file paths, the deliberate limitation on `pos.pricing_mode` defaulting to `assisted`, and the list of what is deliberately left outside the sale transaction (and why) are in `micro_checklist.md`'s Stage 47.2/47.3 entries — this section is the index pointer, not a duplicate.

## 133. Stage 47.1 — Deny-by-default authorization, privacy and segregation of duties (2026-09-05, code + schema + tests)

All eight of Stage 47.1's sub-items built: **47.1.1/47.1.2** (route-capability registry over all 461 `apiMiddleware` routes, and the five sensitive capabilities the A-01 finding named) earlier the same day, then **47.1.3-47.1.8** — a sensitive-field policy that replaces "no field rows means every field" (`engines/sensitive_fields.go`, merged into `fieldPermissions()` so read, form-meta, write, CSV import and PIM bulk edit all inherit it with no call-site change); scope semantics separated from role capability (`engines/scope_policy.go` — location strict where the doctype declares it mandatory, permissive where optional, plus the self and 3PL-owner dimensions the old inline clause had no notion of, all fail-closed); twelve task-derived role templates (`engines/role_templates.go`, which the route-capability allowlist is now *derived from* rather than duplicating); a six-conflict SoD catalog with evidence-based detection and a one-page "why allowed / why denied" administrator preview (`engines/sod_catalog.go`); a reviewed, reversible per-tenant grant migration that changes nothing until an owner approves a before/after diff and never touches a custom role (`engines/role_template_migration.go`, `db/migrations_stage47_1_role_templates.sql`); and a generated authorization contract matrix of 7,837 route×role assertions plus HTTP-level unauthenticated/deactivated/demoted/cross-location/sensitive-field checks.

Three real, pre-existing exposures were found and closed while building this, none of them hypothetical: the shipped seed's `('Cashier','Payslip',TRUE,…)`/`('Cashier','EmployeeLoan',TRUE,…)` grants with no field restriction anywhere; `renderMyRequestsTab` fetching every employee's Leave/ExpenseClaim/Grievance and filtering by employee **in the browser**; and the audit/system log routes being `LevelAdmin`, which made the Auditor role's own capabilities unreachable. Full per-item detail, evidence and file paths are in `micro_checklist.md`'s Stage 47.1 entries — this section is the index pointer, not a duplicate. 47.1's parent stays open on one deliberately operational step: no tenant's roles have been migrated to the templates yet, because that is an owner's reviewed decision, not a migration's side effect.

## 128. Stage 37.11 — Role dashboards, savable layouts, digests, drill-through (2026-09-01, code + schema)

Pre-build audit of the existing exec dashboard (`renderExecDashboard`, `public/app.js`) found it fully hardcoded - 4 literal cards + a literal `sales-register` trend chart, no persistence, no per-role variation. Generalized rather than replaced: the four cards became `DefaultDashboardTiles()`'s data, not literal code.

- **37.11.1**: `DashboardLayout` doctype - the `OMSSavedView` precedent exactly (engine-written directly to `documents`, not the generic doc-create API). One addition beyond `OMSSavedView`'s shape: an optional `role` field - unset keeps a layout private ("savable layouts"), set makes it a shared default for that role ("role dashboards").
- **37.11.2**: Savable - `SaveDashboardLayout`/`ListDashboardLayouts`/`DeleteDashboardLayout` (`engines/dashboard.go`), delete owner-only even for a role-shared layout. Frontend: a layout picker + Save-as/Delete/Add-tile controls on the Dashboard tab, each tile closeable inline.
- **37.11.3**: `DashboardDigest` doctype + `StartDashboardDigestWorker` - the `ScheduledReport` precedent applied to a whole layout: runs every tile, assembles one combined CSV, delivers through the existing outbox. `module_key` (`reports`) confirmed already registered/`default_enabled` before writing the migration - avoided repeating 37.9's module-registration gap.
- **37.11.4**: Drill-through - pure frontend reuse, no backend change. Every tile calls the same `execDashboardOpenReport` the original 4 cards used, opening Report Catalog with that report pre-selected where `has_drill_down` rendering already exists.
- **37.11.5**: BI data-mart deferral (26.10.6) re-checked, not re-decided - a digest tick adds no new query shape, thresholds unaffected. No action.
- **Surface/schema**: additive `db/migrations_stage37_11_dashboards.sql` (dev only). New `engines/dashboard.go` (+`_test.go`), one new handler file (`internal/server/handlers_dashboard.go`), 3 new routes, 1 new background worker. `public/app.js`'s dashboard generalized into `renderExecDashboard` + `renderExecDashboardBody` + `defaultDashboardTiles()`.
- **Verified**: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...`/`node --check public/app.js` clean; `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` fully green including new `TestStage3711Dashboards` (validation, owner-only vs role-shared visibility, delete permission boundary, digest worker running both tiles and advancing the schedule). **Live over HTTP** (port 9269, stopped afterward): private vs role-shared visibility confirmed between `manager1`/`cashier1`, delete-permission boundary confirmed, a real `DashboardDigest` created via the generic doc API. **Real-browser verification via Playwright**: logged in via an injected token, added/removed a tile, confirmed drill-through opened Report Catalog with the right report pre-selected, saved/selected/deleted a layout end-to-end with no console errors. Every fixture cleaned, zero active residue confirmed.
- **Next**: Stage 38's remaining 38.4 (webhooks), 38.6 (async job runner), 38.7 (integrator sandbox) - now built, see §129-131 above.

## 131. Stage 38.7 — Self-service sandbox tenant for integrators (2026-09-01, code + schema)

Built last of the three remaining Stage 38 items (its webhook side-effect-off hook depends on 38.4). A sandbox is a normal tenant - provisioned through the exact same `ProvisionTenantSchema` every real tenant uses - flagged via two additive columns on `public.tenants` (`is_sandbox`, `sandbox_expires_at`), not a new table.

- Deliberately does **not** seed a synthetic demo dataset - a real, separate, materially larger feature. An integrator tests their own integration against the Public API v1 by creating their own records through it.
- "Self-service" describes the integrator's experience once a sandbox exists (own login, own API credentials) - provisioning itself stays Super-Admin-gated, the same as every other tenant-provisioning action.
- Auto-expiry refused at login **and** on Stage 29.8's existing live session re-check (`apiMiddleware`) - deliberately NOT wired into `db.GetTenantSchema` itself (~100+ call sites, too broad a blast radius).
- Reset truncates exactly the set `ProvisionTenantSchema`'s own comment documents as "per-tenant transactional data" - refuses on any non-sandbox tenant, leaves users/config untouched, extends expiry. Delete drops the schema + registry row with the identical guard - the only place in this codebase's non-test code that runs `DROP SCHEMA`.
- **Surface/schema**: additive `db/migrations_stage38_7_sandbox_tenants.sql`. New `engines/sandbox.go` (+`_test.go`), one new handler file, 4 new Super-Admin routes, a 2-line hook into `handleLogin` and `apiMiddleware`. No frontend screen - matching every other Stage 38 admin-facing piece.
- **Verified**: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` fully green including new `TestStage387SandboxTenants`. **Live over HTTP** (port 9270, stopped afterward): provisioned, permission-boundary-checked, logged into (correctly required MFA like any Super Admin), reset, expiry-forced-then-login-refused, reset/delete refused against the real `default` tenant, then deleted with both schema and registry row confirmed gone. Zero residue confirmed.
- **Stage 38 is now fully closed** except the explicitly-deferred 38.2d (OAuth2 client-credentials grant).

## 130. Stage 38.4 — Webhook subscriptions with HMAC signing, retry/backoff and DLQ (2026-09-01, code + schema)

Built on top of 38.6 (same session, built first). Extends `outbox.go` exactly as the backlog item names it: `WebhookSubscription` is a plain generic-doc-API doctype (the `ScheduledReport` precedent), and `dispatchWebhooksForEvent` (`engines/webhook.go`) is called from `processOutbox` once an outbox event is already committed - fanning out to one `webhook_delivery` async job per matching Active subscription. The job runner's own retry/backoff/DeadLettered handling IS this item's DLQ - no second retry mechanism built.

- HMAC-SHA256 signing (`X-Webhook-Signature`) over the real request body, secret mandatory.
- SSRF guard (stdlib `net`/`net/http` only): https-only, every resolved IP must be genuinely public, checked at both create time and immediately before every delivery attempt (DNS can change between the two), redirects never followed.
- Sandbox side-effect-off (38.7's concrete need): a sandbox tenant's delivery is simulated, zero real HTTP calls - proven live via a canary server never touched.
- New module_key `integrations`, registered proactively in the same migration (Stage 37.9's lesson).
- **Surface/schema**: additive `db/migrations_stage38_4_webhook_subscriptions.sql`. New `engines/webhook.go` (+`_test.go`), a 4-line hook into `processOutbox`, 1 new `currency.go` dispatcher case. No new handler/route file.
- **Verified**: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` fully green including new `TestStage384WebhookSubscriptions`. **Live over HTTP** (port 9270, stopped afterward, shared pass with 38.6): a real subscription against a TEST-NET-2 address (RFC 5737, no real third party contacted), a real seeded outbox event picked up by the live ticker, a real job enqueued/claimed/attempted (genuine HTTPS POST, correct timeout, correct reschedule) - proving the full live pipeline, not just unit tests. Retry/Cancel confirmed over the real routes. Zero residue confirmed.
- **Next**: 38.7 (sandbox tenant) - now built, see §131 above.

## 129. Stage 38.6 — One general async job runner with retries, DLQ and a visibility screen (2026-09-01, code + schema)

Built first of the three remaining Stage 38 items - 38.4 and 38.7 both depend on it. The foundation `micro_checklist.md`'s own §47.11.4 already named as the target every bespoke ticker in this codebase should migrate onto incrementally, later - this stage builds the runner and its visibility screen only, no existing ticker touched. A dedicated `async_jobs` table (the `integration_event_outbox` precedent, not a generic doctype).

- Real `SELECT...FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` claiming (Pending due, or Leased with an expired lease), batch 10, every 10s.
- Lease is a claim deadline (5 min), not a live heartbeat - honestly scoped to this codebase's synchronous handlers; `UpdateJobProgress` exists for a future long-running one.
- Exponential backoff to `max_attempts` (default 5) then `DeadLettered` - that status IS the DLQ. `ReplayJob`/`CancelJob` mirror `RetryIntegrationEvent`'s precedent.
- Idempotent enqueue via a partial unique index on `(job_type, idempotency_key)`.
- Hourly retention sweep (`platform.job_runner_retention_days`, default 30) - the `SweepPublicAPIRuntime` precedent.
- New "Async Jobs" 4th tab on the existing Activity Log screen, same pane pattern as the pre-existing Integration Payloads tab.
- **Real bug caught mid-build**: the retention sweeper's first draft passed a schema name where `SweepJobRunnerRetention` expected a tenantID, silently sweeping `tenant_default` instead of the real tenant for every non-default schema - fixed via the existing `tenantIDForSchema` helper, the same fix `StartPublicAPIRuntimeSweeper` already needed for the identical reason.
- **Surface/schema**: additive `db/migrations_stage38_6_async_job_runner.sql` (`async_jobs` added to `ProvisionTenantSchema`'s clone list with the `api_credentials`-style guard). New `engines/jobrunner.go` (+`_test.go`), one new handler file, 3 new routes, 2 new workers, 1 new setting.
- **Verified**: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...`/`node --check public/app.js` clean; `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` fully green including new `TestStage386JobRunner`. **Live + real-browser** (port 9270, stopped afterward, shared pass with 38.4): list/retry/cancel exercised against a real job; the new Async Jobs tab verified in a real browser via Playwright (all 4 tabs, correct headers/empty-state, and per-status action-button rendering with 4 seeded jobs), zero console errors. Zero residue confirmed.
- **Next**: 38.4 (webhooks, rides this runner) - now built, see §130 above.

## 126. Stage 37.9 — Quality & maintenance: inspection plans, CoA, NCR/CAPA, preventive maintenance (2026-09-01, code + schema)

Pre-build audit found all four completely absent. GRN receiving's own QC (Stage 26.5.2) is a pure quantity split with no structured per-item test list, so `PostGRNReceiptWithQC` is deliberately left untouched. `ReasonCode` is reused as-is for NCR root-cause (a new "Quality" category value).

- **37.9.1**: `InspectionPlan` - a pure generic-document Master: per-item test parameters + sample size.
- **37.9.2**: `CertificateOfAnalysis` - Draft→Released/Rejected, `overall_result` derived (any Fail fails the whole CoA), Release refused on Fail. Rejection reuses `TransitionBinStockCondition`/`bin_stock_batch` quarantine (Stage 42.1). **Real bug caught by this stage's own test**: the first draft moved only the parent `bin_stock` aggregate, never the `bin_stock_batch` sub-ledger, which `moveBatchStockCondition`'s own doc comment says is required together with the parent call.
- **37.9.3**: `NonConformanceReport` (NCR/CAPA) - Draft→Investigating→CorrectiveActionPlanned→Closed, root cause validated against an Active Quality `ReasonCode`, corrective action required before closing.
- **37.9.4**: `MaintenanceSchedule` + `MaintenanceOrder` - the `RecurringSalesContract` spawn-a-document shape, not the dunning-style notify-only scan, since preventive maintenance needs an actionable work item.
- **Surface/schema**: additive `db/migrations_stage37_9_quality_maintenance.sql` (dev only). New `engines/quality_maintenance.go` (+`_test.go`), `internal/server/handlers_quality_maintenance.go`, 10 routes, 1 worker.
- **A second, more consequential gap found during this stage's own live-HTTP pass, affecting Stage 37.8 too**: neither `quality` nor `service` was ever registered as a real `module_key` (`public.modules`/`module_entitlements`) - the same registration Stage 27 had to add for `wms`/`oms`. The generic document-create path checks module entitlement per request and refused `InspectionPlan`/`MaintenanceSchedule`/37.8's `ServiceContract` with `SAAS-0191` the whole time; dedicated-route doctypes (`ServiceTicket`, `CertificateOfAnalysis`, `NonConformanceReport`) were unaffected, which is why 37.8's own live check didn't surface it. Fixed in this stage's migration (registers both module keys, backfills every schema) rather than a separate follow-up - re-verified live that both `ServiceContract` and `MaintenanceSchedule` now create successfully.
- **Verified**: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...`/`node --check public/app.js` clean; `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` fully green including new `TestStage379QualityMaintenance` (CoA derivation + the quarantine bug catch, NCR's full lifecycle, maintenance scheduling + order lifecycle). **Live over HTTP** (port 9267, stopped afterward): a Fail CoA's release refused, Reject correctly quarantining both bins holding the batch in both `bin_stock` and `bin_stock_batch` (confirmed via direct SQL), the full NCR lifecycle (which surfaced the `ReasonCode.category` closed-vocabulary gap, fixed in the same migration), and - after the module-registration fix - a real Asset/MaintenanceSchedule/ServiceContract all creating successfully via the generic doc API. Pre-build audit found all four completely absent, plus a structural finding: `SalesInvoice` is lump-sum only (no `lines` field) - deferred revenue is recognised at the whole-invoice level, not per line.

- **37.6.1**: `SalesInvoice.deferred_revenue`/`deferred_term_months` - a flagged invoice's `PostSalesInvoice` posting credits 2600 Deferred Revenue instead of 4100; auto-creates an Active `DeferredRevenueSchedule` (no maker-checker of its own - the decision was already made).
- **37.6.2**: `PrepaidExpenseSchedule` - its own independent Draft→Approved lifecycle; approval posts the upfront `Dr 1800/Cr 1100` and starts recognition.
- **Shared worker** (`StartAmortizationWorker`) recognises both types monthly, last month absorbing the paise remainder. Two new roll-forward reports.
- **37.6.3**: `RecurringSalesContract` - the `CreateRecurringJournalTemplate` shape, spawning a Draft `SalesInvoice` on schedule; each spawned invoice still goes through `PostSalesInvoice`'s credit-limit gate.
- **37.6.4**: `PriceListVersion` - `ExchangeRate`'s effective-dated-row pattern, not a snapshot table. Approving a version supersedes the prior open-ended one; `ResolvePriceForSKU` still resolves a Superseded version for dates inside its own historical window.

- **Two real bugs caught by this stage's own test before shipping**: a double rupee→paise conversion corrupted a schedule's `total_amount` by 100x; price resolution wrongly excluded a superseded-but-historically-valid version, breaking backdated lookups. Both fixed before commit.

## 121. Stage 37.4 — Budgeting, cash-flow forecast, credit limits, dunning (2026-09-01, code + schema)

Pre-build audit found all four completely absent, and one structural finding shaped the whole design: neither `SalesOrder` nor `SalesInvoice` carries a real `Customer` Link - customer identity flows as a free-text name throughout, the same convention `GetCustomerLedgerReport` already relies on. Credit-limit matching (37.4.2) inherits that name-based convention rather than adding a second, inconsistent identity model - a real Link-based rework of customer identity across these doctypes is a materially larger, separate undertaking.

- **37.4.1 Budgeting**: `Budget` is a pure generic-document doctype (`Currency`/`ExchangeRate` precedent - no dedicated create/apply function, never posted to the GL). `GetBudgetVarianceReport` compares Approved rows against real `gl_postings`, one level more granular than `GetCostCenterPL`'s own shape (per account, not per account_type).
- **37.4.2 Credit limits**: `Customer.credit_limit` (optional). `CheckCustomerCreditLimit` wired into `PostSalesInvoice` - the deliberate, human-triggered point AR is actually recognized, not the automated Draft-creation cascade, which the function's own doc comment explains is unsuitable for a hard refusal.
- **37.4.3 Due dates**: `SalesInvoice.due_date`/`VendorInvoice.due_date` (both optional). `GetReceivablesAgeingReport` now ages off `COALESCE(due_date, created_at)`.
- **37.4.4 Dunning**: `StartDunningWorker` reuses the existing `DispatchNotification`/outbox machinery entirely - no second delivery path - scanning for invoices past configurable reminder/escalation thresholds, idempotent via a stamped last-notified tier.
- **37.4.5 Cash-flow forecast**: `GetCashFlowForecast` is a real forward projection (unlike the historical-only `GetCashFlowStatement`), from today's real Cash/Bank GL balance plus open invoices due within the horizon.
- **Surface/schema**: additive `db/migrations_stage37_4_budgeting_credit_dunning.sql` (dev only). New `engines/budgeting.go` (+`_test.go`), 2 settings, 3 reports, no new handler file.
- **Two real bugs caught by this stage's own test before shipping, not live**: `CheckCustomerCreditLimit`'s Customer lookup queried a bare `name` column that doesn't exist on `documents` (needed `data->>'name'`) - silently always no-op'd until the test caught it. `GetCashFlowForecast`'s outflow query read `total_amount` for `VendorInvoice`, which actually stores `invoice_amount` (a pre-existing, differently-named field for the same concept SalesInvoice calls `total_amount`) - outflows always computed as zero until fixed.
- **Verified**: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...`/`node --check public/app.js` clean; `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` fully green including new `TestStage374BudgetingCreditDunning`. **Live over HTTP** (port 9262, stopped afterward): a real credit-limit refusal with the exact expected message, and all three new reports responding correctly. Every fixture hard-deleted afterward, zero residue confirmed.
- **Next**: 37.5 (financial statement builder with dimensions and drill-down), then 37.6-37.11 in plan order, then Stage 38's remaining 38.4/38.6/38.7.

## 120. Stage 37.3 — Costing & valuation, incl. landed cost allocation (2026-09-01, code + schema)

Pre-build audit found this codebase had NO costing method anywhere - `StockLedgerEntry`/`bin_stock`/`inventory_availability` track quantity only. Two real, previously-undiscovered gaps fell out of that audit and are closed by this stage, not just the checklist's own "5 sub-items":

- **POS checkout's "COGS" was never real.** `PostSalesFinanceBooking` posted `Dr 5100/Cr 1200` using whatever `cost_price` the client's cart JSON happened to send - unverified against anything, effectively a placeholder.
- **`2100` GRN Suspense had never been credited by anything.** `PostGRNFinanceBooking` (the intended Dr 1200/Cr 2100 GRN-receipt posting) was dead code with zero callers, while `PayVendorInvoice`'s debit to `2100` has been running the whole time - meaning every vendor payment this system has ever posted has driven that account further into an incorrect balance, with nothing on the other side.

**Design**: a single company-wide (not per-location) moving-weighted-average unit cost per item (`item_cost`, `engines/costing.go`) - the Ind AS 2/AS 2 acceptable method, avoiding FIFO/LIFO layer tracking this codebase has no infrastructure for. The average only moves on receipt (a GRN, or a landed-cost top-up applied once a freight/customs bill arrives later) - never on issue - so nothing needed to intercept this codebase's many stock-out paths to stay correct; a real "current value" is computed at report time by multiplying real on-hand qty against the stored rate instead.

- **37.3.1** `item_cost` + the moving-average engine.
- **37.3.2** `LandedCostVoucher` (Draft → Applied, one-shot) - spreads charge lines proportionally by each GRN line's own base value, posting `Dr 1200 / Cr 2110` (new Landed Cost Clearing account).
- **37.3.3** `ResolveCOGSUnitCostPaise` wired into `FinalizePOSCheckout` - a real cost wins over the client-submitted figure; an uncosted item falls back unchanged, so nothing changes for a tenant that has never used GRN-based receiving.
- **37.3.4** `GetInventoryValuation` report - on-hand qty × the current rate, computed fresh rather than tracked as a parallel pool.
- **37.3.5** `RecordGRNReceiptCosting`, hooked into `PostGRNReceiptWithQC` - resolves each line's ex-GST base cost from the GRN's own PO (reusing `PreviewPurchaseOrder`'s existing GST-aware taxable computation) and posts `Dr 1200 / Cr 2100` - the missing credit side of the 2100 gap above.
- **Surface/schema**: additive `db/migrations_stage37_3_costing_valuation.sql` (dev only, surgical apply). New `engines/costing.go` + `costing_test.go`, `internal/server/handlers_costing.go`, 2 routes, 1 GL account, 1 report.
- **Stated limitations**: company-wide not per-location costing; a landed cost voucher applied after intervening activity spreads over the item's *current* pool using the original GRN qty as the weight (an approximation once the pool has moved on); GST input credit (1500) is deliberately not recognised at GRN time (GST law ties it to a matched tax invoice, not a PO estimate) - a separate, pre-existing VendorInvoice-side gap, not attempted here.
- **Verified**: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...`/`node --check public/app.js` clean; `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` fully green including new `TestStage373CostingValuation`. **Live over HTTP** (port 9261, stopped afterward): a real Item + Approved PO + GRN created via the actual `POST /api/v1/doc/GRN` path, correct item_cost and 1200/2100 GL entries, a landed cost voucher created and applied over HTTP with correct 1200/2110 entries and average-cost top-up, and the `inventory-valuation` report reading back the right figure. Every fixture hard-deleted afterward, zero residue confirmed.
- **Next**: 37.4 (budgeting, cash-flow forecast, credit limits, dunning), then 37.5-37.11 in plan order, then Stage 38's remaining 38.4/38.6/38.7.

---

## 119. Stage 37.2 — Multi-entity & intercompany: entity-scoped posting, mirrored entries, reconciliation, consolidation (2026-09-01, code + schema)

User asked to build the remaining open Stage 37/38 backlog, starting with 37.2 (the largest ERP-core item still open after 37.1's multi-currency work closed). `LegalEntity` (Stage 17.9) already existed as a Master, linked from `Location.legal_entity`, but nothing transacted across entities — this closes that gap entirely inside one tenant's schema (confirmed via `engines/saas.go` that one schema = one tenant = one consolidation group; there is no cross-tenant consolidation model in this codebase and this stage does not invent one).

- **37.2.1 Entity-scoped posting**: `PostingOptions.Entity` / `JournalVoucherOptions.Entity` (`engines/finance.go` / `journal_voucher.go`), the exact Stage 26.6.8 `CostCenter`/`Department` precedent — additive, variadic, zero existing call sites touched. New nullable `gl_postings.entity` column.
- **37.2.2 `IntercompanyTransaction`**: the `JournalVoucher` maker-checker shape (Draft → Pending Approval → Approved), but approval posts **two** independently-balanced `PostDoubleEntry` calls — one per entity's book (FROM: Dr 1700 Due from Intercompany / Cr its chosen account; TO: Dr its chosen account / Cr 2500 Due to Intercompany) — because `PostingOptions.Entity` is whole-posting and the two legs need two different entities. That makes the pair non-atomic across one DB transaction by construction; a mid-way failure lands in a named "Partially Posted" state with a `RetryPostApprovedIntercompanyTransaction` escape hatch, rather than silently losing the second leg.
- **37.2.3 `GetIntercompanyReconciliation`**: per entity-pair, the IC ledger's claim vs. each side's real control-account balance. Caught a real sign bug in its own first draft (2500 is a liability and needed to be read credit-normal, not debit-normal like 1700) via this stage's own test before it ever shipped.
- **37.2.4 `ComputeIntercompanyEliminations`**: consolidation-worksheet-only — never posted to any entity's real book (no fictitious "Consolidation entity" invented). Always eliminates the matched 1700/2500 pair; also eliminates the revenue/expense pair when the chosen accounts are actually typed that way, stated as a scope boundary rather than attempting a general unrealised-profit elimination this codebase has no machinery for.
- **37.2.5 `GetConsolidatedTrialBalance` + `GetEntityTrialBalance`**: the tenant-wide trial balance before/after eliminations, plus a per-entity breakdown, both as registered reports (no new frontend code).
- **Surface/schema**: additive `db/migrations_stage37_2_multi_entity_intercompany.sql` (applied to dev only, surgical single-file apply — `-migrate-status` still shows the pre-existing, unrelated `migrations_stage42_6_labour_billing_depth.sql` pending from an earlier session, left untouched). New `engines/intercompany.go` + `intercompany_test.go`, `internal/server/handlers_intercompany.go`, 2 routes, 2 GL accounts, 3 reports.
- **Verified**: `go build ./...` / `go vet ./...` / `node --check public/app.js` clean; `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` fully green including the new `TestStage372MultiEntityIntercompany`. **Live over HTTP** on a throwaway server (port 9260, stopped afterward): full create → submit → approve → Posted lifecycle for a real ₹2,500 intercompany transaction between two fixture entities, all three reports read back correct figures (reconciliation `in_balance: true`; consolidated 1700/2500/5400 each net to zero post-elimination; 4100's revenue reduced by exactly the eliminated amount) — every fixture hard-deleted afterward, zero residue confirmed by direct SQL.

## 117. Stage 36.4/36.6/36.7 — export & syndication, DAM depth, enrichment & quality — Stage 36 closes (2026-08-30, code + schema)

Closes the rest of Stage 36 (PIM parity, Unbxd level): export & syndication depth (36.4), DAM depth (36.6), and enrichment & quality (36.7, its last five sub-items — 36.7.2 had shipped earlier). With 36.1-36.7 all now built and verified, Stage 36 is **complete** and has been moved out of `micro_checklist.md` into `docs/archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md`. Full item-by-item detail lived there before the move and now lives in that archive entry; this section is the index.

- **36.4 — export & syndication.** Three new doctypes (`PIMExportTemplate`, `PIMExportSchedule`, `PIMCatalog`). A template's columns are scoped to exactly a channel's own `ChannelFieldMap` target fields when a channel is set (so "per-channel format" reuses `BuildChannelPayload` rather than a second payload-building path), with a headerless mode and a parent/variant-collapsed shape. `PIMCatalog` exposes a product group as a shareable, tokenised, read-only link (digest-only token storage, the same crypto/rand shape 36.3.4's import hook and 38.2a's API keys use) resolved by a new public `pim-catalog-share.html` viewer that makes only one unauthenticated GET. Bulk channel download deepens the existing publish-diff preview from "what we last sent" to "what the channel actually holds" via a new optional `ChannelReader` interface (Shopify only — a stated scope limit, not a guess), sharing one `buildChannelDiff` helper with the existing preview so the two views can't disagree.
- **36.6 — DAM depth.** On-demand image transform presets (small/medium/large/square) generated lazily and cached by `checksum+preset`, reusing (not duplicating) the existing thumbnail's resize math. Bulk zip up/download with filename-based auto-association (`ITEMCODE__role.ext`, round-trippable, decompressed-size-bounded against zip bombs) reuses the exact same `SaveMediaFile` a single upload already goes through, so every existing guard (extension allowlist, content sniffing, checksum dedup, Main Image demotion) applies to a bulk file too. A new catalog-wide **Media Library** tab gives tag/role/file-type search and inline tag editing the browse UI Unbxd's own DAM apps offer, sitting alongside (not replacing) the Workbench's existing per-item gallery.
- **36.7 — enrichment & quality, the last five sub-items.** Channel-shaped content assist (`shape=marketplace`: tighter title, bullet points, meta description — still local/offline per 26.4.11's governance decision, deliberately generic rather than naming a platform). Related products (same family + shared global attribute values, ranked by overlap — the other half of 26.4.2's duplicate detection). UPC/EAN generation and check-digit validation: one canonical right-to-left GS1 mod-10 algorithm correct for EAN-8/UPC-A/EAN-13 alike (a naive left-to-right weighting silently flips which positions take which multiplier depending on odd/even base length — caught by pinning the implementation against three published real-world GTINs, not hand-computed ones), generation seeded from GS1's own internal-use `020`-`029` prefix range through the existing document-numbering machinery rather than a second counter. Bulk catalog translation seeds a Draft in a target language from each item's Approved source-language content — the "translation source" question the plan spec flagged as needing a decision was resolved the same local-only way 26.4.11 already was: no translation API, no network call, just a deterministic starting point a human still has to translate and push through the existing approval gate. **A real gap found and closed**: `engines/field_permissions.go`'s `RejectRestrictedFieldWrites` has always gated the generic single-document update path, but neither `BulkUpdateDocuments` (PIM bulk-edit/Group Actions) nor the CSV/template import path ever called it — a role blocked from editing a field one document at a time (the shipped Cashier/`Item.gst_rate` policy) could still push that same field through in bulk. Fixed at the one shared choke point in each function rather than at every call site.
- **Scope touched**: 2 new migrations (`db/migrations_stage36_4_export_syndication.sql`, `db/migrations_stage36_6_dam_depth.sql`, `db/migrations_stage36_7_enrichment_quality.sql`), 8 new engine files (`pim_catalog.go`, `pim_export_template.go`, `pim_export_schedule.go`, `pim_dam.go`, `pim_content_assist.go`'s shape extension, `pim_related.go`, `pim_barcode.go`, `pim_translation.go`, plus their test files), 3 new handler files, ~15 new routes, 6 new PIM tabs (Import Templates/Schedules retroactively, Export Templates/Schedules, Catalogs, Media Library), one public static viewer page.
- **Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `node --check public/app.js` clean at every sub-stage close; the full new/extended test files all passing, `go test ./... -p 1` **fully green** (including a shared-DB tag-collision false failure caused by this session's own live-verification residue, traced to `ProductMedia`'s reads gating on `status` rather than the generic `deleted_at` column and fixed by hard-deleting the fixture rather than soft-deleting them). Every migration applied directly to the dev database (ledger row inserted matching `erp-server -migrate`'s own format) to avoid also applying the unrelated already-pending `migrations_stage42_6_labour_billing_depth.sql`. Live-HTTP passes for 36.4/36.7's every route; 36.6's file-write routes could not complete a full live-HTTP round-trip **on this specific Windows dev machine** (Controlled Folder Access silently drops a freshly-built, unsigned binary's write when its working directory sits under `Documents\`, even through a directory junction) — not a code defect: `engines/pim_dam_test.go`'s own `TestMain` already proves the read/write logic correct against a real `%TEMP%` path in-process, and production's working directory is not under `Documents\`. Not committed, not deployed.

## 116. Stage 36.5 + 36.3 — declarative transform rules, and PIM import depth (2026-08-28, code + schema)

Opens Stage 36's remaining five top-level items (36.3-36.7 — import, export/syndication, transform rules, DAM depth, enrichment) with the two built in dependency order: 36.5 first (a shared value-transform seam), then 36.3 (import depth), which consumes it. Full item-by-item detail, including the scope calls made explicit before building, is in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 36.

- **36.5 — the gap was that a channel field mapping could only rename a value, never transform it.** `ChannelFieldMap` (Stage 16.1) maps a source field to a target field and passes the value through unchanged; there was no way to say "uppercase this" or "prefix the SKU" without writing Go. New `PIMTransformRule` (Master) holds an ordered list of steps drawn from a **closed vocabulary** (`trim`, `uppercase`, `lowercase`, `prefix`, `suffix`, `truncate`, `default_if_empty`, `find_replace_literal` — literal, not regex, `number_format`, `date_format`), the same shape and reasoning Stage 36.2.3's workflow conditions already established: a rule is authored in a form, and a form that accepts an expression is a remote-execution surface. `ApplyPIMTransformRule` is the one seam; `ChannelFieldMap` gained one additive `transform_rule` field so `BuildChannelPayload` (`engines/connector.go`) applies it when set, blank meaning today's exact passthrough. Verified live: `SKU-GOLD RING` came out of a trim→uppercase→prefix rule applied to an item named "Gold Ring" through a real channel-publish preview, and deactivating the rule mid-flight correctly failed the payload build loudly rather than silently falling back.
- **36.3 — six sub-items, all closing a gap in the pre-existing `BulkImportCSV`.** That engine already had dry-run preview, batching and create/update classification; what it lacked was a way to point it at a source whose column headers don't match this doctype's own field names, a way to trigger it on a schedule or from an external system, and any awareness that an Item can be a variant with a parent. `PIMImportTemplate` (column mapping + per-column transform rule + default value) and `PIMImportSchedule` (Drop Directory or API Hook) are two new doctypes; `RunPIMImportTemplate` remaps a source file through a template and hands it to the **same shared batching core** a plain CSV upload uses — a small refactor of `engines/import.go` (`csvRowsToDocData`/`runDocDataImport` split out of `BulkImportCSV`/`importBatch`) means the two paths cannot drift apart. Variant-parent-awareness (`pimVariantParentPreflight`) is wired into that shared core directly, so it applies to *every* Item import, not just a templated one. A native SFTP client was explicitly scoped out (would need a new dependency or hand-rolled wire-protocol work); a directory the worker scans doubles as an SFTP source once ops mounts it as a filesystem path. The API hook (`POST /api/v1/pim/import/hook`) authenticates with a header-carried per-schedule secret (SHA-256 digest-only storage, Stage 38.2a's shape) rather than a token-in-the-URL, because `middleware.go`'s `publicRoutes` is a deliberately small allowlist of exact path strings, not a prefix rule.
- **A real pre-existing bug found while adding this stage's own tests, unrelated to either feature**: `db.InitDB` never closed the previous connection pool before opening a new one. Harmless in production (called once), but the test suite calls it at the top of nearly every DB-touching test function in a package, and enough test files now exist that the accumulated leaked idle connections could exhaust Postgres's connection limit partway through `go test ./engines/...` — reproduced deterministically, fixed with one `if DB != nil { DB.Close() }`, benefits every existing and future test in the suite.

**Scope touched**: two new migrations (`db/migrations_stage36_5_transform_rules.sql`, `db/migrations_stage36_3_import_depth.sql`; 3 doctypes total, one additive field on `ChannelFieldMap`), five new engine files (`pim_transform.go`, `pim_import_template.go`, `pim_import_schedule.go` + their two test files), two new handler files, 8 new routes, one new worker (`StartPIMImportScheduleWorker`), one small refactor of the pre-existing `import.go` and `connector.go`, one one-line fix in `db/db.go`. No frontend surface yet — both sub-stages are API-only so far, the same pattern Stage 37.1.5 left its own two new routes in.

**Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `node --check public/app.js` clean; new `engines/pim_transform_test.go` and `engines/pim_import_test.go` (pure-function table tests for all 10 transform functions, template/schedule validator refusal/acceptance cases, the variant-preflight table, and a full drop-directory worker run exercised directly at the Go level since the real worker only fires hourly) all passing, and `go test ./... -p 1` **fully green** after the `db.InitDB` fix (the pre-existing `TestWMSP2/SlottingSuggestions` shared-DB flake reproduced once and cleared on retry, as previously documented — unrelated to this work). Both migrations applied directly to the dev database (ledger rows inserted matching `erp-server -migrate`'s own format) rather than via the global `-migrate` flag, to avoid also applying an unrelated already-pending migration from Stage 42.6's concurrent session — the same concurrent-session-safe apply approach §115 used. **Live over HTTP** on two throwaway servers (ports 9153 and 9163, both stopped and released, every fixture deleted afterwards): the transform-rule round-trip above, plus template create/list/mapping-preview, an unknown-field template refused by name, dry-run vs. real templated import, a transform-rule-wired column actually changing the stored value, a variant row with a missing parent refused by name, and the API hook running a real import with **zero authentication** (no bearer token, no session) — wrong token 404s, correct token imports, rotating invalidates the old one immediately. Not committed, not deployed.

## 115. Stage 35.8/35.9 — Settlement reconciliation ("UniReco") and returns depth (2026-08-25, code + schema + UI)

Closes the last two open items of Stage 35's OMS parity plan: 35.8 (5 sub-items) and 35.9 (3 sub-items). Full item detail in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 35.

- **35.8 — a real matching engine, not just a manual GL post.** The pre-existing `ProcessMarketplaceSettlement` (Stage 13.7) trusted the caller for total sale/commission/net payout and reconciled nothing against what was actually invoiced — it stays untouched as the manual fallback. New `MarketplaceSettlementLine` rows arrive through the **existing generic CSV import path** (no new upload endpoint), and `ReconcileMarketplaceSettlements` resolves each line to an order via the existing `channel_order_mapping` table, compares the marketplace's reported gross against the order's own posted `SalesInvoice` total, and auto-posts a within-tolerance match or holds a `Variance` for `RaiseSettlementDispute`/`ResolveSettlementDispute`/`WriteOffSettlementVariance`. One shared GL poster keeps the auto-match and write-off paths accounting-identical: Cash/Bank, Commission Expense, two new tax-credit receivables (TDS/TCS), a new fees expense, and a new variance-write-off expense absorbing whatever gap remains — proven balanced by test on both paths.
- **35.9 — three real behaviours on top of the Stage 26.12.5 returns workflow, no new doctypes.** Reverse pickup reuses the Stage 26.12.4/35.5 courier machinery completely unmodified (same `CreateLogisticsBooking`/`AllocateCourierAWB`/`ScheduleCourierPickup`, same `RecordDeliveryEvent` tracking-ingestion choke point, now branching on a `shipment_direction=Reverse` tag to auto-receive the return instead of firing a forward delivery notification). Exchange/alternate-item is a same-value-only swap: the exchange SKU's stock is deducted from the same return location in the same transaction as the returned item's own receipt (reusing `bundles.go`'s ATS-check-then-ledger-entry shape), so a shortage rolls back the whole QC call, not just the exchange leg. Return routing auto-resolves a blank `return_location` for an RTO via the existing Nearest-Pincode allocation strategy, reused in reverse; a Customer Return (no shipping-address source) keeps requiring an explicit location.
- **A real gap found and stated, not fixed here**: the `ReturnRequest`/`RefundRequest` workflow has never had a management UI — only a read-only "Returns" section inside the OMS console's order detail (35.2.2). 35.9 is complete and tested at the API layer; a full Returns screen is a separate, larger item.

**Scope touched**: new `db/migrations_stage35_8_settlement_reconciliation.sql` (4 GL accounts, 1 doctype, append to `ReasonCode.category`), new `engines/settlement_reconciliation.go`, new `internal/server/handlers_settlement_reconciliation.go`, 4 new routes, 2 new settings (`oms.settlement_variance_tolerance`, `oms.settlement_aging_days`), 2 new registered reports, 1 new app.js panel (extends the existing Marketplace screen). Returns depth touched only `engines/returns.go` (exchange + routing + reverse-pickup entry point) and `engines/marketplace.go` (the one new branch in `RecordDeliveryEvent`) — no schema migration needed there, since neither `ReturnRequest` nor `LogisticsBooking` was ever registered with `doctype_fields`.

**Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `node --check public/app.js` clean; new `engines/stage35_8_9_test.go` covers row-math rejection, a clean auto-match with a balanced GL split, a held Variance that does not post, a write-off that closes it balanced against the full invoiced amount, a same-value exchange (both stock movements + refund correctly excluded), its insufficient-ATS rollback (asserting the *original* item's own receipt was rolled back too), and RTO auto-routing (nearest of two candidate locations). The pre-existing `TestEngines/ReturnsRTOQCRefund` and `TestEngines/AllocationSourcing` regression subtests both still pass unchanged. Full `go test ./... -p 1` green across all four packages on the combined shared tree. Migration applied to dev via a concurrent-session-safe single-file apply (not `-migrate`, which would also have picked up the unrelated, still-pending Stage 42.6 migration sitting in the same directory from a concurrent session). Not committed, not deployed.

## 114. Stage 35.7 — commercial bundles and physically stocked kits (2026-08-22, code + schema + UI)

All four Stage 35.7 items are complete. `ProductBundle` is intentionally not another manufacturing BOM: it describes a sellable commercial composition, with Virtual/Stocked fulfillment and Parent/Fixed/Component pricing. Validation pins every SKU to an active Item, forbids duplicate/self/nested components and keeps one active definition per bundle SKU.

Virtual availability has no balance to drift. The new sellable-SKU seam derives each component through the existing seven-term `computeATS`, divides by required component quantity and takes the limiting result. Network availability sums only bundles complete at one facility, rather than combining stranded parts across locations. That seam now feeds both `/availability` and channel ATS push. At order creation a Virtual line becomes attributed component SalesOrderLines before sourcing/reservation; the parent/fixed revenue is allocated exactly, while the SalesOrder retains the commercial bundle summary. A Stocked kit remains one normal stock/pick line.

Assembly and disassembly use one transaction for `BundleAssembly`, every component/kit availability mutation and every append-only `StockLedgerEntry`. Consumption checks ATS, not raw available stock, so reserved or held components cannot be stolen by the kit floor. A shortage leaves no partial movement or operation row; a unique request key makes transport retries safe. The OMS panel displays definitions and location ATS and drives stocked-kit operations through a WMS-gated route.

**Schema/concurrency**: additive `db/migrations_stage35_7_bundles_kits.sql` was applied surgically and ledger-recorded on dev, propagating both doctypes/fields/permissions/indexes to `tenant_default` and `tenant_new_schema`. The parallel Stage 42.6 migration was not applied, and its files were not edited. Committed `2a45c49` (2026-08-22), not yet deployed.

**Verified**: the migration-backed Stage 35.7 regression covers all pricing modes, definition refusals, ATS, expansion/reservation, exact revenue, stocked-kit behavior, assembly/replay/disassembly, shortage rollback and ledger evidence. Final build/vet/JS syntax checks and the entire serial Go suite are green.

---

## 113. Stage 35.6 — channel breadth becomes an operational SDK (2026-08-22, code + schema + UI)

All seven Stage 35.6 items are code-complete. The new connector contract declares auth validation, order pull, inventory push, catalogue publish, status push, error mapping and capabilities; the scheduler and UI consume capabilities instead of platform-name conditionals. Amazon and Flipkart implement their public seller contracts, WooCommerce implements REST v3, and Myntra/Meesho/Ajio/Nykaa/Blinkit/Zepto/Swiggy Instamart use a fail-closed negotiated-path adapter because their seller contracts are private. Quick-commerce descriptors bind orders to a store location and forbid split allocation.

Inventory sync reuses the existing availability truth: active channel configuration writes the conservative oversell buffer into `channel_buffer`, then all outbound quantities pass through `computeATS`. Mapping rows now retain external product and optional channel-location ids. Unmapped order SKUs open one deduplicated `ChannelSKUException`; resolving the mapping closes the queue row. `ChannelSyncRun` feeds the new OMS panel with last sync, lag, 24-hour failure rate, open exceptions and capability-aware manual actions.

**Scope and honesty boundaries**: the established Shopify/BigCommerce/Magento/Adobe Commerce catalogue implementations remain intact and are exposed as catalogue-only capabilities; their existing webhook/poll intake was not replaced by fabricated operations. Amazon/Flipkart/WooCommerce are fixed public-contract implementations, while six Indian partner channels require real negotiated endpoint paths in encrypted credentials. No seller account was called. Amazon order intake uses the current `2026-01-01` contract; its shipment confirmation remains on Amazon's still-live legacy operation and requires item ids/quantities.

**Schema/concurrency**: `db/migrations_stage35_6_channel_breadth.sql` is additive, dev-applied and ledger-recorded, including propagation to existing tenant schemas. New work is confined to the Stage 35.6 connector/sync/test/handler files and the OMS panel, with narrow shared edits in `orders.go`, `channel_orders.go` and `routes.go`. The parallel Stage 42.6 files were preserved and its migration was not applied. Committed `2a45c49` (2026-08-22), not yet deployed.

**Verified**: official Amazon/Flipkart contracts were re-read and corrected before handoff; HTTP tests pin Amazon, Flipkart and WooCommerce shapes, and the real-Postgres test pins exception dedupe → mapping → resolution. `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `node --check public/app.js`, `go test ./db`, focused server tests and all `TestStage356*` tests are green.

## 113. Stage 42.5.5 — Multi-owner stock segregation, closing Phase 42.5 and Stage 42 (2026-08-24, code + schema)

Closes the one item §110 left open: 42.D2 ("is 3PL/multi-owner a real target?") was resolved — yes, build it, the same call already made for §112's 42.6.6-42.6.9. Phase 42.5 is now 8/8 and Stage 42 (58 items across 6 phases) is fully closed. Full item detail in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 42.

- **A new breakdown table, not a column on `bin_stock`**: `bin_stock_owner` sits under `bin_stock` the same way `bin_stock_batch` already does, and for the identical reason — an `owner_id` column would force it into `bin_stock`'s primary key, a destructive rework the first principle forbids. `RecordOwnerStock`/`ConsumeOwnerStock` enforce the same "breakdown can't exceed the parent" invariant as the batch pair.
- **Backward-compatible by construction**: `Bin.owner_id` (Stage 26.5) is untouched and stays the fallback attribution for any bin/SKU slice nobody has explicitly segregated — the combined query the billing paths now run returns byte-identical totals to before this item existed, for any tenant that never calls `RecordOwnerStock`.
- **Closes the exact approximation both billing paths named**: `GetStorageBillingReport` (26.5.15's v1 report) and `CaptureStorageBalanceSnapshot`/`GetStorageBillingV2` (§112's v2) now read real per-owner stock instead of assuming a bin belongs wholly to one owner. §112's task-completion event monitor (`stage42TaskOwner`) now resolves the actual per-SKU owner of a completed task before falling back to the bin-level guess.

**Scope touched**: new `db/migrations_stage42_5_5_owner_segregation.sql`, new `engines/wms_owner_stock.go`; narrow edits to `engines/wms_3pl_billing.go` and `engines/wms_labour_billing.go` (both billing paths rewired onto the new combined query), `engines/inventory.go` (`StockLedgerEntry.OwnerID`, same convention as `BatchNo`/`SerialNo`), `engines/saas.go` (added to the tenant-provisioning clone list — the exact 26.11.2 bug class), `engines/wms_stage42_5_test.go` (new subtest), `internal/server/handlers_wms_inventory_depth.go` + `routes.go` (two new routes). No third-party dependency, no frontend screen — reachable via the two routes plus the new `owner-stock-inquiry` report and the two existing billing reports.

**Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./... -p 1` fully green across all four packages, including the new subtest and the pre-existing `TestWMSP2/StorageBillingReport`/`TestStage42_6LabourBillingDepth` regressions (unchanged assertions, still pass against the rewired queries). Migration applied surgically to the dev DB (not via `-migrate`, which would also have picked up the still-pending, unrelated §112 Stage 42.6 migration). Live-verified over real HTTP on a scratch server (port 9199, stopped after): assign, over-assignment correctly refused, the inquiry report, and consume all round-tripped against real dev-DB data. Not committed, not deployed.

## 112. Stage 42.6 — Labour and billing depth (2026-08-22, code + schema)

All nine listed Phase 42.6 rows are built (the source heading calls the phase eight items, but the checklist contains 42.6.1 through 42.6.9).

Configurable `ChargeGroup`, `RateGroup`, `ChargeCode`, and dated `ChargeContract` records flow into immutable `CapturedCharge` records; owner-attributed completed tasks auto-capture idempotently, with a manual accessorial endpoint for other events.

Captured charges batch into a deterministic existing `SalesInvoice`, which is posted through `PostSalesInvoice` so it produces the ordinary AR/GL document rather than a parallel billing object.

## 111. Stage 35.5 — Courier integration crosses the provider boundary (2026-08-22, code + schema)

All six Stage 35.5 items are code-complete using the parity plan's default first providers, Delhivery and Shiprocket; actually calling either account remains credential-gated. Full item detail is in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 35.

- **One provider contract, two real adapters**: `CourierAdapter` isolates AWB allocation, pickup, cancellation, quotes and webhook parsing. Provider credentials reuse the existing encrypted `channel_credentials` store under `courier:<provider>`; the API can write them and report configured/not-configured, never read them back.
- **Durable shipment semantics**: a provider AWB replaces the old internal placeholder only after the courier confirms it, and a retry returns the already-allocated AWB rather than buying a second label. Pickup/cancel references are recorded on the same `LogisticsBooking`.
- **Labels and tracking**: a dependency-free 4×6 PDF renderer draws a checksum-valid Code 128-B as vector bars and preserves the existing invoice-before-label guard. Signed tracking payloads normalize Delhivery/Shiprocket vocabulary into the existing delivery/RTO state machine; provider event ids are recorded idempotently.
- **Rate shopping and NDR**: serviceable quotes sort by charge then ETA while isolating one provider's outage. Failed delivery opens/refreshed an `NDRCase`, with explicit re-attempt, close and RTO actions instead of collapsing every failure into RTO.

**Scope touched**: additive dev-applied migration `db/migrations_stage35_5_courier_integration.sql`; new `engines/courier.go`, `courier_adapters.go`, `shipping_label_pdf.go`, `courier_stage35_test.go`; new `internal/server/handlers_courier.go` + `handlers_courier_test.go`; nine additive routes in `routes.go`; two exact webhook entries and their 30/min category in `middleware.go`. Zero third-party dependencies, no frontend change, no live-provider claim without credentials.

**Verified**: `go build ./...` and `go vet ./...` clean on the combined tree; migration-backed Stage 35.5 regression suite green; `internal/server`, `internal/kb` and `db` suites green. A whole-suite attempt hit the repository's documented `db.InitDB` connection-pool ceiling in unrelated engine tests; this Stage's suite was rerun clean after the pool settled. Brain coverage is 100%, 0 files unclaimed.
