## 110. Stage 42.5 — Inventory control depth: physical inventory, CycleClass, replenishment breadth, slotting v2, facility hierarchy/copy/cross-facility inquiry (2026-08-22, code + schema)

7 of 8 items (42.5.1-42.5.4, 42.5.6-42.5.8); 42.5.5 (multi-owner stock segregation) excluded, still gated on open decision 42.D2 (is 3PL a real target?). Full item-by-item detail in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 42.

- **42.5.1 (physical inventory)**: `PhysicalInventory` header doctype + `StartPhysicalInventory`/`ReconcilePhysicalInventory`/`ClosePhysicalInventory`/`CancelPhysicalInventory` (`engines/wms_physical_inventory.go`). Deliberately **not** a second counting mechanism — every line it creates is a plain `CycleCountLine`, and reconciliation/posting reuses `ReconcileCycleCount`/`PostCycleCountAdjustment`/the existing maker-checker approval exactly as Stage 20.22/26.5.10 built them, so `handleDecideApproval`'s finalize-on-approve hook needed zero changes. The freeze reuses 42.2.6's `bin_status='Counting'` value rather than a new flag, and this Stage extended that check into picking allocation (`engines/traceability.go`'s `allocateByOrder`/`allocateFEFO`), which had previously only been enforced on the putaway side.
- **42.5.2 (`CycleClass`)**: new Master doctype for configurable count-frequency tiers. `GetCycleCountPlan` classifies by a tenant's own cumulative-cutoff tiers when any are defined, and falls back to the untouched `GetABCCycleCountPlan` (still the fixed 20/30/50 A/B/C split) when none are — satisfying "the default, not the only option" without a divergent reimplementation.
- **42.5.3 (replenishment breadth)**: demand-driven (`GetDemandDrivenReplenishmentSuggestions`, keyed off open Pick-`WarehouseTask` demand rather than a static min_qty), wave-triggered (`GetWaveReplenishmentSuggestions`, scoped to one Wave's own SKUs), and dynamic pick-face (`GetDynamicPickFaceSuggestions`/`ApplyDynamicPickFaceMinMax`, recomputed from trailing sales velocity) alongside 26.5.5's original min/max. All three share the same reserve-bin selection the min/max path already used (extracted as `findReplenishmentReserves`).
- **42.5.4 (slotting v2)**: unslotting (`GetUnslottingSuggestions`, keyed off `Item.status = 'Cancelled'` — this codebase's real "leaving the catalogue" signal, there being no separate Item Active/Inactive field), capacity/dimension-aware re-slot (`GetDimensionAwareSlottingSuggestions`, v1's Tier-A case redone to actually check candidate bins' spare capacity instead of blindly picking the first PickFace bin), and clean-location consolidation (`GetConsolidationSuggestions`, empties a SKU's smallest fragmented bin into its largest when it fits). All read-only reports; execution reuses the existing `ExecuteBinReplenishment`.
- **42.5.6/42.5.7 (facility hierarchy + copy)**: `Location` gained optional `parent`/`level` fields; `GetFacilityDescendants` (cycle-safe BFS) + `GetFacilityRollup` (roll-up inquiry, registered report `facility-rollup`). `CopyFacilityConfig` clones a source Location's Active Zones/Bins onto an existing empty target Location — config only, every cloned bin starts genuinely empty.
- **42.5.8 (cross-facility inquiry)**: the in-transit bucket itself already existed (`inventory_availability.in_transit`, Stage 17.6) — the actual gap was the cross-location view, filled by `GetCrossFacilityStock` (report `cross-facility-stock`).

**Scope touched**: 1 new migration (`db/migrations_stage42_5_inventory_control_depth.sql`), 3 new engine files (`wms_physical_inventory.go`, `wms_facility.go`, `wms_stage42_5_test.go`), 1 new handler file (`handlers_wms_inventory_depth.go`, 16 routes), additive edits to `traceability.go`/`wms_pack_count.go`/`wms_putaway_ext.go`/`wms_slotting.go`/`routes.go`. Zero new dependencies, no frontend changes — all 7 items are reachable via API + the generic DocType form + the report framework; a dedicated Physical Inventory floor-ops screen and a facility hierarchy tree view are follow-up UI work.

**Verified**: `go build`/`go vet` clean, `go test ./... -p 1` fully green across all four packages (5 new subtests). Migration applied clean to the dev DB. Live-verified over real HTTP on the dev server: `facility/descendants`, `cycle-count/plan`, `bin-replenishment/demand-driven` responded correctly; `physical-inventory/start` against real dev-DB stock at `WH01` froze 7 bins and created 7 lines, then `physical-inventory/cancel` unfroze them. Dev server stopped and Postgres restored to its pre-session state afterward.

## 109. Stage 46 — Money precision: paise migration (2026-08-21, code + schema)

Closed the two items both `docs/DURABILITY_AUDIT_2026-07-31.md` and `docs/ERP_LOOPHOLES_ANALYSIS.md`'s 2026-08-20 re-evaluation still carried as genuinely open. Full item-by-item detail in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 46.

- **Item code/SKU charset allowlist** (the durability audit's fast tourniquet for finding 6, stored XSS): `itemCodePattern` in `validateItemMasterRules`, rejects HTML/JS metacharacters in an Item's `code` field. Checked real fixture SKUs across the test suite first to size the allowed charset without breaking anything (slashed codes like `PO/HQ/26-27/000001` turned out to belong to PurchaseOrder's own `code` field, a different doctype, so didn't constrain this one).
- **The money-precision decision (finding 7)**: both docs deferred this to the user rather than guessing, since it changes computed financial figures on a live production system. Asked directly; the user chose the full paise `BIGINT` migration over the cheaper `math.Round`-only band-aid.
- **Architecture**: paise internal (`gl_postings.debit`/`.credit`, `PostDoubleEntry`'s maps), rupees external (every report/API response converts at its own boundary via new `RupeesToPaise`/`PaiseToRupees`/`PaiseMap` helpers in `engines/finance.go`) — chosen specifically so the frontend and API contract needed zero changes.
- **The actual bug fixed at its source**, not just the storage type: `pos_checkout.go` was truncating a line's unit price to whole rupees *before* multiplying by quantity (worse than the GST truncation itself - loses precision on every unit, not once per sale), and `PostSalesGSTBooking`/`PostExemptSalesReclass` truncated an already-precise float. Both now convert straight from the original float. The FX revaluation/settlement subsystem's cross-period persisted state was deliberately left in whole rupees (reworking its internal arithmetic was judged higher-risk and out of scope) — its postings scale mechanically via `PaiseMap` at the `PostDoubleEntry` boundary instead.
- **Scope actually touched**: 1 additive multi-tenant migration (`db/migrations_stage45_money_paise.sql` - filename predates discovering a concurrent session had already taken "Stage 45" for an unrelated UI sweep; left as-is since migration filenames are permanent), ~20 `engines/*.go` files (finance/reporting core, POS checkout, vendor invoice/TDS/sales invoice/FX, journal vouchers, debit/credit notes, returns/refunds, payroll, assets, marketplace settlement, bank reconciliation). No new dependency, no frontend edit.

**Verified**: `go build`/`go vet` clean throughout. Provisioned a from-scratch throwaway Postgres instance (own port, own data directory) via the embedded migration runner - all 123 migrations applied cleanly, `gl_postings.debit`/`.credit` confirmed `BIGINT`. Full `go test ./... -p 1` green after fixing 5 test files whose assertions read raw `gl_postings` values and needed the same ×100 the migration performs. Backfill arithmetic double-checked in isolation against a temp table seeded with legacy INT-style rows. Live end-to-end proof of the audit's own worked example via a scratch server: `CalculateGST(999, 18, false)` now posts CGST=₹89.91/SGST=₹89.91 (₹179.82 total, exactly right) instead of the pre-fix ₹178, trial balance still balanced to the paisa. Scratch server and Postgres instance torn down after verification; no production database touched. **Not done**: the production deploy itself (backup → ship binary+migration together → confirm live) is deliberately left for the user's go-ahead, since it's the one step that mutates real financial data in place - see micro_checklist.md 46.7.

## 108. Stage 42.4 CLOSED — Outbound depth: waves, sortation, cartonization v2, packing validation, deconsolidation, loading + Bill of Lading, pre-ship gate, and VAS/kitting off the existing BOM (2026-08-21, code + schema + frontend)

Closed all 11 items of Phase 42.4 in one pass. Full item-by-item detail in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 42.

- **42.4.1/42.4.2 (waves)**: `WaveTemplate` + a real `Wave` document layered on top of 26.5.6's existing free-text `wave_id` tag — a Wave's `id` is exactly the value `AssignTasksToWave`/`GenerateWavePickList` already tag/read, so every pre-42.4 wave keeps working unchanged. `TransitionWaveStatus` is the one forward-only lifecycle choke point, guarded a second way by `validateWaveMasterRules` against the generic doc-save path — the same direct-edit-bypass defence 42.3.5's `Hold` entry established. "Release is when tasks become dispatchable" lands at `GenerateWavePickList` itself (`waveDispatchGate`): a Planned wave's pick list is refused, and a Released wave auto-advances to In Progress on first pull. **42.D5 resolved**: `StartWaveAutoCreationWorker` reuses `StartScheduledReportWorker`'s exact ticker/schema-fanout shape, not a second timer.
- **42.4.3 (sortation)**: `SortStation` + `SortSlot`, `AssignSortSlot`/`ConfirmSortSlot`/`ClearSortSlot` using the `GetNextTask` `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` precedent so two sorters can't claim the same slot. Closes the "batch pick with no sortation" hole in 26.5.6 — a wave's consolidated pick can now be routed back to individual orders at a wall. New Sortation screen.
- **42.4.4 (cartonization v2)**: `SuggestCartonizationV2`, first-fit-decreasing by volume with a weight+volume ceiling; the original qty-only `SuggestCartonization` is untouched. "Dimensional" realised as `Item.volume` (already existed for 42.2.6's bin-capacity check) rather than new L/W/H fields — a first-fit-decreasing packer only needs one scalar per axis. Live-verified: a 3kg-ceiling carton correctly split into 4 boxes instead of v1's 1.
- **42.4.5/42.4.6 (pack station, packing validation)**: `PackStation`/`PackTemplate` masters need zero bespoke frontend (same generic-Master free ride Zone/PutawayStrategy get). `PackingValidationTemplate` + `CompletePackTaskWithValidation` now sit behind `POST /wms/pack-complete`, running an additive pre-pack checklist before deferring to the existing, unchanged `CompletePackTask` — a tenant with no template configured packs exactly as before.
- **42.4.7 (deconsolidation)**: `DeconsolidateLPN` re-tags one bin's stock across multiple outbound-destination LPNs within 26.5.4's existing `bin_stock_lpn` ledger — no new table, no new doctype. A physical bin move is already `ExecuteBinReplenishment`/`CrossDockPutaway`'s job; this is specifically the "one container needs to serve more than one outbound destination" case.
- **42.4.8/42.4.9 (loading, BOL)**: `LoadingTask` against a `DockDoor` + `Trailer`, scan-verified carton-to-trailer (`ScanCartonToTrailer` refuses a non-Invoiced, already-loaded, or wrong-manifest package). Feeds the existing `Manifest` machinery (`GenerateManifest`/`HandoverManifest`), both **deliberately untouched** — LoadingTask is additive, not a hard gate forced in front of an existing, well-tested handover path. Bill of Lading is assembled read-only from a completed load and printed through the existing browser print-sheet mechanism (a new `#bol-print-area`, same `@media print` allowlist pattern `#invoice-print-area` already uses) — no server-side PDF renderer, no new stored document. Pallet exchange counters live as fields on `LoadingTask` itself.
- **42.4.10 (pre-ship gate)**: `PreShipValidationRule`, inert until configured, evaluated by `EvaluatePreShipGate` right before `CompleteLoadingTask` moves Loading→Loaded — all cartons scanned, no loaded SKU under an Active `Hold` at the location, every loaded package has an invoice attached.
- **42.4.11 (VAS/kitting)**: `task_type='VAS'` already existed on `WarehouseTask` (42.2.1); this item adds `bom_id`/`output_qty`/`output_lpn` and `CreateVASTask`/`CompleteVASTask`. **Turned out not to need Stage 35.7 after all** — the plan's own text says "reuses the existing manufacturing BOM," and that BOM (Stage 13.13e/26.9) already exists independently of 35.7's still-unbuilt OMS/PIM bundle concept. `CreateVASTask` refuses an output item that doesn't match the named BOM's `parent_item`, at creation. `CompleteVASTask` consumes components via `PostInventoryLedgerWithVoucher` — the same primitive `IssueProductionMaterial` uses for real Production Orders — and produces the output as a real putaway into `to_bin`, optionally grouped into an LPN. Deliberately does not reuse `ProductionOrder`'s own lifecycle machinery, which would mean building a second task system on top of the first.

**Verified**: `go build`/`go vet`/`node --check app.js` clean; full `go test ./... -p 1` green across all four packages, including 7 new subtests in `engines/wms_stage42_4_test.go`. All 8 new migrations applied cleanly to the dev DB (one label-length fix mid-apply — `doctype_fields.label` is capped at 100 chars — then 8 applied, 117 already up to date). Live-verified over real HTTP on a throwaway server (port 8199, stopped and released): cartonization v2's weight-forced split, a Wave's full lifecycle including a refused illegal skip, the full Sortation cycle, the full Loading→scan→complete→depart→BOL cycle, and VAS's create-time refusal plus a real complete confirmed by direct query (94/3/3 across `inventory_availability`/`bin_stock`/`bin_stock_lpn` — exactly what 6-consumed/3-produced/3-grouped should read). All live fixtures removed afterward via a dedicated cleanup pass; the repo-internal scratch tool used to seed/inspect them deleted with zero trace in `git status`. `graphify update .` run, `docs/brain/` redrawn at 100% coverage with no manual `brain.map.json` edit needed (all 9 new engine files auto-claimed by the existing "wms" glob). **Not done this pass**: no real-browser Playwright pass over the 2 new frontend screens (Sortation, Loading) or the Cockpit's new Wave Monitor panel — verified via `node --check` + live HTTP only. Not committed, not deployed.

## 107. Stage 42.3 CLOSED — Inbound depth: dock scheduling, yard, hold/release, configurable receipt rules, catch weight, planned cross-dock, hazmat compliance, and RF receiving (2026-08-20, code + schema + frontend)

Closed all 10 items of Phase 42.3 in one pass. Full item-by-item detail in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 42.

- **42.3.1/42.3.2/42.3.3 (dock scheduling)**: `DockDoor` master + `Appointment` doctype, both riding the generic doc engine's existing metadata-driven CRUD (no new handlers needed for either). `validateAppointmentMasterRules` is the real scheduling gate — door existence/Active, direction compatibility, start<end, and a half-open-interval overlap count against the door's `max_concurrent_appointments` (default 1). New calendar screen (`renderAppointmentCalendarView`): a Day view laid out as a CSS-flex horizontal timeline, Week view as a door×day chip grid — no calendar library, per the plan.
- **42.3.4 (yard)**: `Trailer` master + `YardCheckIn` log, one-way InYard→AtDoor→Departed enforced by `validateYardCheckInMasterRules` (requires `checked_out_at` exactly on the Departed transition). New Yard Board screen. Infor's 3D yard view stayed out of scope, as planned.
- **42.3.5 (hold/release)**: `PlaceHold` is immediate (its own action endpoint, since `Hold` is deliberately not creatable through the generic doc API); release only through a `HoldReleaseRequest` riding the *existing* maker-checker engine unchanged, with one new branch in `DecideApproval`. `computeATS` gained a `held` parameter threaded through all 6 call sites plus a new `inventory_availability.hold_qty` column - the choke point, not a per-caller retrofit.
- **42.3.6 (configurable receipt rules)**: a `ReceiptValidationRule` master resolved by `getReceiptValidationRule`, consulted by the *existing* PURCHA-0087/0088 checks in `validateGRNRules` rather than a parallel new rule engine - no rule configured reproduces the pre-Stage hard 0%-tolerance/always-blocked behavior exactly.
- **42.3.7 (catch weight)**: `Item.is_catch_weight` + new optional fields on the GRN received-line shape (`actual_weight`, dimensions), enforced by `ValidateReceiptCatchWeightLine`. GRN Workbench gets a weight/dimensions row mirroring the existing batch-row UX.
- **42.3.8 (planned cross-dock)**: `CrossDockPlan` + `PlannedCrossDockPutaway`, 26.5.3's opportunistic `CrossDockPutaway`'s ahead-of-time counterpart - consumes an already-Planned plan instead of scanning live demand; `Transship` stages into its own `XSHIP-<destination>` bin.
- **42.3.9 (hazmat compliance)**: `gtin`/`country_of_origin`/`hazmat_class` on `Item`; a putaway compatibility check (`checkHazmatCompatibility`) wired directly into `PutawayToBin` - the one choke point every putaway already runs through, so cross-dock and planned cross-dock inherit it for free.
- **42.3.10 (RF receiving)**: scans an ASN, then matches each SKU scan directly against `expected_items[].sku` and auto-confirms at the expected qty - this codebase's barcodes already encode the SKU code itself, so no barcode→SKU lookup step exists or is needed (confirmed by re-reading how POS's own scan-to-cart already works). Deliberately qty-confirm only; batch/serial/catch-weight capture stay on the desktop GRN Workbench, the same scope split Infor's own RF screens draw. Resolves open decision 42.D4 as moot: built as a plain keyboard-wedge text input regardless of whether physical scanner hardware is confirmed, since a real HID scanner emulates exactly that keystroke pattern.

**Two real bugs found and fixed during live verification, not written and assumed correct:**
1. **HR/Admin could release a Hold by editing it directly**, completely bypassing the `HoldReleaseRequest` maker-checker gate — `role_permissions.allow_update=FALSE` (set on every role including HR/Admin) turned out not to matter, because HR/Admin bypasses the generic doc endpoint's RBAC check entirely (the same systemwide override `DecideApproval`'s own location check already documents). Closed by adding `validateHoldMasterRules`, a doctype validator — unlike `role_permissions`, a validator runs for every caller with no admin bypass. `ReleaseHold`'s own direct-SQL status write (only reachable from an Approved `HoldReleaseRequest`) never touches this validator, so the legitimate path is unaffected. Regression-covered by `TestHoldDirectEditGuard`.
2. **A new business-rule error silently became a bare 500 with no log line at all.** The catch-weight check's first draft invented `GOODSR-0098` as its error code; `error_catalog_generated.go` is generated from `ERP_Standard_Message_Control_Matrix_Final.xlsx` (`DO NOT EDIT BY HAND`), and `writeAPIErrorDetail` falls back to `GLOBAL-0302`/500 for any code it can't find in that map — silently, before the point where it would otherwise log the error. Confirmed live (curl returned 500 with zero corresponding server-log line). Fixed by reusing `GLOBAL-0002`, the same generic code every other new Stage 42.3 check already uses instead of minting a new one.

**Verified**: `go build`/`go vet`/`node --check` clean; full `go test ./... -p 1` green including 6 new subtests in `engines/wms_stage42_3_test.go` (appointment scheduling/overlap, yard transitions, the Hold direct-edit guard, PlaceHold/ReleaseHold round-trip, hazmat compatibility, catch-weight/tolerance resolution). All 8 new migrations applied cleanly to the dev DB (`erp-server -migrate`: 8 applied, 106 already up to date). Live-verified over real HTTP on a throwaway server (port 8097) end-to-end for every one of the 10 items, including the two bugs above being reproduced *before* the fix and confirmed closed *after* it — not merely re-running the happy path. Not committed, not deployed.

## 106. Stage 42.2 CLOSED — Zone/PutawayStrategy/AllocationStrategy masters, directed putaway, exception follow-on, the warehouse cockpit, and a real bug found in Stage 45's own render mechanism (2026-08-19, code + schema + frontend)

Asked to finish the rest of Phase 42.2 (the warehouse task spine — 4/10 done, 42.2.1-42.2.4 already closed by the prior session). Closed the remaining six items, 42.2.5-42.2.10. Full item-by-item detail in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 42.

- **42.2.5 (`Zone` master)**: deliberately did not make `Bin.zone` a `Link`, despite the plan's own wording — the fourth time this Stage chose a validated Data field over a Link for a human-typed code, for the identical reason (a Link's value must be `documents.id`, and rewriting every live Bin's zone value to an id would be destructive for a cosmetic type change). Resolves open decision 42.D6.
- **42.2.6 (bin capacity)**: reuses the existing `capacity` field as max-qty rather than duplicating it under a new name; adds `max_weight`/`max_volume` (the first real readers of `Item.weight`/`Item.volume` anywhere in this codebase) and an operational `bin_status`, enforced inside `PutawayToBin`'s own transaction.
- **42.2.7 (directed putaway)**: `SuggestPutawayBin` is advisory-only, wired into the Putaway screen's new "Suggest Bin" button — never a gate on the real putaway. Weighs velocity (reusing `GetABCCycleCountPlan` directly), zone sequence, best-fit capacity, hazmat/temperature compatibility (two new optional Item fields), and existing-batch consolidation, behind a `PutawayStrategy` master's `criteria` whitelist.
- **42.2.8 (`AllocationStrategy`)**: generalises 26.5.6's `allocateFIFO` into `allocateByOrder`, one query every non-FEFO strategy shares. Cannot override FEFO for a batch-tracked item — that stays `ResolveAllocationStrategy`'s unconditional first check, exactly as 42.1.5 left it.
- **42.2.9 (exception codes)**: extends `ReasonCode` (append-only on `category`, the same precedent 26.5.10 set) rather than a new doctype. `TransitionWarehouseTaskStatus` now requires a real Active `WMS Exception` ReasonCode to enter `Exception` — free text no longer works. `applyExceptionFollowOn` actually triggers the one follow-on with a real mechanism (Create Count Task); Reallocate/Hold are recorded as an audit event naming what a human must do, honestly, rather than guessed at.
- **42.2.10 (warehouse cockpit)**: `GetWarehouseCockpit` assembles six independently-best-effort sections behind one endpoint; a new sidebar screen. The plan's own dock/appointment strip is omitted, not faked — 42.3 doesn't exist yet.

**A real, pre-existing bug was found and fixed in this pass's own live-browser verification — not in Stage 42's code, but in the very mechanism Stage 45 (the entry below) shipped the same day.** `renderView`'s detached scratch-container fix for the double-render race left every `render*View` function's own `document.getElementById(...)` calls unable to find elements they had just created — `getElementById` searches the live document, and the scratch container wasn't attached to it until the render finished. Confirmed on three unrelated screens (Putaway, Bin Conditions, Wave Picking) via Playwright before concluding it was the buffering mechanism itself. Fixed at the one choke point: the scratch container is now attached off-screen instead of fully detached, preserving Stage 45's anti-flicker intent while letting every renderer's DOM queries resolve again.

**Verified**: `go build`/`go vet`/`node --check` clean; `go test ./... -p 1` fully green including 5 new subtests. Live-verified over real HTTP + Playwright on a throwaway server (port 8146): Zone/Bin validation, PutawayStrategy/AllocationStrategy whitelist + uniqueness rejections, directed-putaway suggestion (screenshotted), the full Exception → ReasonCode → auto-created Count task → cockpit chain end-to-end, and the cockpit's six sections rendering live with zero console errors. All fixtures removed afterwards, confirmed by a zero-row sweep. Not committed, not deployed.

## 105. Stage 45 round 1 — UI/UX design-bar sweep: the render race, the clipped tab bars, and a mid-session misdiagnosis corrected (2026-08-19, frontend only)

User reported 8 issues live against `:8080` deployment screenshots (duplicate panels across three Financial Accounting screens, F&A frozen header, help icon styling, a Wholeops rebrand ask, Setup submenu visuals, PIM tab bar clipping at 90% zoom, a setup banner that shouldn't be dismissible), then asked for a broader "no one has ever seen it, brainless-intuitive" design-bar sweep and to fix everything found. Full item-by-item detail in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 45.

- **The duplicate-panel bug was a router-level race, not three separate screen bugs.** `renderView()`/`renderViewContent()` appended each view's content straight into the live `#view-root`; a second navigation call arriving before the first one's fetch resolved (double-click, or any duplicate trigger) cleared the first call's loading placeholder but not its later-arriving content — both landed, permanently, side by side. Fixed with a `viewRenderToken` counter: each call now builds into a detached scratch container and swaps it into `#view-root` only if its token is still current when its fetch resolves, so a superseded call's output is discarded rather than appended. This is a shared choke point fix (every one of ~45 views routes through this one function), not a per-screen patch — the three screens the user happened to screenshot were incidental, any of them could double-render given the same click timing.
- **A mid-session misdiagnosis, corrected before it became a wrong fix.** The initial theory blamed `.table-wrapper { contain: paint }` for the F&A frozen-header bug. Checking the actual `renderChartOfAccountsPanel` code disproved it — that screen doesn't use `.table-wrapper` at all. No code was changed for this item; the Stage 41.2 sticky-header fix and an existing Chromium-caching workaround are both already correct in the local tree, so the live report most likely reflects a `:8080` deployment that predates one of those two fixes rather than a real local bug. Left as a redeploy-and-recheck item rather than a guessed-at second fix.
- **Module tab bars (PIM/Finance/Reports/HR/Manufacturing) now scroll instead of silently clipping** — `.page-container`'s `overflow-x: hidden` plus each tab bar's plain unwrapped flex row meant that at a narrow enough width (90% zoom, reported), trailing tabs were not just hard to find, they were unreachable through the UI with zero recovery path. Fixed by scoping `overflow-x: auto` to each tab bar itself, not by loosening the page container's own overflow (which would have risked a page-wide horizontal scrollbar for unrelated content). **The first attempt was caught by live verification, not assumed correct from the diff**: `overflow-x: auto` alone, inside `.page-container`'s column flexbox, suppresses the item's content-based automatic min-height (a real CSS Overflow spec interaction — the *used* value of `overflow-y` becomes `auto` regardless of what's declared) — the tab bar collapsed to `height: 0` instead of becoming scrollable. The actual fix is `flex-shrink: 0` (exempts the item from shrinking at all, sidestepping the min-height calculation rather than trying to win it); an accompanying `overflow-y: visible` turned out, on live re-measurement, not to prevent the spec's axis-pairing on its own — `getComputedStyle` still reports `auto` with it set. Re-verified live before calling it closed.
- **Setup-prerequisite banner made permanently non-dismissible**, superseding the Stage 41.2 session-scoped-dismiss design on explicit user direction. Setup submenu group headers got a solid background chip; the sticky search box's border-only seam got the same `box-shadow: inset` fix Stage 41.2 already established for `th` — same bug class, reused rather than reinvented. New `.btn-icon` modifier fixed the help button's stretched-rectangle padding. A new sidebar "Knowledge Center" entry (also auto-picked-up by the sidebar-driven global search index) closes the gap where the only way into the full KB was a drawer footer link or knowing `/help` by heart. "Custom ERP" renamed to "Wholeops" in its 3 hardcoded spots; a "Powered by Wholeops" line and client-logo/tenant-branding upload were deliberately deferred as their own scope — no branding entity exists in this schema yet to hang either on.

**Verified**: `node --check public/app.js` clean, `styles.css` brace-balanced. A live Playwright verification pass against a scratch local server was dispatched in the background and had not yet reported back as this entry was written — see `docs/ai_handover.md` §6 for the pending-verification note and update both docs once it lands. Not committed, not deployed.

## 104. Stage 42.1 CLOSED — outbound lottable validation, UOM conversion, real Code 128, and two bugs live verification caught (2026-08-19, code + schema + docs)

Asked to build through the whole open backlog. Picked up Phase 42.1 where the prior session left it (42.1.1-42.1.9 already closed) and finished the remaining three items — 42.1.7, 42.1.10, 42.1.11 — closing the phase at 11/11. Full item-by-item detail in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 42.

- **42.1.7 (outbound lottable validation)**: a new `LottableConstraint` master — (customer, item-or-blank-for-all, attribute_key, allowed_values) — and `ValidateLotForCustomer`, the single-lot choke point wired into `handleBatchConsume` via an optional `customer` field. **Scoped down deliberately, and said so**: pushing the check into `AllocateFromStock`'s FEFO query to pre-filter pick lists was NOT built, because `GenerateWavePickList` pools allocation demand by SKU across every task in a wave before a single lot is chosen — threading one customer's constraint through that pool is a real design question (whose constraint wins when two customers in the same wave disagree?), not a filter to bolt on. Carried forward as a named question for 42.2's task-spine retrofit rather than guessed at here.
- **42.1.10 (UOM conversion)**: `UOM` + `UOMConversion` (item/from_uom/to_uom/factor) masters and one choke point, `ConvertUOMQty` — tries the direct edge then the inverse (factor divided out), so a tenant enters "1 CASE = 12 EA" once. No `stock_uom` added to `Item`: a direct per-item edge covers every consumer this pass needed, and a base-UOM field nothing reads yet would be a premature field. Wired into all three named consumers — cartonization (`CartonizationItem.UOM`, converted to eaches before packing), pick UoM (a display-only `PickUOM`/`PickQtyInUOM` on `PickListLine`, never blocking a pick over a missing conversion), and 3PL billing units (`StorageBillingRate` gets optional `item`/`billing_uom`; naming both switches the billing report from a whole-location each-count to that one item's units) — deliberately not pricing, per the plan's own scope note.
- **42.1.11 (scannable barcode symbology)**: hand-rolled Code Set B Code 128 (`engines/code128.go`), no new dependency. **The real scope turned out narrower than the plan assumed**: the QZ Tray silent-print path already emits a scanner-correct barcode via the label printer's own native ZPL `^BC` command, so this item only had to fix the *other* path — the `@media print` browser fallback, which rendered the barcode as plain monospace text with zero actual bars. Because a single wrong digit in a 106-row pattern table produces a barcode that looks right but does not scan, the table was fetched from Wikipedia's published ISO/IEC 15417 table rather than typed from memory, and the checksum algorithm is pinned against an independently-sourced worked example (`support.idautomation.com`'s `"1-X7VQBT"` → checksum 80/`'p'`) rather than a self-consistency check alone. Visually verified too: the generated SVG, dropped into the real `.sticker-label` CSS, was screenshotted in a headless Chromium via Playwright — genuine bars of varying width with correct quiet zones, not just a string that contains `<svg>`.

**Two real bugs found and fixed in this pass, neither in the three items above — both in code that was already shipped and marked done:**

- **The generic document-save path never actually called the master-data validator for four doctypes, including two from this very phase.** `internal/server/handlers_core_doc_engine.go` had three scattered `if doctype == ...` blocks (Item, Vendor/Customer, ProductContent) calling `ValidateMasterDataRules` — not the doctype-agnostic choke point its own switch statement implies. `Batch` (42.1.2), `SerialNumber` (42.1.8), `LottableConstraint` (42.1.7) and `UOMConversion` (42.1.10) each had a correct rule function, wired into the switch, and a passing unit test — and were **never reachable through the real save path**. A duplicate `(item, batch_no)`, a self-converting UOMConversion, a duplicate lottable constraint: all saved clean through the live UI the entire time. Found only because this pass's own live-verification curl'd the *rejection* cases the earlier ones' passes had only exercised acceptance for. Fixed by collapsing the three blocks into one unconditional call — `ValidateMasterDataRules` already returns `nil` for any doctype not in its switch, so this is exactly as safe as the scattered version and now covers every doctype added to the switch from here on, with no fifth copy of the same wiring. The "one shared choke point" fix this repo's first principle asks for, applied to itself.
- **`GetFXGainLossRegister`'s default date window used Go's UTC clock to bound rows stamped by Postgres's own clock — the exact anti-pattern documented two functions above it, in the same file, by 37.1's own `PostingDate` comment.** On this Asia/Calcutta deployment that is a 5.5-hour daily gap: a posting made in the first hour of a local day is already "tomorrow" by the function's UTC-clock reckoning, so it falls outside its own "3 months ago through today" default window. Found because `TestFXGainLossRegisterReadsTheLedger` failed **deterministically**, not flakily, at the exact moment this session ran the full suite (00:5x local) — re-run three times in isolation, failed all three, which ruled out the usual shared-DB-pollution explanation before it was assumed. Fixed by reckoning "today" from `SELECT CURRENT_DATE` when the caller left either date blank, matching the pattern the file's own `PostingDate` already used. Same lesson as the Stage 14 lockout bug, the Stage 35 SLA bug, and 37.1.4's `SettlementDate` note — now four instances of one bug class in this codebase's history, worth a name if a fifth ever turns up.

**Verified**: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./... -p 1` clean throughout, including a rerun with `-count=1` twice consecutively to confirm the new tests' own cleanup is genuinely idempotent (a bug in `uom_test.go`'s own cleanup pattern — a prefix `LIKE` match against a code shaped as a suffix — was caught and fixed the same way). Live-verified over real HTTP on throwaway servers: LottableConstraint/UOM/UOMConversion creation and rejection, `batch/consume` honouring a customer's lottable contract, a sticker print returning a real `barcode_svg`, and — after the choke-point fix — Batch/SerialNumber/LottableConstraint/UOMConversion all correctly rejecting duplicate and malformed rows for the first time. All live fixtures removed afterwards. `docs/brain/` redrawn (100% region coverage) after adding `engines/uom.go`, `engines/uom_test.go`, `engines/code128.go`, `engines/code128_test.go`, `engines/stickers_test.go`.

**Everything in this entry is uncommitted, in the same working tree as the prior session's 42.1.1-42.1.9 work** — see `docs/ai_handover.md` §6 for the full file list and handover state.

**Same session, continued into Phase 42.2 (the warehouse task spine, "the architectural phase" per the plan) — 42.2.1 and 42.2.2 also built.** `WarehouseTask` (`engines/warehouse_task.go`) is the one object every floor action now emits into, with `TransitionWarehouseTaskStatus` as its lifecycle choke point (mirroring `SetBatchStatus`/`TransitionSerialStatus`). All five pre-existing floor actions (`PutawayToBin`, `ExecuteBinReplenishment`, `CrossDockPutaway`, `PostCycleCountAdjustment`, `ScanPickItem`) now additively log a `Completed` task through one `LogCompletedWarehouseTask` choke point — closing 26.5.13's "picking isn't instrumented" gap as a side effect, without the `userID`-parameter signature change 26.5.13's own attempt at this was rejected over (`ScanPickItem`'s retrofit lives in its HTTP handler instead, which already has the user id). Diagnosing this item's own test run surfaced a third finding: the connection-pool-leak-per-`db.InitDB`-call issue this repo's own Stage 35.4 entry already documented can still tip an unrelated, untouched test over in a full `go test ./engines/... -p 1` run — confirmed environmental (passes in isolation, and the whole package passes split into two name-based halves), not a regression, and the fix scope stayed the same as 35.4 set it (guard new test files, leave the rest alone). Verified: build/vet clean, all new tests green, live HTTP putaway + pick-scan each producing a real `Completed WarehouseTask` row.

Phase 42.2 continues into 42.2.3 (task queue + dispatch) next.

## 103. Stage 39.11/39.12/39.16/39.17 — the Knowledge Center gets its content, 6 articles → 23 (2026-08-17, content + code)

Asked to start writing Stage 39's content, which was 11 open items described as "mostly content authoring". Four of them are now closed. Full item-by-item detail in `micro_checklist.md` Stage 39.

**Two code changes came first, because the seed content had already got cross-linking wrong and every article in this pass links to others.** `first-login.md` linked `first-order.md` the way one Markdown file references another; rendered, that is a *relative* href, so from `/help/first-login` the browser asked for `/help/first-order.md` — a slug that does not exist. `error-codes.md` pointed at `../../guides/ERROR_CODES.md`, which is not reachable from inside the application at all. Both had shipped, and nothing caught either. The fix is at the renderer rather than in the articles: `articleURL` rewrites any scheme-less, host-less `*.md` link to `/help/<basename>`, mirroring `slugFromPath`. Authors keep writing ordinary Markdown links — the sources stay readable on disk and in a repo browser — and cannot get the app's URL shape wrong because they never type it. It runs *after* `safeURL`, so it cannot be used to smuggle a scheme past the allowlist, and that is pinned by a test.

**Three reference articles are generated, not written.** `cmd/gendocs` gained `-kb-out` and now emits `error-code-reference` (all 302 codes), `report-catalog` (every registered report) and `country-phone-rules` (all 54 countries, from `engines.PhoneCountries()`). They are written as **Markdown into `docs/kb/`**, not as HTML into `internal/kb/content/` — so `genkb` remains the only thing that renders an article, and the generated pages get the same anchors, the same search index and the same access model as a hand-written one rather than a parallel path that would drift from all three. This is the repo's settled rule applied again: a hand-written copy of a list the code owns is wrong the day someone touches the code.

**That forced one small export.** A generator writing its own contents list has to predict the anchor the renderer will emit. GitHub's anchor rule and this renderer's disagree on runs of punctuation — `data-import--excel-upload` against `data-import-excel-upload` — which is precisely the kind of difference that produces a table of contents whose links quietly stop resolving and that nobody notices for months. `HeadingSlug` is therefore exported and used by both, with a test asserting they agree.

**The content itself, 17 new articles.** Getting Started grew to five (what the system is; finding your way around; the ten-step worked example from `USER_GUIDE` §13). Role Journeys is seven — six jobs plus an overview that exists because the ask named six *jobs* while the system ships four *roles*, and writing six "role" articles without saying which is which would have been the more misleading choice. Reference gained a ~50-term glossary, ~60 abbreviations and an FAQ. Troubleshooting gained a symptom index keying ~70 symptoms to the codes they produce, because the reference answers "what does this code mean" and the question people actually arrive with is "this happened, now what". Every code cited in it was read out of the live catalog rather than recalled.

**Two mechanical checks now exist that did not before**, both run over the built output: zero dangling cross-references (all 20 distinct `/help/` targets resolve to a real slug) and zero invalid screen ids (all 40 declared `screens:` values match a real `renderView` id). Those are 39.8's job to automate, and they found nothing this pass only because the renderer fix removed the class of error that was already there.

**Live-verified** on a throwaway server (port 9142, stopped afterwards): 23 articles in the intended sidebar order, articles fetch with correct titles and tables of contents, rendered hrefs are the rewritten form — and the **public index is still narrowed to the two opted-in articles**, so 39.6's access model holds at 23 articles exactly as it did at 6.

**Left open and said so rather than quietly begun:** 39.13 (module handbooks, the largest remaining item), 39.14, 39.15, and 39.18 — the last of which *deletes* five guide files that `CLAUDE.md` and many docs reference by path, and wants to be its own reviewed unit rather than a side-effect of an authoring session.
